# Master Issue Registry — Deep Revision, Stage 2

Single consolidated register of every distinct problem surfaced by the project's
audits, fix plans, live-probe logs, roadmap, daemon tracker, and fix history —
re-grounded against **current code on `main` @ `f317c4c`**.

## How to read this

- **Canonical ID:** `MIR-NNN`. Prior IDs from other documents are kept as
  **aliases** (never discarded).
- **Merge rule applied:** entries are merged **only** when they share a *proven
  common root* (same code location / same causal chain that the source docs
  themselves document). Similar symptoms alone were **not** merged. The five
  CORE learning-loop findings are a documented chain but live at independent
  locations with independent fix status, so they are kept as **separate issues**
  cross-linked under the "learning-loop cluster," not collapsed into one.
- **Provenance tag** per issue: `previously_documented` (already in a repo doc),
  `independently_reconfirmed` (re-verified by a later audit and by this pass),
  `newly_discovered` (first raised here).
- **Status vocabulary:** `open`, `partially_fixed`,
  `code_fixed_needs_runtime_verification`, `fixed`, `regression`, `duplicate`,
  `planned_gap`, `stale_finding`, `not_a_bug`, `needs_investigation`,
  `diagnosis_corrected`.
- **`diagnosis_corrected` means the entry's stated mechanism was disproved, and
  nothing else.** It is not a synonym for `fixed`: the defect the entry was
  written about may be entirely unaddressed. An entry carrying it must say what
  the false claim was, which facts refuted it, and — where a real defect
  survives the correction — name the separate open issue that now owns it.

### Verification limitations (Stage 2)

- Evidence is **code reading + regression-test-file existence**. The test suite
  was **not executed** and the **live agent was not run** this stage. Therefore
  no item is marked plain `fixed`; code-level fixes are
  `code_fixed_needs_runtime_verification` (code + a named test present, runtime
  not observed).
- Fix **commits** were not archaeologically enumerated; evidence cites the
  current `file:line` and the guarding test. Line numbers are as of `f317c4c`.
- Nothing was changed, fixed, or deleted producing this register.
- **Update (Stage 3+ / M0 / M1 review — 2026-07-19):** the two limitations above are
  historical to Stage 2 and are **superseded for individual issues**: later passes DID
  execute reproductions and targeted tests, and items meeting the stricter criteria were
  upgraded to `fixed` (MIR-017 runtime-reproduced; MIR-001/004/005 reproduced as pure
  functions). Each issue's own **Status/Evidence lines are authoritative** over this
  Stage-2 note. The tally below is the authoritative current count.

in its own section (e.g. MIR-050 `open` but deliberately deferred, MIR-042
`partially_fixed` with only its counting face closed) is authoritative there —
the table records the status, the section records the nuance.

## A. Learning-loop cluster (episodic/procedural learning quality)

> Documented chain (CORE_AUDIT §1): CORE-03 → CORE-01 (bad answer banks as
> success) → CORE-02 (becomes active procedure) → CORE-05 (fed back as
> experience); CORE-04 inflates confidence along the way. Kept as separate
> issues; "related causes" fields carry the chain links.

### MIR-001 — episode `outcome=success` decided from raw chunk counts
- **Aliases:** CORE-01, MGA-02, A2. **Provenance:** previously_documented (MGA-02) + independently_reconfirmed (CORE-01).
- **Sources:** MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md, CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md, MEMORY_FIX_PLAN.md, LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md.
- **First documented:** MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md @ `accd746` (2026-07-17), audited `3f4f8fa`.
- **Files/functions:** `core/smart_memory.py` `episode_from_agent_cycle` (now ~`:541`).
- **Symptom:** `verified=1, unverified=10` banked as `success`, quality ≈0.09.
- **Root cause:** outcome from chunk counts; old guard `elif unverified > verified and verified == 0` let one verified chunk immunise a mostly-unverified answer.
- **Related causes:** feeds MIR-003, MIR-005; quality metric MIR-002.
- **Production path:** AgentLoop cycle → `loop_methods2._record_experience_memory` → `episode_from_agent_cycle` → `episodic_store.save`.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_episode_outcome_majority_unverified.py`, `tests/test_smart_memory.py`.
- _(SUPERSEDED — see this entry's Status/Resolution; kept as the record of what was considered)_ **Missing tests:** end-to-end tick asserting a bad cycle no longer banks `success` in the live runtime.
- **Status:** `fixed` (Stage-3.3 reproduced; pure function, no concurrency risk). ⬆ from `code_fixed_needs_runtime_verification`.
- **Evidence:** current code `elif unverified > verified:` with comment citing CORE-01/MGA-02; `and verified==0` removed. **Stage-3.3 reproduction (isolated):** `verified=1,unverified=10 → 'partial'` (q=0.091, was `success`); `verified=5,unverified=1 → 'success'`; `verified=2,weak=3 → 'partial'`; `verified=0,unverified=0 → 'success'` (general knowledge preserved). Targeted test `tests/test_episode_outcome_majority_unverified.py` green.
- **Approved fix (doc):** MEMORY_FIX_PLAN A2 — landed and verified.

### MIR-002 — `_compute_quality_score` returns 1.0 on an empty evidence chain
- **Aliases:** CORE-03, LPF-011, A3. **Provenance:** previously_documented + independently_reconfirmed.
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md, CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md, MEMORY_FIX_PLAN.md.
- **First documented:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md @ `e1d8081` (2026-07-18).
- **Files/functions:** `core/smart_memory.py` `_compute_quality_score` (`:51-52`).
- **Symptom:** a zero-evidence (pure general-knowledge / self-declared) answer banks `answer_quality_score=1.0`, contradicting a `fully_unverified` verifier report.
- **Root cause:** `if total == 0: return 1.0`.
- **Related causes:** couples with MIR-001 (outcome), MIR-023 (LPF-002: general knowledge has evidence=0). Same nerve.
- **Production path:** same as MIR-001 (`answer_quality_score` set in `episode_from_agent_cycle`).
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_smart_memory.py` (asserts current 1.0 behaviour).
- _(SUPERSEDED — see this entry's Status/Resolution; kept as the record of what was considered)_ **Missing tests:** a regression asserting empty-chain → neutral floor (only if A3 is adopted).
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20, commit `f16febf`)** ⬆ from `open`. Resolved by alternative (c) — separating "no evidence" from the quality axis — not by the A3 floor.
- **Resolution:** `_compute_quality_score` now returns **`None`** when there are no chunks: with an empty denominator the fraction is undefined, and the top of the scale is not a neutral value. `answer_quality_score` is `float | None`. Each of the four readers states its own treatment: staleness → mid-scale (`_QUALITY_WHEN_UNMEASURED`); pruning → candidate, tracked as `unmeasured` **separately** from `low_quality`; re-ask → lowers the threshold as for weak answers; fast path → refuses replay (`AgentLoop._quality_allows_replay`). The arbitrary-constant forks (a)/(b) were rejected: both keep reporting a measurement that was never taken.
- **Resolution evidence (measured before → after):** staleness groundless `1.33 → 2.08` vs well-evidenced `1.63` (higher = pruned sooner, so the inversion is gone); pruning at `min_quality=0.9` `['well-evidenced'] → ['evidenced','groundless']`; re-ask hint on a groundless answer `False → True`. Guard: measurable ratios unchanged (`8,2 → 0.8`). Tests: `tests/test_quality_score_unknown.py` (7, six fail-before). Full suite 4777 passed / 0 failed.
- **Evidence:** code still returns `1.0` on an empty chain; the docstring reframes it as "neutral." `_compute_quality_score(0,0,0)=1.0`, `(1,9,0)=0.1` (reproduced). **Downstream trace — the 1.0 is NOT inert:**
  - **`core/loop_gates.py:205-281` `_episodic_fast_path`** fires when a similar past episode has `answer_quality_score >= 0.70` **and** `not tools_used` **and** a non-empty `full_answer` (plus `similarity >= 0.85`). An empty-chain answer yields exactly `quality=1.0, outcome='success', tools_used=()`, so it **satisfies the gate**. **Reproduced:** even a *wrong* ungrounded answer ("Sydney" for Australia's capital) → `quality=1.0`, gate = `True`. Effect: on a later near-identical question the ungrounded answer is **replayed verbatim, the full verify pipeline is SKIPPED, and it is re-banked with `verified_chunks=1`** (bypasses a check **and** banks as verified success — exactly the two downstream effects that turn this from a design choice into a defect). The gate never consults the original verifier verdict, so a `fully_unverified` answer is replay-eligible (ties to MIR-016).
  - **`core/episodic_hygiene.py:125`** prunes on `answer_quality_score < min_quality`; a `1.0` ungrounded episode is protected from pruning (kept as high-value).
  - **`core/smart_memory.py:409` / re-ask detection** lowers the repeat-question threshold only when `quality < 0.5`; a `1.0` ungrounded answer suppresses the "user is re-asking because the last answer was weak" hint.
- **Scope/severity:** the fast-path also needs `similarity >= 0.85`, no file hint, no local-critique, non-`:` input — so the sharp case is *near-duplicate general-knowledge questions whose first answer was ungrounded/possibly wrong*. For a genuinely-correct general-knowledge answer, replay is fine; the defect is that the score cannot tell a correct from an incorrect ungrounded answer. Moderate severity, real path.
- **Blast-radius note (Stage-3.3):** ungrounded `quality=1.0` episodes come from more than general knowledge — the **verifier soft-fail** path (`core/loop_verify_replan.py:412`, `st.verifier_failure = True`, when the verifier itself throws; Stage-3.3 cited `core/loop.py` `:1533`, an anchor that had already drifted before the loop split, #312) records `verified=0,unverified=0,fully_unverified=False` → a `1.0` episode. So a verifier *crash* also produces a fast-path-eligible "perfect" episode. Widens the case for fixing MIR-002 at the provenance level, not the score.
- _(SUPERSEDED — see this entry's Status/Resolution; kept as the record of what was considered)_ **Alternatives (still a real fork, but now bounded by the downstream):** (a) MEMORY_FIX_PLAN A3 floor (e.g. 0.3) for `total==0`; (b) keep 1.0 for the *score* but make `episodic_fast_path` additionally require the original episode passed verification (not just `quality>=0.70`); (c) separate a "no-evidence" flag from the quality axis so downstream gates can treat "confidently ungrounded" distinctly. Whichever is chosen must fix the **fast-path replay**, not just the number in isolation.
- _(SUPERSEDED — see this entry's Status/Resolution; kept as the record of what was considered)_ **Alternatives (undecided):** (a) apply A3 floor 0.3; (b) formally accept 1.0-as-neutral and close A3 as won't-fix; (c) separate "no-evidence" from "quality" so general knowledge isn't scored on an evidence axis at all. **Decision needed** — do not treat as a straightforward bug until the product decision is made.

### MIR-003 — one dubious success mints an `active` procedure, no quality gate
- **Aliases:** CORE-02, MGA-03, LPF-013, A4. **Provenance:** previously_documented + independently_reconfirmed.
- **Sources:** MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md, CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md, LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md, MEMORY_FIX_PLAN.md.
- **First documented:** MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md @ `accd746`.
- **Files/functions:** `core/smart_memory.py` `procedure_from_episode` (`:578`) → `ProceduralStore.upsert_from_episode` (`:464`); smoothing `_smoothed_confidence` (`:67`).
- **Symptom:** a single `success` episode with tools mints a procedure born `status="active"`; Beta(1,1) smoothing puts one success at 0.667 ≥ 0.6.
- **Root cause:** `procedure_from_episode` gates only on `outcome=="success" and tools_used`; no `answer_quality_score` threshold; born `active`.
- **Related causes:** upstream MIR-001 (outcome) and MIR-002 (quality).
- **Production path:** `_record_experience_memory` → `procedural_store.upsert_from_episode` → `procedure_from_episode`.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_smart_memory.py` (procedure creation).
- **Missing tests:** ~~a low-quality success does **not** create an `active` procedure~~ — the correctness half is now regression-locked (see the 2026-07-22 addition). The A4 *born-`candidate`* half stays untested because the behaviour it describes is not implemented (no `candidate` status exists).
- **Status:** **`fixed`** (2026-07-22) ⬆ from `code_fixed_needs_runtime_verification` ⬆ from `partially_fixed`. Correctness half regression-locked in #116; A4 maturity gate implemented in the follow-up; **now verified on a live agent against a real model** (see the Runtime verification note below). Both halves closed.
- **Evidence:** the direct gate is **unchanged** (still born `active`, no quality arg). **But** the MIR-001 fix reclassifies most low-quality answers as `partial` *before* they reach here, so the specific CORE-02 trigger is largely closed upstream. The `answer_quality_score<0.5` gate at `smart_memory.py:409` is in the **re-ask/Jaccard** path, **not** procedure creation — it does **not** address this issue. **Stage-3.3 reproduction:** a `verified=1,unverified=10` episode is now `partial` → `procedure_from_episode` returns **None** (no procedure). A genuine `verified=3,unverified=0` success → procedure still born `status='active'` (conf 0.5). So the exploit path is closed; the A4 "born `candidate` + quality threshold" design change is still not implemented (a single *genuine* success still activates immediately).
- **Approved fix (doc):** MEMORY_FIX_PLAN A4 (born `candidate` + quality threshold on birth) — **not applied**.
- **Addition (2026-07-22) — the correctness half is now proven and regression-locked; the design half is isolated and stated precisely.**
  - **Root re-verified as localized.** `ProcedureRecord` is constructed in exactly two places, both in `core/smart_memory.py` (`procedure_from_episode` birth `:1290`, `with_episode` update `:363`); the only external caller is `core/loop_methods2.py:654` `procedural_store.upsert_from_episode`, which routes through the single birth gate `procedure_from_episode` — no bypass. The decision lives in one file. <!-- historical-ref -->
  - **Reproduced (read-only, in-process):** every non-completing episode mints **no** procedure — `verified=1,unverified=10` (`partial`), a grounded-but-`blocked` `verified=3` (`outcome=success` but `completion=blocked`), and a `refused` run all return `procedure_credit_allowed=False` → `procedure_from_episode=None`. Only a genuine `achieved` success mints one, born `active`. The "bad-reinforcement" defect this entry was opened for is therefore **closed**, by `procedure_credit_allowed` (MIR-057 completion axis) combined with MIR-001's outcome reclassification.
  - **Regression-locked** by `tests/test_smart_memory.py::test_grounded_but_incomplete_success_mints_no_procedure` and `::test_incomplete_success_mints_no_procedure_through_the_store`. These cover the previously-untested **completion axis** on procedure birth (a well-grounded `blocked`/`refused` answer — the exact MIR-057 live case), which the existing `failed`/`partial`/`weak` tests do not. **Fail-before demonstrated:** with the gate reduced to the pre-MIR-057 predicate (`outcome == "success" and tools_used`), the same blocked episode mints a `status="active"` procedure and both tests fail; on current code they pass. Full suite `5077 passed`.
  - **What is NOT fixed, stated exactly:** a single *genuine, verified, completed* success is still born `active` immediately (conf 0.5 at birth, 0.667 after `with_episode`). Making it born unproven (A4's *maturity gate*) requires a status value that **does not exist** — `ProcedureStatus = Literal["active","needs_review","obsolete"]` has no `candidate` (MEMORY_FIX_PLAN A4) or `extracted` (MEMORY_LIFECYCLE_CONTRACT, unapproved v2-draft). Adding one cascades to `_procedure_status` coercion (`:1371`), the consolidation status buckets (`:1308-1310`), and the retrieval path that injects `active` procedures into planning, and the two governing docs disagree on the name. **This is an owner architecture decision (status vocabulary), not a bounded bug fix, and is deliberately left unimplemented here.**
- **OWNER DECISION (2026-07-22) — the maturity gate is approved, and the status-vocabulary ambiguity is resolved.** The repository owner decided: *«A new procedure is born as `candidate` and does not participate in normal planning until confirmed by an independent, completed and verified execution.»*
  - **Name resolved:** `candidate` (MEMORY_FIX_PLAN A4), **not** `extracted` (the unapproved M1 contract). This is now the repository-owned policy the implementer may rely on.
  - **Two required behaviours:** (1) birth status = `candidate`, never `active`; (2) a `candidate` procedure is **excluded from ordinary planning retrieval** until it is promoted; (3) promotion to `active` requires a **second, independent, completed *and* verified** execution — i.e. `procedure_credit_allowed` must hold for a *distinct* confirming episode, not the birth episode.
  - **Framing:** this is the **next protective layer against premature learning**, not a fix of the original MIR-003 bug (that bug — dubious/incomplete reinforcement — is already closed and now regression-locked, above). It hardens the *genuine-single-success* case.
  - **Scope / sequencing:** implementation is a **separate follow-up PR**, not this one (this PR changes no behaviour). It will touch `ProcedureStatus` (add `candidate`), `procedure_from_episode` birth, `_procedure_status` coercion, the consolidation status buckets, and the retrieval filter that injects procedures into planning — plus fail-before tests that a one-success `candidate` is not planned with and is promoted only on a second independent completed+verified success. **When that lands, this entry moves toward `fixed`; until then it stays `partially_fixed`.**
- **IMPLEMENTED (2026-07-22, the follow-up PR) — `core/smart_memory.py` only.** The owner decision above is now code:
  - `ProcedureStatus` gains `candidate`; a shared `_procedure_status_for(success_count, confidence)` decides status in the two live setters (`with_episode`, `with_outcome`): `needs_review` if `confidence < 0.6` (demotion unchanged, checked first), else `candidate` while `success_count < _PROMOTION_MIN_SUCCESSES` (=2), else `active`.
  - Birth (`procedure_from_episode`) is `candidate`; a single credited success stays `candidate` (conf 0.667, which the old rule read as `active`).
  - **Planning exclusion:** `ProceduralMemoryStore.search` — the sole path that injects procedures into the planner (`core/loop_methods2.py:354`) — skips `candidate`. Promotion to `active` requires a **second, independent, completed+verified** success (`success_count` only rises through `procedure_credit_allowed`, MIR-057), after which it surfaces to planning. <!-- historical-ref -->
  - `_procedure_status` coercion accepts `candidate`, so a stored candidate is not silently rewritten on load.
  - **End-to-end timeline (tested):** retrieval is at cycle start, promotion at cycle end, so a workflow is first *reused* on the third encounter (born c1, promoted c2, injected c3) — one cycle later than before, by design.
  - **Deliberately out of scope (stated):** the legacy-confidence migration (`recompute_legacy_confidence`, `:820`) and already-stored procedures keep the two-value rule — this decision governs *new* procedures; the consolidation report (a dead sink, MIR-044) is not expanded with a candidate bucket, so a candidate is counted in `procedure_count` but listed in no status bucket.
  - **Tests** (`tests/test_smart_memory.py`): 4 new (`born_candidate_not_active`, `candidate_not_offered_to_planning`, `second_independent_success_promotes_candidate_to_active`, `candidate_status_survives_serialization_round_trip`) + 3 updated to the new behaviour (`single_success_procedure_is_not_certain`, the injection timeline, the consolidation linkage). **Fail-before:** all four new tests and the updated single-success test fail on the pre-change code (`assert 'active' == 'candidate'`). Full suite `5081 passed`.
- **RUNTIME VERIFICATION (2026-07-22) — operator-run, live model, isolated workspace.** Run by the repository owner with `python main.py --workspace runtime/mir003-live --file probe.txt`, provider `openai / gpt-5.6-terra`, a fresh empty store, and three differently-phrased asks that all resolve to the same `tools:file_read` workflow. Log evidence, cycle by cycle:
  1. `procedural_memory_update  created=True, status=candidate, confidence=0.667` — **born `candidate`**, not `active`.
  2. `experience_memory_inject  procedures_selected=0` — **the candidate was withheld from planning**; then `procedural_memory_update  status=active, confidence=0.75` — **promoted by the second independent completed+verified success**.
  3. `experience_memory_inject  procedures_selected=1` — **the promoted procedure is now injected into planning**; `procedure_feedback  offered=1, already_applied=1`; final stored record `status=active, success_count=3, confidence=0.8, workflow_key=tools:file_read, 3 source episodes`.
  This matches the predicted timeline exactly — born in cycle 1, promoted in cycle 2, first *reused* in cycle 3 — because retrieval runs at cycle start and promotion at cycle end. All four properties (born candidate / withheld / promoted on the second success / then planned with) are confirmed outside the test suite.
- **Follow-up defect found BY that live run, and fixed with it.** `:smart-memory` reported `procedures=1 statuses={'active': 0, 'needs_review': 0, 'obsolete': 0}` — one procedure appearing in no status at all, which reads to an operator as a fault. Cause: the tally in `core/loop_methods2.py` hardcoded three statuses and did not know `candidate`; `consolidate_memory` bucketed only the same three. Fixed by deriving the tally from a new `PROCEDURE_STATUSES` constant (`ProcedureStatus.__args__`, so a future status can never go missing the same way) and adding `candidate_procedure_ids` to `ConsolidationReport` (defaulted, so pre-existing reports round-trip unchanged). Guarded by `test_smart_memory_summary_counts_candidate_procedures`, `test_smart_memory_summary_covers_every_procedure_status` and `test_consolidation_report_lists_candidate_procedures`, all fail-before. <!-- historical-ref -->

### MIR-004 — fabricated (unmatched) citations receive positive evidence credit
- **Aliases:** CORE-04, LPF-008. **Provenance:** previously_documented + independently_reconfirmed.
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md, CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md.
- **First documented:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md @ `e1d8081`.
- **Files/functions:** `core/confidence_gate.py` `compute_confidence` (`:57`). <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** a `cited_but_unmatched` chunk (citation resolving to nothing) earned `+0.5`, so `[tool:host_tools]` on an empty chain scored 0.5.
- **Root cause:** `raw = verified*1.0 + cited*0.5 + unverified*-0.25`.
- **Related causes:** amplified MIR-013 (host_tools) and the whole loop.
- **Production path:** verifier → `confidence_gate.compute_confidence` → `low_confidence_gate` log (observational, see MIR-016).
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_confidence_gate.py`. <!-- historical-ref -->
- _(SUPERSEDED — see this entry's Status/Resolution; kept as the record of what was considered)_ **Missing tests:** none critical (test present).
- **Status:** `fixed` (Stage-3.3 reproduced; pure formula, no concurrency risk). ⬆ from `code_fixed_needs_runtime_verification`.
- **Evidence:** current code `cited * -0.25`. **Stage-3.3 reproduction (isolated, with `total_chunks` set):** fabricated cites `verified=0,cited=2,total=2 → 0.0` (was `0.5`); real `verified=2,total=2 → 1.0`; mixed `verified=1,cited=1,total=2 → 0.375`. Targeted test `tests/test_confidence_gate.py` green. (Note: `compute_confidence` gates on `report.total_chunks`, not the sum — a bare stub without it returns 0.0.) <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Note:** `core/confidence_vector.py` header comment still narrates the old `cited_but_unmatched=0.5` — a **stale in-code comment** (see MIR-023); `evidence_score` there delegates to `compute_confidence` per self-audit #4, so behaviour follows the fix, but the comment should be corrected.

### MIR-005 — experience retrieval has no outcome/quality filter
- **Aliases:** CORE-05, LPF-012, A5. **Provenance:** previously_documented + independently_reconfirmed.
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md, CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md, MEMORY_FIX_PLAN.md.
- **First documented:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md @ `e1d8081`.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_methods2.py` `_retrieve_experience_memory` (`:159`, filter at `:24-30`). <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** a `partial`/`quality≈0` episode could be retrieved and injected as prior "experience," closing the bad-learning loop.
- **Root cause:** unfiltered `episodic_store.search(question, limit=3)`.
- **Related causes:** consumes the bad episodes produced by MIR-001/002.
- **Production path:** AgentLoop observe → `_retrieve_experience_memory` → planner/synth context.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_experience_retrieval_outcome_filter.py` (green on `main`).
- _(SUPERSEDED — see this entry's Status/Resolution; kept as the record of what was considered)_ **Missing tests:** a full autonomous-tick end-to-end (unit/behavioural coverage is present).
- **Status:** `fixed` (Stage-3.3 confirmed; read-only retrieval filter, no concurrency risk). ⬆ from `code_fixed_needs_runtime_verification`.
- **Evidence:** `_retrieve_experience_memory` (`core/loop_methods2.py:159`) over-fetches `limit=6` then filters `ep.outcome == "success" or "lesson" in ep.tags`, caps at 3 (comment cites CORE-05/LPF-012). `lesson` episodes are intentionally kept (learn-from-failure). Targeted test green. The filter is a deterministic read-only predicate — a `partial`/`failed` episode cannot surface as "experience." <!-- historical-ref -->

### MIR-006 — universal, self-contradicting success criterion
- **Aliases:** LPF-007. **Provenance:** previously_documented (batch-2, verified in code).
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md.
- **First documented:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md @ `e1d8081`.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_methods2.py` `_interpret` (`:835-839`). <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom (as originally recorded — NOT confirmed):** every goal carries `success_criteria="A grounded answer citing every claim back to a provided source."` even when the planner chose no tools/sources → synthesizer must cite sources never collected.
- **Root cause:** hardcoded universal success criterion; recorded as the root of the "provided materials" phrasing (LPF-020).
- **Related causes:** recorded as upstream of MIR-004/MIR-013 (fabricated citations); the LPF doc calls this a primary root of the citation-fabrication loop.
- **Production path:** *asserted* as goal construction → synthesizer prompt. **This path does not exist** — see the correction below.
- **Existing tests:** none targeted.
- **Missing tests:** a no-source turn is not required to cite sources; general-knowledge answer is not penalised for missing citations.
- **Status:** `diagnosis_corrected`.
- **Correction (2026-08-02, verified by grep over the production tree — `core/ cli/ app/ api/ tools/ scripts/`; tests are excluded because a test naming the field proves nothing about whether the runtime consumes it):**
  - **The previous claim is wrong in its mechanism.** `Goal.success_criteria` is **written once and read nowhere**. The search returns exactly three lines: the assignment at `core/loop_methods2.py:839`, the field declaration at `core/models.py:25`, and a prose mention inside a docstring at `core/completion_obligation.py:33`. Not one of them is a read. It reaches no prompt, no check and no verdict. <!-- historical-ref -->
  - **Facts that refuted it:** (a) the grep above returns no consumer; (b) the real output contract is `SYSTEM_ANSWER` in `core/loop_helpers.py:58`, and it is already conditional — with no `<evidence>` blocks it instructs the model to answer from general knowledge, mark `[general-knowledge]` and lower confidence, which is the opposite of "must cite sources never collected"; (c) `outcome` is computed from the verifier's chunk verdicts, not from this field. <!-- historical-ref -->
  - **The real defect this entry actually documents:** a **dead, misleading field**. It reads like the system's definition of success, is not, and misled both the author of this entry and a later investigator into a false root-cause chain. Removing it is *not* the fix — it is the designed hook for the completion contract (`core/completion_obligation.py:77`, `UNWIRED_SOURCES`), and deleting it would foreclose that design.
  - **What remains open:** nothing in the system represents the deliverable a request asks for. That gap is **not** closed by this correction and is registered separately as **MIR-067**. Do not read `diagnosis_corrected` as "fixed": only the false diagnosis is retired.
- **Evidence:** assignment unchanged. The refutation is a negative result — the absence of any consumer — so it is stated as a command a reader can run and compare against, at the same scope it was verified at: `grep -rn "success_criteria" --include=*.py core cli app api tools scripts`. Three lines, no read among them.

### MIR-067 — nothing represents the deliverable a request asks for
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-08-02) — root-caused after five adversarial rounds against the live agent, when the operator ruled that further probing was treating symptoms.
- **Sources:** this pass. Live runs against the real provider, plus code reading. Split out of MIR-006 when that entry's mechanism was refuted.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_observe.py` `_interpret` (`:199` — lived at `core/loop_methods2.py:835` until the loop split, #312, 2026-08-04) <!-- historical-ref -->; `core/completion_obligation.py` `UNWIRED_SOURCES` (`:77`); `core/smart_memory.py` `assemble_completion_verdict`.
- **Symptom (measured, live):** asked to fix a test, propose a change and file an approval request, the agent performed the verification step only, declared `achieved`, banked `outcome=success` with `usage_eligible=True`, and minted a procedure from the run. It did not overstate: by the system's own definition it succeeded — it produced a grounded, fully cited answer.
- **Root cause:** the recorded goal of every run is `f"Answer the question: {question}"`, and the only machinery that judges completion measures *the answer* — the output contract in `core/answer_format.py` `SYSTEM_ANSWER` (`:27`; lived in `core/loop_helpers.py` until the loop split, #312) <!-- historical-ref --> and the verifier's chunk verdicts. No structure anywhere carries what the request asked to **exist or change** afterwards, so no check can ask whether it does.
- **Why this is not MIR-006:** that entry blamed a constant `success_criteria` for propagating downstream. It propagates nothing — the field is read nowhere (see MIR-006's correction). The gap is an **absence**, not an overriding value.
- **What is already built toward it:** `core/completion_obligation.py` computes duties structurally from three wired sources (`intent`, `plan`, `freshness`) and names the missing piece in its own docstring — *"a contract between task statement, completion check and outcome"*. `acceptance_criteria` is the declared, still-unwired fourth source. As of 2026-08-02 an authoritative unmet duty **does** now lower a claim of `achieved` and blocks procedure credit (`assemble_completion_verdict`), so the mechanism exists; what it lacks is a criterion derived from the request to feed it.
- **Honest limit of the current state:** the three wired sources would **not** have caught the measured symptom above — that run's obligations were all satisfied, because its planned tools ran. Catching it requires the acceptance-criteria source, which requires a representation of the deliverable. That is the open work.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_completion_contract.py` — 17 tests, one class per clause of the operator's ruling.
- **Status:** `partially_fixed` (2026-08-02). The operator ruled the design on 2026-08-02: *the result of a task must be represented by a separate structured completion contract, derived from the request before the work, carrying verifiable obligations and the verification method of each; a plan and a good textual answer do not prove completion; an unmet obligation forbids `achieved`, forbids banking a successful episode and forbids procedural credit; an obligation that cannot be unambiguously derived must be asked about, not guessed.*
- **What is now built:** `core/completion_contract.py` derives the contract from the REQUEST — structurally blind to plan, artifacts and answer, since they are not parameters — and `core/loop.py` fixes it before planning (the `completion_contract` journal event precedes every `act`). `acceptance_criteria` is WIRED: an unmet deliverable becomes a `silently_missing` obligation, which the existing authority chain turns into `partially_achieved`, no banked success, no procedure credit. Measured on the registry's own symptom: a run asked to fix a file that only read it is `triggered=True`; the identical run without a contract is `satisfied=True` (both pinned as tests).
- **What remains open:** (a) the vocabulary recognises three deliverables (`file_exists`, `file_modified`, `tests_green`); anything else that names a path becomes an *ambiguity*, never a guessed duty; (b) **CLOSED 2026-08-13 — clause 6 is now enacted** (was: represented but not enacted). `_contract_ambiguity_gate` (`core/loop_gates.py:151`) turns the first ambiguity into a clarification question **before planning**, wired as the sixth gate at `core/loop.py:373` and pinned by `tests/test_plain_labelled_units_survive_the_paste.py::test_the_ambiguity_actually_asks`, which asserts the planner is never called. The live trigger was run `407a46c8`: a 37k-char journal paste burned 225 units where `needs_clarification=True` had been journalled and ignored. One of clause 6's two rules stays RETIRED. *(Reconciled 2026-08-14; the sentence below still describes the pre-fix state and is kept as the record of what was measured.)* The TARGETLESS-CHANGE rule was withdrawn 2026-08-02 after measuring zero precision — on 62 live requests it fired 8 times and was wrong all 8, the eighth being the operator's own numbered experiment procedure, declared targetless because step 7 said «внеси минимальное исправление». The mixed read+change rule REMAINS (it fired 0 times on the same 62 requests: unexercised, not disproven), so `ambiguities` is still a populated field; nothing acts on it in either case. Historical detail on the two targetless candidates: against the 48 real requests in the live agent's episodic memory, *path named under no recognised verb* raised 4 asks of which 2 were discussion turns merely citing a file, and *change verb with no path* (the rule kept) raised 7, all of them genuine change requests whose target was named in prose. With this vocabulary the signal cannot tell a vague operator from a clear one it fails to parse, so it stays observational under the standing sensor policy; (c) verification reads the run's artifacts, not the workspace on disk.
- **Scope note:** this is an architectural change with a behaviour change (task statement → completion criterion → check → outcome → learning admission). It must not be smuggled into a smaller memory or verdict fix; the operator ruled it a separate piece of work on 2026-08-02.
- **Related:** MIR-006 (the refuted diagnosis this was split from), MIR-016 (a gate that reports without acting).
- **Reconciliation 2026-08-14 (registry restored after nine days without a status owner):** open item (b) closed as above; (a) the three-deliverable vocabulary and (c) verification reading run artifacts rather than the workspace on disk are **unchanged** — no commit in `88fd261..HEAD` touches either. Status stays `partially_fixed`. Adjacent stale text was found in `core/clarification_gate.py` — a comment still claiming `completion_contract.needs_clarification` has no consumer, which 13b60bb had made false. It was left in place at the time because it is code rather than this record; it was deleted on 2026-08-20.

### MIR-007 — episodic tokenizer drops short/numeric/acronym tokens
- **Aliases:** CORE-10. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (CORE_AUDIT marks "NEW").
- **Sources:** CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md.
- **First documented:** CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md @ `19f327c` (2026-07-18).
- **Files/functions:** `core/smart_memory.py` `_tokens` (`:79-96`).
- **Symptom:** `_tokens('AI ML 3D PR os')` → `[]`; numbers `17`/`23` dropped → arithmetic questions collapse to the same token set.
- **Root cause:** `len(token.strip()) > 2` length floor with no digit exception.
- **Related causes:** MIR-008 (dual tokenizer).
- **Production path:** episodic retrieval/keying (`find_most_similar`, `search`).
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_episodic_tokenizer_numeric.py`.
- **Status:** `partially_fixed` (Stage-3.3). ⬇ from `code_fixed_needs_runtime_verification` — the fix covers the **numeric** half of CORE-10 but not the **short-acronym** half.
- **Evidence (Stage-3.3 reproduction):** `_tokens('How much is 17 times 23') → ['17','23','how','much','times']` — numbers **kept** ✅ (the original `17`/`23`-vanish defect is fixed). But `_tokens('AI ML 3D PR os') → ['3d']` — only the digit-bearing `3D` survived; the short **alphabetic acronyms `AI`/`ML`/`PR`/`os` are STILL dropped** (the original finding explicitly named `AI/ML/os/io` as invisible). The code keeps `len>2 OR any(ch.isdigit())`, and its own comment admits "Purely short alphabetic (stopword-like) tokens still fall through."
- **Residual (open part):** short alphabetic acronyms remain invisible to episodic retrieval. This may be a **deliberate scope choice** (2-char alphabetic tokens are often stopword-like and ambiguous), so it sits between `partially_fixed` and a design decision — needs an explicit call on whether acronyms like `AI`/`ML`/`os` must be retrievable. Ties to MIR-008 (the persistent-store tokenizer uses `len>1`, so the two stores disagree on exactly these tokens).

### MIR-008 — two different tokenizers across memory subsystems
- **Aliases:** CORE-11. **Provenance:** previously_documented (consistency-concern).
- **Sources:** CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/smart_memory.py` `_tokens` (len>2, no stopwords) vs `core/memory_policy.py:360` `_TOKEN_RE` (len>1 + stopwords).
- **Symptom:** episodic and persistent stores tokenize the same text differently.
- **Root cause:** two independent tokenizers.
- **Production path:** episodic vs persistent retrieval.
- **Existing tests:** none targeted.
- **Missing tests:** differential tokenizer consistency test (if unification is decided).
- **ИЗМЕРЕНО 2026-08-25, третий независимый заход.** Расхождение живое и крупное: на **181 из 257** живых текстов памяти (70 %) два токенизатора дают разные наборы. Форма расхождения видна: `memory_policy._tokens` режет `central_agent_governance.md` на `central_agent_governance` + `md`, а `topic_tokens` отдаёт и составное имя целиком, и его части, и вдобавок `and`, `the`, `0`.
- **Расхождение ОСОЗНАННОЕ и объяснено в самом коде.** `topic_tokens` намеренно не режет стоп-слова: правка 2026-08-22 показала, что после их удаления «почему тест падает» и «почему тест НЕ падает» совпадают дословно. Словарь при этом ОДИН — `memory_policy` читает `STOPWORDS` из `core/topic_tokens.py`; разошлось не мнение о словах, а решение его применять.
- **Гипотеза о вреде проверена и отвергнута до публикации.** Предположение: решение «сохранять ли запись памяти» читает два токенизатора сразу, потому что в нём сходятся ворота дедупа и антитело эха. Проверка убила гипотезу — оба зовут ОДНУ функцию `core.memory_hygiene._similarity`, различаясь лишь флагом `order_sensitive`, который разведён явно и с названной ценой ошибки для каждого потребителя.
- **Условие вреда проверено механически, а не предположено.** Обход `core/` по вызовам: ни один модуль не зовёт оба токенизатора. Потребители памяти опыта (`loop_memory_read`, `smart_memory`, `confidence_vector`, `verifier_absence`) и потребитель постоянной (`knowledge_use_policy`) не пересекаются.
- **Banked** тестом `tests/test_no_consumer_reads_both_tokenizers.py`: он держит ровно ту границу, на которой стоит безвредность, и краснеет, называя модуль, если её перейдут. Положительный контроль в тесте обязателен — обход, не находящий ни одного потребителя, «доказал» бы отсутствие пересечения ничем.
- **Status:** `banked` — три независимых захода (2026-07-18, 2026-08-22, 2026-08-25) не нашли неверного результата, а расхождение объяснено и намеренно. **Условие пересмотра:** покраснение сторожа, то есть появление решения, читающего оба набора для ОДНОГО текста.

### MIR-009 — no semantic temporal-truth model
- **Aliases:** CORE-12, MGA-05, A6. **Provenance:** previously_documented + independently_reconfirmed.
- **Sources:** MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md, CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md, MEMORY_FIX_PLAN.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/models.py` `MemoryRecord` (`:150`); ranking in `core/memory_policy.py`.
- **Symptom:** current truth, an exception, and a legacy note can rank as equal facts.
- **Root cause:** `MemoryRecord` has only `ttl_seconds/importance/archived`; no `lifecycle_status`, `valid_from/until`, `superseded_by`, `confirmation_refs`.
- **Production path:** `MemoryRetrievalPolicy.select` (token overlap + recency).
- **Existing tests:** none.
- **Missing tests:** retrieval does not present superseded/proposed/historical as equal current facts (once fields exist).
- **Status:** `planned_gap` (architectural — a missing capability, not a wrong computation).
- **Evidence:** confirmed absent — field list verified at `:150-172`.
- **Approved fix (doc):** MEMORY_FIX_PLAN A6, behind a flag, shadow-first, default `lifecycle_status=unclassified`.

### MIR-048 — procedural memory has no reachable negative feedback (one-way confidence ratchet)
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-003 (birth gate — different root: birth vs demotion), MIR-044. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (M1 review, operator-challenge follow-up).
- **Files/functions:** sole production path `core/loop_methods2.py:364` → `upsert_from_episode` (`core/smart_memory.py:464-467`) → `procedure_from_episode` (`:578-580`) returns `None` for any `outcome != "success"` — so `with_episode` (`:221-240`) never receives a non-success episode. The `failure_count` increment (`:224`) is **dead code in production**; no other writer exists. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** procedure confidence `(s+1)/(s+2)` rises monotonically from 0.667; `needs_review` (`<0.6`) is unreachable via outcomes; **a procedure can fail forever and never demote**. Consolidation's `needs_review` list is structurally near-empty (compounds MIR-044's emptiness).
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20)** ⬆ from `open`. Demotion is now reachable through outcomes.
- **Resolution:** `ProceduralMemoryStore.apply_episode_feedback(episode)` consumes **only** `used_procedure_ids` (MIR-049). No fallback exists — not workflow_key, tool set, name similarity, a fresh retrieval, or inference from content — because MIR-050 measured that keys pool unrelated goals, so any of those would debit an uninvolved procedure. An unresolvable id is reported `orphaned`, never substituted. Outcome matrix: `success` → `success_count+1`; `partial` and `failed` → `failure_count+1`; **cancelled → nothing** (a control signal is not evidence a procedure is bad); `used_procedure_ids` `None` (legacy) and `()` (known-none) are both no-ops and stay distinguishable in the report.
- **Atomicity:** `ProcedureRecord.with_outcome` recomputes counter, confidence and status into one new record, so no reader can see a bumped counter beside a stale confidence.
- **Idempotence:** keyed on the episode id already present in the procedure's own `source_episode_ids` journal — an explicit lookup, not a guess from the counters' shape. Re-processing a queue cannot turn the ratchet twice. Wired after `upsert_from_episode`, which journals the same id on a fresh success, so the two paths compose without double-counting.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_procedure_outcome_feedback.py` — 15 passing, all fail-before. Central case continues the MIR-049 scenario to its conclusion: two procedures sharing a workflow_key, only one attributed, run failed → the attributed one is debited and the other comes out **byte-identical**. Plus the outcome matrix, cancellation as a no-op, no-attribution no-ops, orphaned ids, re-application, multi-procedure (one discrete observation each, not a split share), duplicate collapsing, partial-orphan resilience, and `demotion_is_now_reachable` — four attributed failures move a procedure to `needs_review`.
- **Sequencing (operator decision, 2026-07-20):** attribution must land **before** feedback. Without `used_procedure_ids`, the only way to decide *which* procedure a failed cycle should debit is to re-derive it from `workflow_key` or a similar heuristic — and MIR-050 has now measured that those keys pool unrelated goals (8/48 keys span multiple goal classes). The result would be: failed episode → approximately-matched procedure → `failure_count` incremented on the **wrong** procedure. That is worse than the current ratchet, because a one-way ratchet is inert while misattributed punishment is actively wrong. Exact causal link first, counter updates second.
- **Measured scale (live store, read-only, 2026-07-20):** `failure_count == 0` on **all 65** procedures — the ratchet is not theoretical, it has never once turned. 64/65 are `active`; 53/65 rest on a single success. Non-success episodes do exist (e.g. `partial×2 + success×1` under `tools:shell_exec`), they simply never reach a procedure.
- **Root formula impact:** any lifecycle driver built on these counts (incl. §6.4 of the draft M1) inherits a positive-only signal — this is why "B-enhanced" alone is not a root fix.

### MIR-049 — no usage→outcome attribution for procedures (write-only `_last_procedure_records`)
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-048 (feedback), MIR-050 (identity) — separate roots, kept apart. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (M1 review).
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop.py:392`, `core/loop_methods2.py:229-230` — procedures injected into planning are recorded in `_last_procedure_records`, which is **read nowhere**; banked episodes carry no `used_procedure_ids`. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** a cycle that consumed a procedure and then failed/produced an unverified answer never updates that procedure; the only update channel is coincidental (a later success sharing the same tool sequence). Reputation without attribution.
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20)** ⬆ from `open`. Observation only — counters deliberately untouched, so "is the attribution correct" stays provable separately from "is the feedback policy correct" (MIR-048).
- **Resolution:** `EpisodeRecord.used_procedure_ids: tuple[str, ...] | None` with **three** distinguishable states — `None` = legacy row, attribution unknown and nothing may be inferred; `()` = this version ran and is certain no procedure applied; `(ids…)` = application observed. `resolve_used_procedures(selected, executed_tools)` requires **two** gates: the procedure was selected into the run (retrieval alone is not use) **and** every tool in its workflow actually executed. Judged from execution rather than the plan, so a run cancelled before reaching a procedure's steps never debits it. `AgentLoop._executed_tools` accumulates as steps complete, so an exception cannot discard attribution already earned. Order is first actual completion; the result is a tuple, never a set, so the stored record is reproducible.
- **Central invariant (executable):** two procedures sharing one `workflow_key`, only one selected → the episode names only that one. This is the direct proof that MIR-049 removes the risk MIR-050 measured: attribution follows the selection, never the shared shape.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_used_procedure_attribution.py` — 12 passing, all fail-before. Matrix: retrieved-but-not-selected, selected-but-not-reached, partial execution, full execution, repeat use recorded once, two procedures in deterministic order, attribution surviving `partial`/`failed`, cancellation after application, replay attributing nothing, legacy reading as `None` not `()`, and a dangling id never re-pointed via `workflow_key`.
- **Prerequisite satisfied for MIR-048:** a trustworthy causal link now exists to consume.
- **Why it must precede MIR-048:** MIR-048 needs to know which procedure a failed cycle should debit. The only alternative to a recorded `used_procedure_ids` is re-deriving the link from `workflow_key`, which MIR-050 has now measured to pool unrelated goals. Closing the feedback loop on top of an approximate link would replace an inert one-way ratchet with active misattribution — the system would honestly count failures and charge them to the wrong procedure.
- **Acceptance:** an episode records the procedures that were actually injected into its planning; a procedure's counters change only through that recorded link (or the repaired workflow-match channel), never through a re-derived guess.

### MIR-050 — procedure identity is tool-sequence-only; evidence aggregates across unrelated goals
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-048/049 (they interact: attribution onto a meaningless aggregate is still meaningless). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (M1 review).
- **Files/functions:** `core/smart_memory.py:581` `workflow_key = "tools:" + "->".join(tools)`.
- **Symptom:** episodes with the same tool sequence but unrelated goals upsert into one procedure ("Workflow using file_read"); its success counts and confidence describe a semantically meaningless aggregate, so any lifecycle judgment over it is unfounded.
- **ПЕРЕЗАМЕРЕНО 2026-08-25 тем же способом; вред уменьшился, причина отсрочки — нет.** Процедур **34** (было 65), эпизодов с инструментами **67** (было 68) на **44** различных последовательностях (было 48).
  - ключей более чем с одной целью: **6 из 44 (13 %)** против 8 из 48 (17 %);
  - эпизодов со смешанной уликой: **21 из 67 (31 %)** против 27 из 68 (40 %);
  - худшее столкновение: **3 разные цели** под одним ключом против прежних десяти у `tools:file_read->list_dir`;
  - плотность улики: **1,5 эпизода на ключ** против 1,4 — то есть НЕ изменилась.
- **Замер устойчив к определению «класса цели».** Считал двумя способами — по точному тексту цели и по набору различающих слов — числа совпали до единицы. Значит уменьшение не артефакт определения.
- **Отсрочка починки остаётся в силе по её же основанию.** Причина, по которой составной ключ отвергли 2026-07-20, — разреженность улики; она не улучшилась (1,4 → 1,5). Дробить 67 эпизодов ещё мельче по-прежнему значит менять смешанные счётчики на пустые.
- **Status:** `open` — **harm confirmed, fix deliberately DEFERRED** (2026-07-20). ⬆ from `needs_investigation`: the defined measurement ran, so this is no longer a mechanism without evidence. It is deferred on the merits, not forgotten — see the return condition below.
- **Measurement (live stores, read-only, 2026-07-20; nothing mutated):**
  - 65 procedures / 65 distinct `workflow_key`s; 200 episodes, 68 carrying tools, across 48 distinct tool-sequences.
  - **Keys spanning more than one goal-signature class: 8 of 48 (17%).**
  - **Episodes whose evidence is pooled across goal classes: 27 of 68 (40%).**
  - **Worst collision:** `tools:file_read->list_dir` — 10 distinct goal classes over 10 episodes, i.e. every episode under that key is a different goal.
  - **Outcome mixing under one key (the concrete wrong judgment):** `tools:shell_exec` holds `partial×2 + success×1` from three different goals — one procedure's counters aggregate the success of one task with the failures of others.
- **Why the fix is deferred rather than applied:** mean density is **1.4 episodes per key**. Option (a) (composite key = tools + goal signature) would fragment already-sparse evidence into singleton procedures — the worst key's 10 episodes would become 10 procedures of one episode each, none able to accumulate anything. Changing identity at this data volume degrades the store instead of repairing it. The harm is real but bounded (17% of keys); the cure at current density is worse.
- **Return condition (explicit, so "deferred" cannot become indefinite):** revisit once the store holds enough episodes to run an **offline** comparison of current vs candidate identity **without migrating production data**. That comparison must report: collision rate, share of singleton procedures, episodes-per-key density, outcome divergence within one key, and the effect on retrieval/planning quality. Only then is D-1 decidable.
- **RETURN CONDITION TESTED 2026-08-22, and it is NOT met — measured, not assumed.** The condition was density: revisit once the store holds enough episodes to compare identities offline. Re-run against the live stores, with the 2026-07-20 figures in brackets:

      procedures stored                    34   [65]
      episodes carrying tools              67   [68]
      distinct tool-sequences (keys)       44   [48]
      keys spanning >1 goal class       6/44 = 13%   [8/48 = 17%]
      episodes pooled across goal classes  21/67 = 31%   [27/68 = 40%]
      mean episodes per key               1.5   [1.4]
      singleton keys                    35/44 = 79%
      worst collision   tools:file_read x4 -> 3 goal classes over 3 episodes

  So the deferral was correct on the merits and remains correct: density rose from 1.4 to 1.5 in a month, and 79% of keys already hold exactly one episode, so splitting identity would still fragment sparse evidence rather than repair it. The harm also shrank slightly (17% -> 13% of keys, 40% -> 31% of episodes), so nothing has become urgent.
- **But the return condition is not merely unmet — it is very likely UNREACHABLE BY WAITING, and that changes what "deferred" means here.** Over a full month of operation the density needed to decide did not move (1.4 -> 1.5) while the procedure count HALVED, 65 -> 34. The mechanism is the one MIR-058 measured on the same day from the other side: of the 75 `source_episode_ids` held by surviving procedures, 70 (93%) point at episodes the 200-row FIFO has already evicted. Evidence is being destroyed faster than it accumulates, so a condition phrased as "wait until there is enough" cannot be satisfied by waiting. **Stated as a hypothesis, not a proven cause:** that the episodic cap (200 rows, 63% of them protected and unevictable — MIR-131) is what holds density flat. It is consistent with every number here and with MIR-058, and it has not been demonstrated by intervention.
- **Consequence for the fix order, which is the useful half.** If that hypothesis holds, D-1 cannot be unblocked by patience; it is unblocked only by changing what the store retains — which is MIR-128 (consolidation instead of a FIFO window) and MIR-131 (hygiene the autonomous path can actually run). Those are therefore upstream of this decision, not parallel to it. Recorded so the next reader does not re-defer this entry on the same "wait for density" reasoning for a third time.
- **DEFAULT UNDER THE ESCALATION CONTRACT (2026-08-22, `docs/audit/ESCALATION_CONTRACT.md`):** the deferral stands as the decision, not as a postponement: identity stays tool-sequence-only until MIR-128/MIR-131 change what the store retains. No third density-wait review is scheduled — the unblocking is upstream by measurement. Reversed by one word from the operator.
- **Note:** fix remains a design decision (key = tools + goal-class/token signature, or per-goal sub-buckets) — Open Decision D-1 in `MEMORY_LIFECYCLE_CONTRACT.md` §16, still not prejudged.

### MIR-052 — a programming error in episode construction is masked as a memory outage
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-043 (found during its follow-up work). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-20, during MIR-049 implementation).
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_methods2.py` `_record_experience_memory` — the whole build-and-write block sits under one `except Exception`, logged as `smart_memory_error`. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** a `TypeError` from `episode_from_agent_cycle` (a caller passing an argument the signature does not accept) was swallowed and surfaced only as "no episode was written". Observed live: the delta was zero and every test reported nothing changed, with no failure anywhere pointing at the real cause. A signature or invariant violation is indistinguishable from a corrupt state file.
- **Why it matters:** this is the boundary that hid the previous defect. A future feedback bug will present the same way — "delta is zero" — and cost the same detour.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-07-29, #190). ⬆ from `open`.
- **Fix:** the build step is now guarded separately from the writes. A `TypeError` out of `episode_from_agent_cycle` propagates as the call-signature defect it is; every other failure — at the factory or downstream in the store writes — stays best-effort exactly as before, so a genuine storage fault (corrupt file, lock contention, disk error) still degrades quietly instead of aborting a user-facing answer. Removing the guard outright was the rejected option: it trades a hidden bug for a crashed answer, which is the worse failure.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_episode_write_error_visibility.py` (3), fail-before: a bad call to the episode factory raises; it is not reported as `smart_memory_error`; a simulated storage failure still degrades quietly — the third pins the over-correction so the guard cannot simply be deleted later.

### MIR-053 — the test environment is not network-deny by default
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-20, during MIR-049 implementation).
- **Symptom:** a diagnostic script run outside pytest called the real provider (`gpt-5.6-terra`, one billed request) because `load_dotenv` picked up live keys. Nothing refused it. Test fixtures do delete API keys and set `AGENT_ALLOW_MOCK_ROUTING`, but that is a per-file convention each author must remember, not an enforced boundary.
- **Why it matters:** correctness of the test suite currently depends on every future script being written correctly. The failure is silent and costs real money; it also means a "test" run can mutate external state.
- **Status:** `partially_fixed` (2026-08-03) ⬆ from `open`. The **suite half is closed**: a session-scoped guard in `tests/conftest.py` (`_deny_outbound_network`) makes an outbound `connect`/`connect_ex` (any socket the patch reaches, TCP included) raise loudly by default; loopback stays open (local test servers), opt-in is explicit via `AGENT_TESTS_ALLOW_NETWORK=1`. The full suite passes with the deny active — no existing test was dialing out. The formerly-missing test exists verbatim: `tests/test_network_deny.py` (probe target 192.0.2.1, RFC 5737 TEST-NET-1, non-routable — proving the old behaviour sent nothing anywhere real). **Still open:** diagnostic scripts run OUTSIDE pytest — a conftest cannot reach them; credential-stripping at session level was deliberately NOT added because `tests/conftest.py` documents (PR #178) that CI runs under real secrets and the placeholder fixture defers to them; the socket deny makes key presence harmless inside the suite instead.
- **Fix direction (remaining half):** diagnostic scripts requiring explicit opt-in for network access (e.g. a shared bootstrap refusing `load_dotenv` keys unless a flag is passed).
- **Missing tests:** ~~an unmocked provider call inside the suite fails rather than dialing out~~ — landed 2026-08-03 (`tests/test_network_deny.py`).
- **Reconciliation 2026-08-14 (registry restored after nine days without a status owner):** **unchanged.** `tests/conftest.py` did change in `88fd261..HEAD` (three commits: `StreamingFakeLLM`, the `AGENT_CATALOG_AUTOREFRESH=0` default, stdin-reader hang guards) — none of them touches this entry. The remaining half is by definition outside pytest, and `_deny_outbound_network` / `AGENT_TESTS_ALLOW_NETWORK` still appear in no script under `scripts/` or `tools/` that runs on its own. Status stays `partially_fixed`.

- **FIELD-CHECKED 2026-08-23; unchanged, and reproduced by accident the same day.** The suite half remains closed. The remaining half — diagnostic scripts run OUTSIDE pytest — has no guard of any kind: `load_dotenv` appears only in `agent_tick.py`, there is no shared bootstrap that scripts must pass through, and no opt-in flag gates a script's network access. A grep for any refusal outside `tests/` returns nothing.
- **Reproduced.** Today's MIR-045 measurement was itself such a script: `build_agent` was constructed outside pytest against a copy of the live workspace, and the session log recorded `llm_provider=openai, llm_model=gpt-4o-mini`. Nothing refused it. It happened to make no billed call because the maintenance pass ran dry — luck, not a boundary.
- **Not a risk for the unattended week**, and that is why it stays where it is: during the week only `agent_tick` runs, and it is SUPPOSED to call providers. This entry's exposure is developer-side — a future diagnostic script spending real money silently — so it neither blocks the launch nor is made safer by it.
- **Status:** `partially_fixed`, unchanged. Fix direction unchanged: a shared bootstrap for scripts that refuses `load_dotenv` keys unless a flag is passed.

### MIR-054 — conflict detection reads source code as factual claims (16/16 false positives)
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-047 (made these actionable), MIR-011. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-20, live REPL run).
- **Files/functions:** `core/knowledge_pipeline.py` `_subject_value` (`:560-578`) — pattern `^(.{3,80}?)\s+(?:is|are|=|:)\s+(.{1,120})$`.
- **Symptom:** the pattern treats an assignment as a proposition, so `path = self._path_for(trace_id)` and `path = (workspace / DEFAULT_APPROVAL_INBOX_PATH)` become contradictory statements about a subject named *path*. On the live registry (3105 claims) **all 16 detected conflicts are false**: every subject is a Python identifier (`path`, `payload`, `tags`, `parsed`, `objective`, `tool`, `scheduler`, `report`, `tests`, `implementation`, `evidence`, `_valid_statuses`, `approval_inbox_path`, `budget_ledger_path`) or a docstring template (`this module`, 13 claims; `anything extra`). 52 claims are involved. Zero are genuine contradictions.
- **Why it matters now:** it used to be harmless noise, because conflict review only reported. **MIR-047 made it act** — a conflicted claim now quarantines the memory record it produced. We made the mechanism effective without checking the quality of its input.
- **Blast radius today:** none yet. Quarantine only targets records carrying a `claim:<id>` provenance tag, and that tag was added the same day, so no existing record can be hit. The exposure begins at the next `:ingest-project`, where legitimate records would be withdrawn from retrieval because two unrelated files assign the same variable name.
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20)** ⬆ from `open`. Acceptance criterion met: **0 conflicts on the live registry, was 16.**
- **Resolution:** the fix is about *provenance*, not a smarter string heuristic. `ConflictResolver.resolve` now skips claims whose source does not assert anything: `memory` (the agent's own prior output — two goals recorded by two runs are two tasks, and citing yourself is not a second source), `log` / `tool_output` / `test_result` (observations of one moment, not standing claims), `code_repository`, and any source whose locator ends in a code extension.
- **Written as a blacklist, deliberately.** The first attempt was a whitelist of "propositional" types and it was wrong twice: it dropped prose held in `file` sources (`docs.md`, `a.txt`) and it dropped `forum`. A forum post *does* assert things — it asserts them unreliably, which is what `trust_level` exists for. Excluding it would silently discard a real disagreement instead of resolving it, and six existing tests caught exactly that.
- **Live verification:** 16 → 0. The subjects that disappeared were `path`, `this module` (13 claims), `evidence`, `report`, `tags`, `payload`, `implementation`, `tests`, `approval_inbox_path`, `scheduler`, `budget_ledger_path`, `anything extra`, `tool`, `_valid_statuses`, `parsed`, `objective` — every one a Python identifier or a docstring opener.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_conflict_source_scope.py` — 8 passing, all fail-before. The live-registry assertion names any survivor, so a partial fix cannot pass quietly. Four cases pin why each class was false; three GUARDs prove a genuine contradiction between prose sources still raises (two articles, documentation vs a book), and that code cannot refute an article.
- **Original fix direction (kept):** the subject/value split is meaningful for prose, not for code. Options: exclude code-derived claims from conflict detection entirely; reject subjects that look like identifiers (snake_case, leading underscore, ALL_CAPS) or generic docstring openers; or require the conflicting claims to share a source *type* where the proposition form is meaningful. Whichever is chosen must be measured against this same live registry — a fix that leaves any of these 16 standing has not worked.
- **Missing tests:** two assignments of the same variable in different files do not conflict; a genuine contradiction between two prose sources still does; the live-registry false-positive count is 0.

### MIR-055 — memory retrieval logs what it selected, never what it rejected (`selected=0` is unreadable)
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-052 (same class — a diagnosable event presented as an outage), MIR-043 (the unattended profile that holds the stores while being denied reads), MIR-049 (`offered` is the counterfactual to its `applied`). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-20, from reading live logs during the MIR-054 work).
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_methods2.py` `_retrieve_experience_memory` (`:221`; log at `:315`, early return at `:246`), `_retrieve_persistent` (`:132`; logs at `:154`, `:167`, `:183`), `_record_experience_memory` (`:482`; `procedure_feedback` log at `:605`). <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** `episodes_selected=0` against a store holding 200 episodes is true and useless — it cannot distinguish "nothing matched the question" from "everything matched and was withheld," and those two call for opposite responses. Same hole in `procedure_feedback`: `applied=0` cannot distinguish "no procedure was suggested" from "two were suggested and neither was applied" — only the second says anything about retrieval quality. And when `experience_retrieval` was denied the method returned silently, so a permission denial and an empty store produced identical logs.
- **Why it matters:** three defects in the same week were found by reading a live log, and each time the missing piece was why a record did **not** come back. The instrument used to find memory defects was blind on exactly the axis the defects live on.
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20, commit `8c81b86`)** — registered *after* the fix; discovery and repair happened in one pass, so this entry is a record, not a work item.
- **Resolution:** three sites now carry the counterfactual under a bounded reason vocabulary — `experience_memory_inject` → `rejected_by {outcome, not_eligible, over_limit, retrieval_disabled}`; `persistent_memory_inject` → `rejected_by {role_scope, below_threshold}`; `procedure_feedback` → `offered`. Counts are aggregated **by reason, never per record**, and a reason that did not fire is **absent rather than zero**. A per-record trace would grow with the store and cost more than the retrieval it observes — the same failure mode as switching a mechanism on without checking the volume of its input, which is how MIR-054 happened. `retrieval_disabled` is logged as its own reason rather than staying silent, because the unattended profile (MIR-043) legitimately holds the stores while being denied reads.
- **Live verification (copy of the live stores; nothing mutated):** `experience_memory_inject → selected 0, rejected_by {not_eligible: 6}`; `persistent_memory_inject → selected 1, rejected_by {role_scope: 6, below_threshold: 95}`. The first line previously read only `episodes_selected=0`.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_retrieval_rejection_trace.py` — 6 passing, all fail-before: quarantined episodes named as the reason, reasons kept separate, no rejections → an empty mapping rather than zeros, `retrieval_disabled` as its own reason, counts aggregated not per record (this one pins the vocabulary as bounded so it cannot drift into a per-record dump), and the persistent-side reasons. Full suite 4872 passed / 0 failed.
- **Not addressed here (by design):** the trace observes retrieval, it does not change what retrieval selects. `not_eligible: 6` on the live store is a reading of MIR-043's declared residual (episodes written `usage_eligible=False`), surfaced rather than resolved.
- **Correction (2026-07-20, superseded in part by MIR-056):** the trace was real but its **numbers were not trustworthy**. The persistent side computed `rejected_by` by subtracting list lengths, which mislabelled **4 of 6** realistic questions on the live store, and the experience side counted only what `search` returned, so `rejected_by == {}` on a 200-episode store stayed reachable — the exact line this entry exists to eliminate. What is `fixed` here is the *presence* of a counterfactual at three sites; the *correctness and completeness* of the counts is MIR-056. A reader quoting numbers logged between `8c81b86` and the MIR-056 fix should treat the persistent-side reasons as unreliable.

### MIR-056 — rejection reasons are inferred by the observer instead of reported by the decider
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-055 (this is its root; that entry logged the trace, this one makes the numbers true), MIR-054 (same recurring shape — a mechanism switched on without checking what it was actually consuming). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-20, operator challenge: "find the root, it may be in another file").
- **Files/functions:** the root is **not** in the file MIR-055 was fixed in. `core/memory_policy.py` `MemoryRetrievalPolicy.select` returned survivors only; `core/smart_memory.py` `EpisodicMemoryStore.search` likewise; `core/knowledge_use_policy.py` `KnowledgeUsePolicy.filter` did know its three distinct causes but expressed them only as prose in `KnowledgeUseDecision.reasons`. The observer, `core/loop_methods2.py` `_retrieve_persistent`, therefore reconstructed causes arithmetically: `("role_scope", len(records) - len(allowed))`, `("below_threshold", len(allowed) - len(selected))`. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** subtraction cannot separate causes it never saw, so the trace reported confidently wrong reasons.
  - **Persistent side, measured on the live store (103 records, read-only):** **4 of 6** realistic questions were mislabelled. Worst case `procedural confidence beta smoothing` → logged `below_threshold: 100`, truth `below_threshold: 28, over_limit: 72`. "Almost nothing here is relevant" and "72 relevant records were thrown away by a cap of 3" are opposite diagnoses — which is verbatim the sentence MIR-055 was written to make sayable.
  - A stopword-only question (`и в`) blamed the store — `below_threshold: 103` — when the truth is `no_query_tokens: 103`, a query-side defect. The reader is sent to the wrong subsystem.
  - `role_scope` absorbed all three of the use policy's reject branches, so a **quarantined** record (`obsolete`, `conflicted` — MIR-047's own tags) was indistinguishable from a record outside the role's scope.
  - **Experience side, live store (200 episodes):** the loop iterated `search`'s survivors, so everything the search itself dropped was invisible. `quarterly revenue in singapore` → `episodes_selected=0, rejected_by={}`.
- **Why it matters:** a wrong reason is worse than an absent one. An absent reason makes a reader look; a wrong reason makes them look in the wrong place and stop. This trace exists specifically to be the instrument used when hunting memory defects.
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20)** — registered and fixed in one pass, after the MIR-055 entry was challenged.
- **Resolution — the decider reports, the observer merges.** `MemoryRetrievalPolicy.select_with_report` → `RetrievalSelection(selected, rejected_by)` with `no_query_tokens` / `below_threshold` / `over_limit`; `EpisodicMemoryStore.search_with_report` → `EpisodeSearchResult(episodes, rejected_by)` with `no_query_tokens` / `no_overlap` / `over_limit`; `KnowledgeUseDecision.code` gives the existing prose a machine-readable companion (`role_scope` / `quarantined` / `not_applicable`) and `KnowledgeUseReport.rejected_by` aggregates it. `select` and `search` now **delegate** to their reporting siblings, so there is one decision implementation rather than two that can drift. `_merge_rejection_reasons` in `core/loop_methods2.py` sums what each stage reported; no call site subtracts anything. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Accounting invariant (the property that retires subtraction):** `len(selected) + sum(rejected_by.values()) == len(candidates)`. Verified on all 7 live queries. The one place it cannot hold is the repair-lesson second door, which re-admits an episode the first pass already charged to a reason; rather than guess which bucket to debit, that is reported as its own `readmitted` count, and the reconciliation is stated at the call site.
- **Deliberately kept:** counts stay aggregated **by reason, never per record** — the volume rule from MIR-055 is what makes the trace affordable on a 200-episode store, and a per-record trace would have made the bucket question moot at the cost of the trace itself.
- **Live verification (read-only, nothing mutated):** persistent `procedural confidence beta smoothing` → `{'below_threshold': 28, 'over_limit': 72}`; `и в` → `{'no_query_tokens': 103}`; episodic `quarterly revenue in singapore` → `{'no_overlap': 200}` (was `{}`); `how do I deploy the service` → `{'no_overlap': 76, 'over_limit': 118}`. Every query accounted for exactly.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_retrieval_rejection_at_source.py` — 10 passing, **all 10 verified fail-before** by stashing `core/` and re-running. Three at the root (policy reports its own reasons; a cap is separated from a floor; `select` returns exactly what the report selected — the anti-drift guard), two on the accounting invariant, and five through the loop (capped records not called below-threshold, quarantine not called role-scope, drops inside `search` still reported, full-store accounting).
- **Full suite:** 4882 passed, 0 failed.

### MIR-057 — evidence quality is banked as task completion: a grounded non-completion becomes a `success` that trains a procedure
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-001 (same field, narrower manifestation — marked `fixed`, and its code comment explicitly puts this case out of scope), MIR-002 (the other reader of the evidence axis), MIR-048/MIR-049 (the feedback loop that turns this from inert to active), MIR-006 (universal success criterion), MIR-043 (the eligibility gate this rides through). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-20, live run).
- **Discovery note (historical, NOT an alias):** in the preceding working session this defect was provisionally proposed as **MIR-055**. Before it was formally registered, that number was mistakenly taken by a different finding — retrieval observability — in commit `fca27ed`. Numbering was deliberately left unchanged by operator decision (2026-07-20), because renumbering would make committed messages and the registry denote different problems. **MIR-055 is not an alias of this entry and never described this defect in any committed document.**
- **Files/functions:** `core/smart_memory.py` `episode_from_agent_cycle` (`:959-985`) derives `outcome`; `decide_usage_eligibility` (`:877`, rules `:907-913`) consumes it; `ProceduralMemoryStore.upsert_from_episode` credits the procedure.
- **Scope — grounded non-completion, not only refusal:** the class is *any* cycle that ends without achieving the goal while reporting that honestly and with citations. The live case is a **blocked** answer — the evidence budget truncated the file, so the fact needed to answer was never available — not a policy refusal and not an inability of the model. Declining, being blocked by a budget or a permission, and stopping part-way all present identically to the outcome derivation, which is why the entry is scoped to the class rather than to refusals.
- **Symptom (live store, read-only, verified in the data rather than quoted from a log):** the **only** episode ever admitted to steer later answers is a blocked non-completion.
  - `ep-run-run_fb343a5734836e699c81e6eea06e24c8`, 2026-07-20T00:12:25 — question *«сколько строк в файле core/loop_methods2.py»*, summary *«Точное число строк определить нельзя: предоставленное содержимое обрезано»*, `outcome=success`, `v=3 u=2 w=0`, `usage_eligible=True`. <!-- historical-ref -->
  - The agent did **not** answer, said honestly why — the content it was given was truncated — and was rewarded for saying it well.
  - **The inverse in the same store:** `ep-run-run_bfedf6e529bb090adf0b43afc6ab44a3` — *«17 × 23 = 391»*, a complete and correct answer — is `usage_eligible=False`, because general knowledge carries no verified chunks. The system admitted the non-answer and quarantined the answer. That pair is the defect stated in two records.
- **Root cause:** `outcome` is derived from chunk counts alone, and **every** branch reads the evidence axis — `aborted`/`replan_exhausted` → `failed`; `unverified > verified` → `partial`; `weak >= verified` → `partial`; otherwise `success`. Nothing in the derivation asks whether the goal was achieved. Verification measures whether claims are grounded; it cannot measure whether the question was answered. A well-cited non-completion scores well **because** it honestly cited why it could not finish. Two different questions were folded into one field, and the field is named after the one it does not measure.
- **Second-order propagation:** the conflated value does not stay in the episode. `decide_usage_eligibility` gates admission on `outcome == "success"` and `verified_chunks > 0` — both evidence, neither completion — and `upsert_from_episode` credits the procedure. One wrong field becomes a wrong admission and a wrong reputation.
- **Blast radius (measured 2026-07-20, read-only, nothing mutated):**
  - **Procedure `tools:file_read`: `success_count=5, failure_count=0, confidence=0.857, status=active`, and its most recent `source_episode_id` is exactly the blocked episode, `updated_at` matching to the second.** The chain non-completion → `success` → eligible → procedure credit → injected into planning is closed and observed, not hypothesised. Note the credited procedure did its job — `file_read` executed; what failed was upstream of it, which is precisely why a blanket debit would be as wrong as the current blanket credit.
  - No longer inert: MIR-048 made procedural feedback reachable, so each false success now actively raises confidence rather than sitting unused.
