  - **Legacy exposure — 177 of 198 unclassified episodes would be admitted by the current policy:** 108 through the `lesson` exception (which bypasses `outcome` by design), of which **69 carry a stored `failed` (30) or `partial` (39)**; the remaining 69 are admitted on stored `outcome == "success"`. **64 of the 177 carry explicit refusal/incompleteness text** (47 of them stored as `success`). 116 carry at least one signal; 61 carry none. A further 5 stored `success` values predate the MIR-001 fix and today's logic would call them `partial`. **Read this as policy exposure, not live exposure:** it counts what `decide_usage_eligibility` would admit if these records were re-classified. Retrieval additionally requires the stored `usage_eligible` bit, which no legacy row carries, so the number actually reaching a prompt was already 0 — see the corrected residual below.
  - **Consequence for MIR-043's residual:** the `usage_eligible` legacy migration is **blocked** on this entry (operator decision, 2026-07-20). It gates on this same `outcome`, so running it would launder the defect onto 177 records at once instead of one per cycle.
- **Status:** `fixed` (verified against code and live probes 2026-08-02). ⬆ from `open` — the entry had gone stale: fix direction **(b)** was implemented by the completion-axis work (branch `mir-057-task-completion-axis`; `CompletionState`, `assemble_completion_state`, `procedure_credit_allowed`, all of which cite this entry in their own comments) and the registry was never updated. Checked criterion by criterion rather than assumed:
  1. **The live blocked shape** (`v=3, u=2`, declared blocked) probed through `episode_from_agent_cycle`: `completion_state="blocked"`, `procedure_credit_allowed=False`, `decide_usage_eligibility=False`. `outcome` still reads `success` — by design: option (b) keeps it as the evidence axis, and no consumer reads the old conflation (credit and admission both gate on the completion axis). Pinned: `test_completion_procedural_gates.py::test_a_blocked_success_earns_nothing`.
  2. **`blocked`/`refused`/`partially_achieved` never raise `success_count`** — credit requires `effective_completion == "achieved"` AND `outcome == "success"`; probed all three → `False`. Pinned: `::test_neither_credit_nor_debit_for_the_middle_states`, `::test_both_axes_are_required_for_credit`.
  3. **A complete answer with a local disclaimer stays credited** — probed `achieved` with `v=4, u=1` → credit `True`. Pinned: `::test_an_achieved_success_still_earns_credit`.
  4. **Neutrality** — `procedure_debit_allowed` debits only what the run itself demonstrates (exhausted replan); declared failure, blocked, cancelled are all neutral, each with its reason in the docstring. Pinned: `::test_a_declared_failure_is_neutral`, `::test_a_cancelled_run_is_neutral`, `::test_an_abort_tag_alone_does_not_authorise_a_debit`.
  5. **`lesson` is separated, not merely preserved** — retrievable-as-context vs replayable-as-proven are now different doors: `test_completion_episodic_gates.py::test_an_unknown_lesson_is_still_retrievable_as_context` and `::test_an_unknown_lesson_is_not_replayable`; procedure credit is gated independently of the tag.
  6. **Legacy stays read-only and fail-closed** — `scripts/completion_legacy_report.py` is the read-only deliverable (tested in `test_completion_legacy_report.py`); no reclassification ran, and the boundary fix under MIR-064 (2026-08-02, D-6 ruling) keeps new rows from joining the unclassified population. The `usage_eligible` migration remains a separate task, per this entry's own scoping and the operator's no-reconstruction rule.
  - The proposed test file name (`test_outcome_is_completion_not_evidence.py`) was never created; its proposed content landed across `test_completion_procedural_gates.py`, `test_completion_episodic_gates.py` and `test_completion_axis_schema.py`, which is where the criteria above point.
- **Why the marker scan is not the fix:** the keyword scan above is a **sizing instrument, not a proposed mechanism**. It can over- and under-count, and shipping it as a gate would replace a wrong signal with an unreliable one.
- **Historical (pre-fix, 2026-07-20) — there was no completion signal in the system then.** Kept as the analysis that motivated the axis; the shipped `CompletionState` is that signal now. This must not be understated into "a signal exists and is not consulted". What exists are detectors of *particular kinds of evidence shortfall* — `evaluate_low_evidence_policy` (`core/low_evidence_policy.py:210`), the `⚠️ Не подтверждено` note (`core/loop_helpers.py:356`), the `Unverified:` section marker (`core/loop_helpers.py:125`). Each answers "is this claim sufficiently supported"; **none of them establishes that the goal was reached**, and a cycle can be blocked with every claim it does make fully supported — which is exactly the live case. Treating these as a completion signal would repeat this defect one layer up: a second evidence measure wearing a completion label. **A structured completion signal has to be produced, not discovered.** <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Fix direction (historical; option (b) below was chosen and shipped — see Status):** (a) Minimal: derive a structured non-completion state (blocked / refused / partial) at answer time and forbid it from being `success`. (b) Structural: give completion its own axis beside the evidence axis, the way MIR-041 separated `verification_status` from `response_origin`, rather than overloading `outcome` a second time. **(b) should be weighed first** — this entry exists because one field was asked to answer two questions, and (a) risks answering the second one with a third proxy.
- **Closure criterion (measurable, all required):**
  1. the live blocked episode above does not bank `success`, and its procedure credit does not occur;
  2. a structured `blocked` / `refused` / `partial` state never raises any procedure's `success_count`;
  3. a complete, well-evidenced answer is still `success` — the fix must not buy correctness by declaring everything incomplete; a complete answer carrying a *local* disclaimer about one claim is still a completed task;
  4. **`blocked` / `refused` are NEUTRAL for the procedure that ran** — no credit and no debit — unless the procedure was causally responsible for the non-completion. A budget truncation or a missing permission is not evidence that `file_read` is a bad workflow, and debiting it would replace a false credit with a false failure. Attribution already exists for this (MIR-049 `used_procedure_ids`); causal responsibility is a further judgement and must not be inferred from mere participation;
  5. **`lesson` is clarified, not merely preserved:** the tag may keep a useful lesson from a `partial`/`failed` cycle retrievable, because learning from failure is its purpose — but it must **not** make the episode a replayable `success`, and it must **not** produce positive procedural feedback. Today it does both, by admitting the episode whole through `decide_usage_eligibility` before `outcome` is ever read. Retrievable-as-a-lesson and reusable-as-a-proven-workflow must become separable;
  6. **legacy is handled read-only and fail-closed, not reclassified.** The 198 unclassified episodes stay withheld; the deliverable here is a read-only report of what the corrected model *would* say about them. No automatic reclassification of the 47 stored-`success`-with-marker records — the marker is a sizing heuristic and cannot certify a rewrite. The `usage_eligible` migration is a **separate task, after the model is corrected**, and is out of scope for closing this entry.
- **Proposed fail-before tests** (historical; the named file `tests/test_outcome_is_completion_not_evidence.py` was never created — the content landed across `test_completion_procedural_gates.py`, `test_completion_episodic_gates.py` and `test_completion_axis_schema.py`, mapped criterion-by-criterion in the Status block above):
  1. `test_a_well_cited_blocked_answer_does_not_bank_success` — the exact live shape (`v=3, u=2`, goal not reached) is not `success`;
  2. `test_structured_non_completion_cannot_be_success` — `blocked`, `refused` and `partial` each fail the `success` gate on their own, independent of how well-evidenced the answer is;
  3. `test_a_complete_answer_with_a_local_disclaimer_is_still_success` — a finished task whose one caveated claim carries `⚠️ Не подтверждено` is **not** demoted; this is the guard that keeps (2) from degenerating into "any hedge means failure";
  4. `test_non_completion_does_not_credit_a_procedure` — `success_count` byte-identical after the cycle;
  5. `test_non_completion_is_neutral_for_an_uninvolved_procedure` — no debit either, when the procedure was not causally responsible;
  6. `test_a_non_completion_is_not_usage_eligible` — admission refuses it even if outcome is forced;
  7. `test_a_complete_evidenced_answer_is_still_success` — guard against over-correction;
  8. `test_a_lesson_stays_retrievable_without_becoming_a_replayable_success` — the `lesson` clarification: still surfaced as a lesson, not admitted as a proven workflow, and no positive procedural feedback;
  9. `test_general_knowledge_behaviour_is_unchanged` — `v=0,u=0` stays where MIR-002 left it, so this fix does not silently move that boundary;
  10. `test_legacy_stays_withheld` — the corrected model does not admit any of the 198 unclassified episodes as a side effect; legacy remains fail-closed until its own migration task.
- **Implementation (landed 2026-07-20, six commits):** `210187f` schema — `declared_completion` + `completion_state`, assembled at banking and frozen, legacy never reconstructed. `7b54aa8` a crashed verifier no longer credits a procedure (narrow half, fixed on its own). `c4eda7b` the synthesizer declares through an attempt-bound nonce marker, stripped once so the verifier, the user and storage see the same text. `be585eb` the three episodic readers require `achieved`. `e559c90` `procedure_credit_allowed` / `procedure_debit_allowed`, credit needing both axes and debit narrowed to a run-demonstrated failure. `bfba72d` the read-only legacy report. Structural overrides outrank the declaration throughout; no reader consults `declared_completion`.
- **Shadow measurement before the gates went live** (worktree at `c4eda7b`, throwaway stores, no credentials, mock routing, live data untouched) — 16 controlled cycles: **0 false-achieved, 0 marker/nonce leakage**, frozen state matched the manual label **13/16** with **3 safe abstentions** (marker forgotten, token invented, wrong nonce — all `unknown`), the blocked live-case correct, and user content preserved verbatim. Real marker compliance was deliberately NOT claimed: the synthesizer output was scripted, so a compliance figure would have been true by construction. It is measured during the planned local autonomous end-to-end runs.
- **Residual, measured read-only after the gates landed (2026-07-20):** all 200 live episodes read `unknown`; **0** pass positive reuse, fast-path, procedural credit or procedural debit, **and 0 pass the curated-`lesson` arm either**. ⚠ **Correction (2026-07-20, found in a live run):** this line first said 108 lessons were still admitted. That was wrong. The figure came from `decide_usage_eligibility`, the banking-time policy, which returns True for a lesson whatever its stored bit says. **Retrieval reads `is_usage_eligible` — the stored bit — and a legacy row carries none**, so the live agent admits no legacy episode at all, lessons included. Confirmed in a live cycle: `rejected_by={'no_overlap': 122, 'over_limit': 72, 'not_eligible': 6}` — six legacy lessons refused by the bit, not by completion. The diagnostic and its test now model retrieval rather than the policy. **Safe episode backfill candidates: 0** — `replan_exhausted` is the only durable fact the assembly table can classify from, and no live record carries it, so the legacy episodes are unclassifiable rather than merely unclassified and there is no migration input. The separate residual on the procedural side is **MIR-058**. Reproduce with `python scripts/completion_legacy_report.py`.

### MIR-059 — a working binary is swapped for a broken one, and the arguments are not translated with it
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-010 (found in the same live cycle; that entry made the failure VISIBLE, this one is why it happened at all). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-20, live run).
- **Files/functions:** `tools/shell_exec.py` `_platform_alias` applied unconditionally at the dispatch site.
- **Symptom:** a cycle asked for the line count of `core/loop.py`, the planner produced `grep -c ^ core/loop.py`, and the answer came back `blocked` — for an environmental reason with nothing to do with the question. The tool had swapped `grep` for `findstr` even though real grep was installed, and then handed it a path it cannot parse.
- **Root cause, two independent faults:**
  1. **The swap was unconditional.** `_platform_alias` mapped `grep`->`findstr` on Windows whether or not the requested binary existed. Measured: `shutil.which("grep")` resolves to Git's `usr/bin/grep.EXE`, and that binary answers `grep -c ^ core/loop.py` with `4093` using **either** slash style. A working tool was replaced by a failing one.
  2. **Only the NAME was translated, never the arguments.** `findstr` reads `/` as a switch prefix, so `core/loop.py` parses as `core` plus `/l /o /o /p` — hence `FINDSTR: Cannot open loop.py`. Reproduced directly: the same command with a backslash path returns 0.
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20)**.
- **Resolution:** `_resolve_binary` prefers the requested command whenever it is on PATH and falls back to the platform equivalent only when it is absent, returning `(name, substituted)`. `_normalise_argv_for` rewrites path separators **only when a substitution actually happened** — the requested binary already understands its own dialect, and rewriting for it would be damage. The output records `executed_command` and `binary_substituted`, because the live log showed `argv: ['grep', ...]` beside `FINDSTR:` in stderr with nothing connecting them, and a substitution nobody can see is one nobody can debug.
- **Flags are deliberately NOT translated.** `grep -c` has no findstr equivalent worth guessing at, and a half-built dialect mapper turns every unmapped flag into a new silent failure. After MIR-010 a fallback that cannot read its arguments fails visibly, which leaves the planner able to see it and re-plan.
- **Live verification:** the exact failing invocation now returns `executed_command=grep, substituted=False, exit_code=0, stdout='4093', execution_status=success` — where it previously produced `blocked`.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_shell_binary_resolution.py` — 8 passing, 7 fail-before. Covers an installed binary never being substituted, the equivalent used only as a fallback, an absent pair still raising, separators rewritten only on substitution, argv byte-identical without one, flags passed through untranslated, the executed binary recorded, and the live invocation end to end.
- **Residual:** resolution depends on PATH, so the same argv can reach different binaries from different shells. That is now honest (use what is installed) rather than silently wrong (always swap), but it stays environment-dependent and worth remembering when reading a log.

### MIR-058 — legacy procedural counters lack completion provenance
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-057 (its forward policy stopped the accrual this entry is about), MIR-051 (same store, different defect — that was an invariant violation in a derived field, this is standing earned under a superseded rule), MIR-050 (identity, which bounds what any repair could mean). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-20, measured while building the MIR-057 legacy report).
- **Files/functions:** data in `data/procedural_memory.jsonl`. The code is correct as of `e559c90`: `procedure_credit_allowed` and `procedure_debit_allowed` gate every path. Nothing writes an unverifiable counter any more.
- **Symptom:** MIR-057's gates are forward-only. They stopped new credit; they did not unwind credit already granted. **47 of 65 procedures hold non-zero counters together with source references to episodes that carry no completion verdict** — aggregate `success=72, failure=0`. Those procedures are `active` (46) or `needs_review` (1), and are still ranked by confidence and injected into planning: the retrieval path reads `confidence`/`status`, not completion, because a procedure is not an episode and has no completion axis of its own.
- **What is proven and what is not — stated precisely because the distinction is the whole entry:** `source_episode_ids` is an applied-feedback journal. It proves a procedure was **updated by** an episode; it does **not** prove that a particular increment came from that episode. Counters are aggregates and there is no per-increment ledger, so **individual increments are not attributable to individual episodes.** References to episodes already evicted by the store's 200-record FIFO window cannot be examined at all (`missing` in the report; e.g. `tools:file_read->list_dir` holds 10 legacy references out of 13, 3 unexaminable). The honest claim is therefore **"these counters cannot be verified under the completion policy"**, not "these counters are wrong" — some of that standing was very likely earned by genuinely completed work.
- **ПЕРЕЗАМЕРЕНО 2026-08-25 её же инструментом (`scripts/completion_legacy_report.py`), и число упало по ХУДШЕЙ из причин.** Процедур с ненулевыми счётчиками и непроверяемыми источниками: **10 из 34** (было 47 из 65). Но неклассифицируемых старых ссылок теперь **0**, а ссылок на **удалённые из хранилища эпизоды — 47**. Счётчики стали непроверяемыми не потому, что их починили, а потому, что уборка вынесла улику, оставив зачёт.
- **Самый наглядный случай, одной строкой отчёта.** `tools:file_read`: **31 ссылка на эпизоды, все 31 отсутствуют**, успехов 24, уверенность **0,962**, статус `active`. Самая уверенная и самая используемая процедура опирается на улику, которой не осталось ни одной строки.
- **Механизм найден 2026-08-25 и записан в MIR-128.** Уборка эпизодов о процедурах не знает вовсе (слова «procedur» нет ни в `core/memory_hygiene.py`, ни в `core/loop_hygiene.py`), а связь «какие эпизоды подпирают процедуры» ВЫЧИСЛЯЕТСЯ (`consolidate_memory` → `linked_episode_ids`) и не имеет ни одного потребителя вне отчёта. То есть знание, которое защитило бы улику, есть и не используется.
- **Это подтверждает собственное предупреждение записи** («число дрейфует ВНИЗ по мере заполнения хранилища, и это не выздоровление») и уточняет его: дрейф идёт и при ОПУСТОШЕНИИ хранилища, причём сильнее — эпизоды удаляются, а начисленная по ним уверенность их переживает.
- **Status:** `open` (newly_discovered). Diagnosed only; no repair attempted and none designed.
- **Why it is not part of MIR-057:** distinct root (data written under a superseded rule vs. a policy that measured the wrong thing), distinct blast radius (65 stored procedures vs. every future cycle), distinct remediation (a decision about existing standing vs. a gate), and distinct completion criteria. Folding it in would let a data question ride out on a logic fix.
- **Evidence:** `python scripts/completion_legacy_report.py` — read-only, verifies its own sha256 before and after, and prints the per-procedure legacy-source coverage, the confidence distribution and the status distribution.
- **The count drifts DOWNWARD as the store fills, and that is not recovery.** The figures above were taken on 2026-07-20 with 200 legacy episodes. After a 14-cycle live run the same command reported **36 of 65, aggregate success=61** — not because standing was verified, but because the episodic store's 200-record FIFO window evicted the referenced episodes, so their references now read as `missing` and drop out of the count. The unverifiable credit is unchanged; only the evidence for identifying it is disappearing. Any remediation that waits will have less to work with, and a report run on a later date must be read with its date.
- **Fix direction (undecided — a real fork):** (a) leave the counters and let them be diluted by new, verifiable outcomes — cheapest, and honest so long as the report exists to explain them; (b) reset the unverifiable portion, which cannot be computed exactly for the reason above and would therefore be an estimate presented as a correction; (c) retire and re-earn — mark affected procedures `needs_review` so they must prove themselves again. **(c) is the only option that neither invents a number nor pretends the standing is verified**, but it costs the store most of its procedural memory at once, and MIR-050 has already measured that these keys pool unrelated goals, so what would be re-earned is of uncertain value. Do not decide this without also deciding MIR-050.
- **RE-RUN 2026-08-22, and the fork this entry describes HAS CLOSED BY ATTRITION.** The report now prints `procedures with non-zero counters and legacy source references: 0 of 34`. That is not recovery, and this entry predicted it in the paragraph above. Measured directly against the two stores rather than trusting the headline: 34 procedures survive (was 65), **10 still carry non-zero counters** (aggregate success=31, failure=2), they hold **75 `source_episode_ids` between them, of which 5 resolve to an episode still in the store and 70 — 93% — are gone**. So the evidence trail is 93% destroyed. Option (b) "reset the unverifiable portion" can no longer be computed even as an estimate, and option (c) "retire and re-earn" can no longer identify WHICH procedures to retire. Waiting did not preserve the choice; it consumed it.
- **AND THE INSTRUMENT ITSELF IS NOW INVERTED — this is a defect in the sensor, not only in the data.** `scripts/completion_legacy_report.py:105` defines `legacy_ids` as episodes **present in the store** whose `completion_state` is `None`; line 127 counts a reference as legacy only if it resolves into that set; line 128 skips the procedure entirely when none do. An evicted episode therefore cannot be legacy by construction, so as the FIFO window turns over, affected procedures silently leave the report and take their own `refs_missing` count with them — that number is computed at line 141 but only printed inside `if touched:`. **The headline converges to zero exactly as the credit becomes less verifiable.** Today it reads `0 of 34` while 70 references point at nothing, and a reader who trusts the top line concludes the problem is gone. A sensor whose reading improves as the thing it measures gets worse is worse than no sensor; that is the same failure class this registry recorded for `expected_effect` and for the wall-class walls, arrived at from a different direction.
- **THE INSTRUMENT IS REPAIRED, 2026-08-22 evening — the data fork below stays the operator's.** A reference that resolves to nothing now counts as *unexaminable* rather than *not-legacy*: the skip condition requires BOTH no legacy refs AND no missing refs, and the headline prints the split («unclassified legacy refs: N; references to evicted episodes: M»). On the live store the same command that printed «0 of 34» in the morning now prints **«10 of 34 … references to evicted episodes: 47»**. Pinned by five tests in `tests/test_completion_legacy_report.py`, break-tested by restoring the inversion (both witnesses redden), including the boundary that zero-because-clean stays unflagged and that zero-counter procedures stay out — the report is about unverifiable standing, not reference hygiene. One of its own witnesses first passed for a false reason (the static prose always contains the word «missing») and was strengthened to require the affected procedure's own line — recorded because that is this day's instrument-of-the-instrument lesson again.
- **What a repair to the INSTRUMENT would have to do (the data fork stays the operator's, unprejudged — as written before the repair):** count a reference that resolves to nothing as *unexaminable* rather than as *not-legacy*, and report unexaminable separately from clean, so the two can never be read as one number. That is a reporting change with no effect on stored state, and it is the prerequisite for the data decision rather than a substitute for it — without it, any future run of this report will keep saying zero.
- **Missing tests:** whichever option is chosen needs a fail-before test that the affected procedures no longer present unverifiable standing as verified, plus a guard that verifiable standing is untouched. **Added 2026-08-22:** and a test that the report distinguishes zero-because-clean from zero-because-evicted — the case that now exists live and that the current report cannot express.

### MIR-060 — verification confirms that the source exists, never that the derived claim follows from it
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-046 (**same line of code, different axis** — see the no-merge basis below), MIR-042 (what may enter the chain), MIR-002 (the ratio these verdicts feed), MIR-057 (the other axis `outcome` cannot express). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-20, operator instruction during the structured-outputs capability spike).
- **Files/functions:** `core/verifier_core.py:120-153` — `ev = match_citation(c, chain)`; if it resolves and `strict_ok` survives, `any_matched = True` → `verdict = "verified"`. Content gates in that branch are exactly two: `_memory_citation_is_independent` (trust class, MIR-046's fix) and a literal figure-containment test gated on `is_statistical_claim` (`core/verifier_utils.py:59-69`, `core/verifier_patterns.py:49-72`).
- **Symptom:** a claim whose truth is a **computation over** the cited excerpt — a count, a sum, a difference, a ratio, a comparison, an inference — is marked `verified` because the citation resolved. The verifier never evaluates the claim against the excerpt, so it cannot distinguish a correct derivation from a false one.
- **Measured, in-memory, read-only (2026-07-20).** Synthetic chain, one `file` evidence with excerpt `"alpha=1\nbeta=2\ngamma=3\n"`, `verify(expects_contract_headers=False)`:

  | claim (all citing `[file:vals.txt]`) | true? | verdict |
  |---|---|---|
  | "The three values sum to 6" | yes | `verified` |
  | "The three values sum to 99" | **no** | `verified` |
  | "The file defines 7 keys" | **no** | `verified` |
  | "gamma is smaller than alpha" | **no** | `verified` |
  | "The file contains beta=2" | yes (literal) | `verified` |
  | "Coverage rose 40%" | **no** | `topic_supported_but_claim_unverified` |
  | "The average value is 12" | **no** | `topic_supported_but_claim_unverified` |

  Four false derived claims score **identically to the true one and to the literal quotation**. Only the last two differ, and only because `_STAT_TRIGGER_RE` fired on `%` / `average`.
- **The one band that does bite is containment, not evaluation.** Re-running "The average value is 12" against an excerpt extended with an unrelated `port=12` flips the verdict from `topic_supported_but_claim_unverified` to **`verified`**. The test asks whether the figure's characters appear somewhere in the excerpt; it does not compute the average, and it has no notion of which number belongs to which quantity. Its trigger set is also narrow — `%`, currency, `N x`, numeric ranges bearing a time unit, and the words `most`/`majority`/`average` (and Russian equivalents). A count, a sum, a line number, a latency in `ms`, a version comparison — none of them trigger it at all.
- **The semantic check is never consulted once a citation resolves.** `_find_semantic_support` (NLI) and `_find_structured_support` are reachable **only** from the branch where nothing matched (`core/verifier_core.py:163-188`). Measured directly: verifying "The three values sum to 99" with an LLM stub that answers `NO` to every NLI question yields `verified` and **0 NLI calls**. Resolution short-circuits evaluation — the machinery that could catch this exists in the same file and is structurally unreachable for exactly the claims that need it.
- **Why this is NOT part of MIR-046 (no-merge basis).** Same line, orthogonal axes. MIR-046 asked *whose* evidence may count and fixed it by adding `Evidence.origin` and demoting non-`user-explicit` memory. This entry asks *whether the cited content supports the claim* and is untouched by that fix — every case measured above uses a `file` evidence that MIR-046 considers fully independent. The connection is real and worth recording: MIR-046's own symptom line already noted "verified iff the citation resolves to a record in the chain, never because the content was checked", and then scoped its fix to the trust axis. **The content axis was observed there and never registered.** Distinct root, distinct fix (a claim-vs-excerpt entailment judgement vs. a provenance field), distinct closure criteria. Folding them together would let a fixed entry appear to cover an open one.
- **Blast radius.** `verified_chunks` is not a display value. It drives `_compute_quality_score` (MIR-002), the `outcome` derivation, `decide_usage_eligibility` (`verified_chunks > 0` is an admission condition), procedural credit, and the confidence gate. A false derived claim therefore does not merely reach the user — it can make its episode look well-supported enough to be admitted as reusable experience. The MIR-057 completion axis is an **independent** guard: it asks whether the goal was reached, not whether a claim was computed correctly, so a completed task resting on a false derivation passes both.
- **Operator-cited observations (supplied, not independently located):** responses **184 / 182 / 172** were cited as showing the verifier confirming the presence of a source while the computed assertion went unchecked. Those response records were **not found in this repository** — no `docs/`, `data/` or `logs/` artifact matches those identifiers. The mechanism above is measured and stands on its own; the three responses would ground it in live output and should be attached when their location is known.
- **Structured Outputs do not fix this, and must not be argued as if they might.** A JSON schema constrains the *shape* of what the synthesizer emits. This defect is in what the verifier *checks* about a claim's content, downstream of any transport. The two are independent; see `docs/audit/PROVIDER_STRUCTURED_OUTPUT_AUDIT.md` §8.
- **Architectural placement (added 2026-07-21; the defect above is unchanged by it).** The check this entry finds missing is named in the target architecture as §5 *Tool Result Validation → Semantic Validation*, and in §8 as *Evidence Consistency Check* / *Factuality Evaluation*. Recorded as orientation only: per that document's own reading rule a named component means "foreseen", not "implemented", and naming a slot neither explains the defect nor discharges it. **This is a reference, not a reclassification — MIR-060 stays a reproducible present defect with the measurements above, not "an unbuilt slot".**
- **The M1 memory-lifecycle contract does NOT close this (measured against the draft, 2026-07-21).** Its verification rules — `docs/audit/MEMORY_LIFECYCLE_CONTRACT.md` §7.4, §12, §14 — are entirely **trust-class** rules: a resolved citation counts `verified` unless the matched evidence's `trust_class` is `agent_derived` or `replay_echo`, and §7.4 states in terms that "citation resolution keeps exactly its current meaning". A `file`-kind evidence derives `trust_class = tool_observation` (§4.3), which is inside the allowed set, so **every case in the table above would still count `verified` under that contract as drafted**. This is not a fault in the contract — its §1 scopes it to record trust, the same axis as MIR-046 — but it must be recorded, because the contract is the canonical M1 design and planning from it would otherwise assume the content axis is covered. Checked by targeted section read plus keyword search across §6–§15 for entailment/semantic/content-check vocabulary; the only hits are trust-class rules.
- **Related documentation already noted the property without registering it.** `docs/audit/MEMORY_MAP.md` §9 records that "the verdict tracks **citation resolution, not content**", and §12 summarises "anything that can be cited and resolves in the chain counts as `verified`". Both observations were carried into MIR-046 and fixed there **on the trust axis only**. `docs/MEMORY_FIX_PLAN.md` A1–A8 contains no item for claim-vs-excerpt evaluation. So the content axis has been visible in three documents and registered in none until this entry.
- **Status:** `partially_fixed` (2026-08-14, reconciliation after the registry was restored) ⬆ from `open`. **Direction (b) shipped and this line failed to say so for nine days.** `#315` (`ec8941e`, 2026-08-05) added `core/claim_arithmetic.py` — deterministic evaluation of sums, counts, averages, comparisons and multiples over a `key=value` excerpt, `SILENT` on shapes it does not recognise — and wired it as the third content gate in `verify` (`core/verifier_core.py:241`). `ClaimReason` carries `code`/`expected`/`actual`/`explanation`/`computed_from`, and a refuted claim becomes a `claim_refuted` `ReplanTrigger` whose reason is copied into `<replan_context>` verbatim. Measured on the 40-task bench: verdict 26 → 29, reason 13 → 17. Four of this entry's five `xfail(strict=True)` markers went XPASS and were removed in that same commit.
  - **The stale-status incident, recorded because it is the lesson.** `#315` edited this entry (+8/−2) to add two bullets about strict-xfail methodology and left the Status line reading `open` … *"no production code changed and no store touched"* … *"five are `xfail(strict=True)`"* — while the same commit shipped a new module and removed four of those five markers. The registry was therefore already wrong about its most-worked entry on 2026-08-06, the day it was deleted. A generated tally cannot catch this: `scripts/registry_tally.py` derives the counts from the Status lines, so a Status line that lies is copied faithfully into the total.
  - **Open half — direction (a), the entailment check.** `_find_semantic_support` (NLI) and `_find_structured_support` remain reachable **only** from the branch where nothing matched (`core/verifier_core.py`), so for exactly the claims that cited a real source the evaluation never runs. Pinned by the one surviving marker, `tests/test_verifier_derived_claims.py::test_the_semantic_check_is_consulted_for_a_resolved_citation` (`xfail(strict=True)`) — confirmed still xfailing on the full suite of 2026-08-14 (7598 passed, 5 xfailed). The three unmarked tests stay as closure criteria 2 and 3: a true derivation and a literal quotation must keep scoring `verified`, so a fix cannot buy correctness by refusing to judge.
  - **FIELD CASE, live traffic 2026-08-14 — the first outside the bench.** Asked
    «Какой у тебя номер процесса и рабочий каталог в этом прогоне?», the planner
    ran `whoami` and `hostname`. Both returned `andre`. The answer stated
    `pid 8116` and `C:\Users\andre\Projects\agent` — both CORRECT, both taken
    from the `<runtime_self>` block. Verdict: **3 of 3 verified, confidence
    high**, sources `['shell_exec:whoami', 'shell_exec:hostname']`. Neither fact
    occurs anywhere in the shell output; the citations resolved on the SOURCE
    LABEL alone. Worse in kind than the bench case: the agent was right on the
    merits and confirmed for the wrong reason, so the verdict carries no
    information — had the block lied, `3 of 3 verified` would have been
    identical. It also shows the blast radius reaching a NEW consumer: the
    output contract began permitting `[runtime:<field>]` on 2026-08-14 and the
    model does not use it, because any resolving label already buys `verified`
    and a shell call is the cheaper label to reach for.
  - **FIELD CASE 2, live traffic 2026-08-15 — the SAME defect seen from the other side.** Within one hour the verifier scored both of these:

    | claim | true? | citable line? | score |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | «`_run_synthesizer_ladder` передаёт модели прошлые неудачи» | **no** | yes — the quoted strings exist in the file | `3 of 3 verified`, confidence **0.836** |
    | «механизма, который заставил бы меня прочитать `knowledge/corrections.md`, не существует» | **yes**, independently confirmed | no — an absence has no line | `0 of 5 verified`, confidence **0.028** |

    The rewarded answer was false; the punished answer was true. Both verdicts follow the same rule, so this is not a second defect: it is the shape of the first one. The entry's title says *never that the derived claim follows from it*, and this pair shows the cost is symmetric — cited falsehood is rewarded AND proven negative truth is penalised.
  - **What a negative claim needs instead (operator, 2026-08-15).** The question the verifier asks is «can the fragment be attached?»; the question it owes is «is the observation sufficient for the claim to be true in the область it asserts?». For a positive claim a file line can be that observation. For «there is no mechanism» it cannot — absence is not quoted, it is proven by covering the search space: **a defined search scope + a known source set + coverage complete enough for that scope + no matching producer, consumer or reference inside it.** That form is a fourth fork option, distinct from (a)/(b)/(c) above: none of them can make a negative claim verifiable, because all three still look for something to attach.
- **Fix direction (undecided; recorded as a fork, not a recommendation):** (a) run the existing entailment check on claims whose citation *did* resolve, instead of only on those that did not — cheapest structurally, but it puts an LLM call on the hot path for every cited claim and makes the verifier's verdict depend on a model, which is the seam MIR-046 was careful about; (b) widen the deterministic structured-fact path (`core/structured_facts.py`) so counts and arithmetic over tool output are checkable without a model — sound where it applies, silent where it does not; (c) introduce a distinct verdict for *derived* claims, so they are neither `verified` nor discarded, and let the consumers of `verified_chunks` decide — honest, and it does not manufacture a judgement the system cannot make. **Do not choose (a) without first deciding what a verifier that can be wrong means for the counts downstream of it.**
- **How wide (b) actually is, checked 2026-08-05.** The fork calls it "sound where it applies, silent where it does not", which understates it for THIS defect class: three of the four false claims in the table — `sum to 99`, `defines 7 keys`, `gamma is smaller than alpha` — are a count, a sum and a comparison over `alpha=1 beta=2 gamma=3`, all decidable mechanically from the excerpt with no model at all. So (b) is not a narrow band here; it covers most of what was measured, and it makes the verifier COMPUTE instead of matching characters. What it cannot reach is the open-ended case — an inference that is not arithmetic — which is exactly and only what (a) buys.
- **Neither (b) nor (c) makes the agent better at judging (operator, 2026-08-05).** (c) relabels: the system stops calling `verified` what it never checked, which repairs five downstream consumers but adds no judgement. (b) computes, which removes a class of error without adding understanding. (a) is the only direction that grants a new ability — does this claim follow from this excerpt — and the price is a model on the hot path and a verifier that can itself be wrong. Recorded so the fork is not read as three flavours of the same thing.
- **Closure criterion (measurable, all required):** 1. a false derived claim with a resolving citation is not `verified`; 2. a true derived claim is not thereby demoted to unverified — the fix must not buy correctness by refusing to judge; 3. a literal quotation stays `verified` on the same path it uses today; 4. the containment test's blind spot is closed or explicitly documented as out of scope, so an unrelated `port=12` can no longer certify an average; 5. whatever the verdict becomes, every consumer of `verified_chunks` (MIR-002 quality score, `outcome`, `decide_usage_eligibility`, procedural credit, confidence gate) is re-examined against it rather than left to inherit a changed meaning.
- **Fail-before tests — WRITTEN 2026-08-05** (`tests/test_verifier_derived_claims.py`, 8 tests): 5 `xfail(strict=True)` and 3 ordinary. Re-measured against current code first — the July table reproduces row for row after the verifier split into `verifier_core`/`_models`/`_patterns`/`_utils`, including the `port=12` flip and **0 calls** to a model that answers `NO` to everything.
  - `strict=True` rather than a characterization test: a test asserting today's wrong answer stays green forever and teaches the next reader that it is intended. Strict xfail goes RED the day the behaviour changes, so the marker cannot outlive the defect. Proven, not assumed: an `xfail(strict=True)` whose body passes is reported `FAILED [XPASS(strict)]`. This is the repository's first `xfail`; the idiom is introduced deliberately for a registered, open, operator-owned fork.
  - The 3 unmarked tests are closure criteria 2 and 3 — a true derivation and a literal quotation must keep scoring `verified`, so a fix cannot buy correctness by refusing to judge.
- **Blast radius, measured on the live store 2026-08-05** (`data/episodic_memory.jsonl`, 94 episodes): **271** `verified` verdicts; **41** episodes rest on at least one; **16** of those were admitted as reusable experience (`usage_eligible`). 18 chunks carry both a citation and a number or comparison — an UPPER BOUND on derived claims, not a count of them: episodes store `full_answer` but not the evidence chain, so containment cannot be re-checked after the fact. The live false-positive RATE is therefore not measurable from stored data; the synthetic table stands as the evidence, and this measures only what rides on the verdict.
- **Not measurable from logs (recorded so nobody re-tries it):** `logs/*.jsonl` carry the `verification` event with `verdicts` but no claim text — `respond` logs `chars`, not the answer. Pairing verdicts to claims from logs alone is not possible; the episodic store is the only place the answer survives.

### MIR-061 — one malformed memory record silently drops every record after it from the provenance chain
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-042 (**same two lines of code, different root** — the trust-filter face), MIR-052 (same anti-pattern family: a broad `except` whose comment describes behaviour the code does not have), MIR-060 (a truncated chain is one way a resolvable citation stops resolving). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-21); **re-registered 2026-07-22** — first landed with its fix in #114, both reverted by #115, now restored on its own so the fix travels without the reverted bundle.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop.py` persistent-record chain injection. The `try` wraps the **entire `for` loop**; the handler is a bare `pass`.
- **Symptom:** a single record that raises while being converted to evidence aborts the whole injection loop, so **every record after it is silently omitted from the provenance chain**. The verifier then evaluates the answer against an incomplete chain.
- **The code's own comment states the opposite behaviour.** It reads *"A malformed record stays out of the chain but the loop completes normally"* — singular, and describing a per-record skip. The loop does not complete; it is abandoned.
- **Measured (2026-07-21, in-process, read-only), using the exact control flow of both sites** with five records of which the **second** is malformed: the persistent-record site (`try` outside the loop) delivered **1 of 5**; the adjacent working-artifact site (`try` inside the loop) delivered **4 of 5**. Two sites doing the same job, on adjacent lines, with opposite granularity — and the one that is wrong is the one whose comment claims to be right.
- **Root cause:** exception scope. Not the presence of the guard (chain assembly genuinely must not abort a run) and not the trust model (that is MIR-042) — simply a `try` placed around the loop instead of around the iteration.
- **Consequence:** the verifier's verdict is computed against whatever reached the chain. Citations that *should* resolve then come back `cited_but_unmatched`, so the answer is marked unverified — or its disclaimer fires — for a reason that has nothing to do with the answer or its sources. Because the handler is a bare `pass`, nothing records that the chain was truncated; the resulting verdict looks like an ordinary evidence shortfall.
- **Why this is NOT part of MIR-042 (no-merge basis).** Same two lines, orthogonal roots. MIR-042 asks *which records may enter* the chain (trust filter on `Evidence.origin`); this asks *whether the records that may enter actually arrive* (fixed by moving the `try` inside the loop). Distinct blast radius, distinct closure criteria, distinct urgency. Folding them would let a data-loss bug ride out on a trust-model fix.
- **History (status superseded 2026-08-23 — see below):** code fixed 2026-07-22. Fixed by moving the `try` inside the loop and logging `memory_evidence_skipped` instead of passing silently; live-agent runtime with a real model not exercised.
- **Resolution evidence:** `tests/test_evidence_integration.py::TestMalformedMemoryRecordDoesNotTruncateChain` — `test_one_bad_record_only_skips_itself` (N records, one malformed → N−1 reach the chain, only the bad one dropped) and `test_skipped_record_is_reported` (the drop is logged, not swallowed). **Fail-before verified:** both fail on the pre-fix `try`-around-the-loop code; both pass after. A guard is added that the two injection sites keep the same granularity so they cannot drift apart again.
- **History note:** the identical fix first shipped in PR #114 (commit `667dd6b`) and was reverted with the rest of #114 by PR #115 for reasons unrelated to this defect; this entry and PR restore it in isolation.

- **RUNTIME-VERIFIED 2026-08-23 through the real method**, which is what this status was waiting for. `_fold_evidence_chain` driven directly with five records: second malformed → **4 of 5** delivered and exactly one `memory_evidence_skipped` logged; first malformed → 4 of 5, one event; three malformed → 2 of 5, three events. Control run with no malformed record delivered 5 of 5, so the probe is proved to see the loop (the first attempt returned 0 of 5 because the entry guard needs BOTH `persistent_block` and `persistent_store`, and measured nothing at all — a false zero caught before it became a verdict).
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-23). Per-record granularity confirmed in operation, and the loss is named rather than silent — the original defect's worst half.

### MIR-051 — stored procedure `confidence` violates the Beta-smoothing invariant (legacy data)
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-048 (same field, different root — this is data, that is a missing code path), MIR-003. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (D-1 measurement, 2026-07-20).
- **Files/functions:** data defect in `data/procedural_memory.jsonl`. The code is **correct**: both writers — `procedure_from_episode` (`core/smart_memory.py:735`, births at `0.5`) and `ProcedureRecord.with_episode` (`:261`) — go through `_smoothed_confidence`. No writer produces `1.0`.
- **Symptom:** **45 of 65 procedures (69%) carry `confidence=1.0`**, unreachable from `(s+1)/(s+2)` for any counter pair. Breakdown: `success=1 → stored 1.0` (39 records, Beta expects 0.667), `success=2 → 1.0` (5, expects 0.75), `success=3 → 1.0` (1, expects 0.8). The remaining 20 match the formula exactly.
- **Root cause:** smoothing was introduced on **2026-07-11** — the split is sharp: every record last updated before that date is unsmoothed, every record after it is correct. Existing rows were never migrated.
- **Why this is NOT part of MIR-048:** distinct cause (unmigrated data vs. an unreachable code path), distinct blast radius (69% of stored procedures vs. all future updates), distinct fix (a one-off recomputation vs. new feedback plumbing), distinct completion criteria, and a migration risk that MIR-048 does not carry. Folding it in would hide a data-integrity defect inside a logic defect.
- **Impact:** those 45 procedures are `active` and injected into planning as proven workflows at maximum confidence — a certainty no measurement ever earned. This directly contradicts `docs/ROADMAP.md` Track B ("procedure confidence is Beta(1,1)-smoothed — a single success is **not** treated as certainty"): on live data, 39 single-success procedures are at exactly 1.0.
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20)** ⬆ from `open` — migrator implemented **and** the live store migrated, verified against a pre-migration backup.
- **Resolution:** `ProceduralMemoryStore.recompute_legacy_confidence(dry_run=…, limit=…)` — an explicit pass, deliberately not hidden inside read or upsert. It works on raw payloads rather than `ProcedureRecord`, because `from_dict` clamps negative counters to 0 and would silently launder a corrupt row into a valid-looking one; unknown fields also survive untouched. Writes go through the store's own writer, so the integrity envelope is recomputed normally. Records already consistent are not rewritten at all, leaving their bytes and checksums untouched. `status` is recomputed alongside `confidence` because it *is* `confidence >= 0.6` — leaving it stale would swap one broken invariant for another.
- **Live migration result (backup taken first):** 65 scanned → 45 corrected, 20 already consistent, 0 invalid. Run as a bounded batch (`limit=10`, resumable — consistent count went 20 → 30) then completed. Verifying dry-run afterwards: `corrected=0, already_consistent=65`. Confidence distribution `{1.0: 45, 0.667: 14, 0.75: 2, 0.833: 2, 0.933: 1, 0.8: 1}` → `{0.667: 53, 0.75: 7, 0.833: 2, 0.8: 2, 0.933: 1}` — **no record holds 1.0 any more**, and the shape matches the success counts exactly (53 single-success procedures → 0.667).
- **Before/after diff vs backup:** same 65 ids; **0 invariant violations**; **0 changes to protected fields** (`success_count`, `failure_count`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `workflow_key`, `name`, `steps`, `trigger_tags`, `source_episode_ids`, `id`); exactly 45 `confidence` changes; 0 `status` changes — every recomputed value stayed ≥ 0.6, so no procedure changed lifecycle state as a side effect of the migration.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_legacy_confidence_migration.py` — 13 passing, 12 written fail-before. Fixtures are seeded **through the store**, so every case exercises the real integrity-envelope row shape rather than a convenient in-memory object. Covers the four correction scenarios, already-consistent byte-identity, idempotence, counters/freshness/identity/content preservation, invalid counters skipped rather than guessed, dry-run writing nothing, and a bounded resumable pass.
- **Not addressed here (by design):** the missing failure history itself — no run reconstructs failures that were never recorded. That is MIR-048, and it is sequenced behind MIR-049.
- **Invariant to restore:** `stored_confidence == _smoothed_confidence(success_count, failure_count)` for every procedure whose counters are valid.
- **Migration requirements (all mandatory):** deterministic; idempotent (a second run is a no-op); available as `dry_run` first; bounded in volume or safely resumable; emits a before/after report; **does not alter `success_count` / `failure_count`**; **does not touch `updated_at`, `last_used_at` or any other freshness field** — a migration must not make stale procedures look recently used and thereby extend their life past hygiene. Recompute every field that is a pure derivative of the same counters (e.g. `status`, which is `confidence >= 0.6`), but **do not reconstruct missing failure history** — that is MIR-048's job, not a migration's.
- _(SUPERSEDED — see this entry's Status/Resolution; kept as the record of what was considered)_ **Missing tests:** invariant holds for all records post-migration; dry-run changes nothing; second run is a no-op; freshness fields byte-identical before/after; counters unchanged.

### MIR-041 — episodic fast-path replays verbatim, skips verification, and re-banks as `verified_chunks=1`
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-002 (the score that makes ungrounded answers eligible), MIR-016 (verify gate observational), MIR-024 (Jaccard similarity), MIR-005. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (Stage-3.3 deep dive).
- **Sources:** this pass (full read + reproduction of `episodic_fast_path`).
- **First documented:** here, against `main` @ `f317c4c`.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_gates.py:205-281` `_episodic_fast_path`; `_last_best_similar_episode`/`_score` set in `core/loop_methods2.py` via `smart_memory.find_most_similar` (Jaccard). <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** when a past episode matches the current question at `similarity >= 0.85` with `answer_quality_score >= 0.70` and `not tools_used`, the loop returns the stored answer verbatim (`return _fp_ep.full_answer`), **skipping planner/act/synthesize/verify entirely**, and records a **new** episode with `verified_chunks=1` (source `memory:<id>`).
- **Root cause (two independent faults):**
  1. **Gate trusts `answer_quality_score`, not verification.** It never consults the original episode's verifier verdict; combined with MIR-002 (`quality=1.0` for empty chain), an ungrounded — even *wrong* — answer is replay-eligible.
  2. **Circular re-banking.** The replay is written as `verified_chunks=1` (i.e. "verified against memory"). That new episode is itself `quality=1.0, outcome=success, tools_used=()` → **fast-path-eligible again**. Reproduced: each replay mints another "verified success," so a wrong ungrounded answer's evidence footprint grows with every near-duplicate ask — a self-reinforcing loop laundering "matches memory" into "verified."
- **Production path:** `AgentLoop.run` → after `_retrieve_experience_memory` sets the best-similar episode → the fast-path block → early `return` (bypasses the whole cognitive cycle).
- **Existing tests:** none targeting the safety of the fast-path (there may be tests asserting it *fires*; none assert it refuses ungrounded/unverified replays).
- _(SUPERSEDED — see this entry's Status/Resolution; kept as the record of what was considered)_ **Missing tests:** the fast-path does NOT fire for an episode that was never verified; a replay is not recorded as `verified`; a replayed ungrounded answer cannot self-reinforce.
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20, commit `c07b742`)** ⬆ from `open`.
- **Resolution:** the replay now banks `verified_chunks=0, unverified_chunks=1` (was `1, 0`). `unverified=1` rather than `0/0` deliberately — an empty chain scored quality 1.0 (MIR-002), which would award the replay top marks precisely for carrying no evidence. The source episode stays named in `source_labels`, so a copy remains traceable to its origin. Two further guards landed alongside: `usage_eligible` (2c, commit `1379193`) fail-closes retrieval so an unclassified episode is not replay-eligible at all, and `AgentLoop._quality_allows_replay` (MIR-002 fix) refuses an unmeasured score. Tests: `tests/test_fast_path_verification_provenance.py` — 7 passing, six were fail-before.
- **Evidence:** full block read at `loop.py:744-796`; reproduced that an ungrounded (and even wrong) answer satisfies the gate and that the re-banked replay is fast-path-eligible again.
- _(SUPERSEDED — see this entry's Status/Resolution; kept as the record of what was considered)_ **Alternatives (decision needed — see the MIR-002 fork):** (a) gate the fast-path on real evidence / a stored verifier verdict rather than `quality>=0.70`; (b) stop re-banking replays as `verified_chunks=1` (record as `self_declared`/non-learning); (c) fix MIR-002's score so ungrounded answers fall below 0.70. Best fix combines "don't replay the unverified" + "don't launder the replay as verified."

### Fix-design contract (MIR-002 + MIR-041) — operator-approved target semantics

> **Superseded as the canonical source:** the single canonical lifecycle contract is now
> `docs/audit/MEMORY_LIFECYCLE_CONTRACT.md` (M1). This section remains as the historical
> defect-level record; where the two differ, the M1 contract wins.

> Chosen approach: **Variant 2 + 3** (gate the fast-path on genuine verification **and** never
> record a replay as verified). **Variant 1 (arbitrarily lowering `quality` to 0.3) is rejected
> as the primary fix** — a quality *score* and a *proof-of-verification provenance* are different
> concepts and must not be conflated. Design MIR-002 and MIR-041 fixes **together** (same
> execution path) but keep them as **separate defects/commits**. Do NOT implement yet — finish
> the read-only memory trace first, then propose the provenance scheme + fail-before tests.

1. **Fast-path is allowed ONLY for an episode carrying explicit proof that the original answer
   actually passed Verify or rested on valid evidence.**
2. **Verification must NOT be inferred indirectly from** `answer_quality_score`, `outcome=="success"`,
   absence of `tools_used`, or `verified_chunks` **when that field was synthetically set by the
   fast-path itself.**
3. **A memory replay must be stored with its own provenance** (`source=episodic_fast_path` /
   `memory_replay`), and **must not receive `verified_chunks=1`** nor count as a new verification.
4. **A replay must not raise trust in the source episode** and must not create a self-sustaining
   verified-success chain.
5. **MIR-002 and MIR-041 stay separate defects**, fixed on the same path but not merged.
6. **No arbitrary `quality→0.3`** as the main fix; quality score ≠ verification provenance.

**Provenance scheme — operator-corrected, TWO INDEPENDENT AXES (do not merge origin and verification into one field):**

- **Axis 1 — `verification_status`**: `verified` | `partially_verified` | `unverified` | `verifier_error` | `not_run`.
  Set from the **actual verifier outcome only**. MUST NOT be inferred from `answer_quality_score`, `outcome`,
  `tools_used`, `source_labels`, or old-record `verified_chunks`.
- **Axis 2 — `response_origin`**: `generated` | `memory_replay` | `persistent_memory` | (any existing origin already in the system).

- **Memory replay must be stored as:** `response_origin = memory_replay`; `verification_status = not_run`;
  keep `source_episode_id` (or equivalent link to the source episode); `verified_chunks = 0`; the replay
  creates **no new evidence**; and the replay episode **must not** become a candidate for the next fast-path.
- **Fast-path requires:** source episode `verification_status == verified` **AND** the record is **not** a replay
  **AND** all existing safe constraints (no file hint, non-`:`, not local-critique, `full_answer` present,
  `not tools_used`) still hold.
- **Old episodes without `verification_status` → treat as `not_run`** and **disallow fast-path**. **No automatic
  migration by guessing** from old fields.

**MIR-002 is NOT fully fixed by this.** This closes only its **fast-path manifestation** (an ungrounded/`verifier_error`
`quality=1.0` episode will no longer be replay-eligible, because the gate now keys on `verification_status==verified`,
not the score). The influence of `quality=1.0` on **hygiene pruning, re-ask detection, and the other readers** remains
a **separate open part of MIR-002**.

**Post-trace deliverables (before any code):** [reader list — DONE, in AUDIT_PROGRESS] · **separate** fail-before tests
for MIR-002 and MIR-041 (approved next step) · then the minimal file set for the fix.

### MIR-042 — memory records enter the provenance chain with no trust-class filter (semantic-side laundering seam)
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-041 (episodic analogue), MIR-009 (missing trust/temporal model), MIR-002. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (Stage-3.3 whole-subsystem trace).
- **Sources:** this pass.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_evidence_chain.py:168-176` (persistent records → `chain.add(evidence_from_memory_record(...))` for every retrieved record) and `:209` (working-memory artifacts → `chain.add(make_evidence(kind="memory", ...))`); `core/evidence.py:603` `evidence_from_memory_record`. Both chain sites lived at `core/loop.py` `:1305-1318` / `:1331-1347` until the loop split, #312, 2026-08-04.
- **Symptom / mechanism:** every retrieved persistent/working memory record is injected into the verifier's provenance chain **uniformly** — the record's `source` (user-explicit vs agent-auto) is read (`getattr(rec,"source",None)`) but **not used to gate or trust-tag** the evidence. A `[memory:<id>]` citation therefore resolves to a *matched* evidence. So a stored fact — including one written by a prior, possibly-unverified answer — can back a later answer as if it were checked evidence.
- **Why it matters (ties to the operator's semantics rule #2):** this is the semantic-memory analogue of MIR-041 — "cited a memory record" is being treated as support without regard to whether the record's content was ever verified. A user-approved `:remember` fact is legitimately trusted; an agent-auto write is not, yet both enter the chain identically.
- **Status:** `partially_fixed` (2026-07-20) ⬆ from `open` — **confirmed** (verifier question resolved at M0).
- **Update (2026-07-20):** the *counting* face is closed by MIR-046 (`5105e03`): a resolved `[memory:]` citation to an `agent-auto` record no longer contributes `verified`. The *injection* face remains open — `loop.py:1305/:1331` still adds every retrieved record to the chain with no trust filter. The consequence is now bounded (presence in the chain no longer buys a verified verdict) but the chain itself is still trust-blind, which matters for any future consumer that reads it directly.
- **Evidence (M0):** `core/verifier_core.py:87-116` — a citation that `match_citation`s **any** chain evidence, including `kind="memory"`, is marked `[verified:…]` and increments `verified_chunks` (line 114-116). Persistent records + working artifacts are injected into the chain uniformly with **no trust-class filter** (`loop.py:1305-1347`). So a `[memory:<id>]` citation to a stored record counts as *verified* — it verifies the citation *resolves to a real record*, not that the record's *content* was checked. **Severity raised:** `knowledge_auto_write` defaults to **True** in production (`app/bootstrap.py:250`), so agent-generated claims are auto-written to persistent memory and can later be cited as "verified." Partial guards: write policy, echo antibody, `AGENT_FREEZE_AUTO_MEMORY`.
- **Missing tests:** an agent-auto memory record cited later does not count as independent verification; user-approved vs agent-auto records are trust-distinguished when entering the chain.
- **Fix belongs to the lifecycle reconstruction** (trust class on records), not a standalone patch — shares the root "memory presence ≠ verification" with MIR-041.
- **Write-side nuance (group 3.4):** the semantic **write** path is better guarded than M0 first stated — `KnowledgeWritePolicy.decide` rejects `unverified`/`conflicted`/low-confidence/untrusted/hype/secret claims, so raw unverified answers are *not* auto-written. The live residual is therefore concentrated in (a) the trust-blind **verdict** (MIR-046) and (b) **post-write conflict not enforced** (MIR-047), plus the mismatch between "verified by source corroboration" (`knowledge_pipeline.py:261`) and "verified by the answer verifier."

### MIR-043 — the autonomous / daemon path is memory-blind (episodic/procedural/consolidation absent)
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (M0). **Sources:** this pass.
- **Files/functions:** `app/bootstrap.py:143-158` — episodic/procedural/consolidation stores are created only under `if with_memory:`; the autonomous path calls `build_agent(with_memory=False)` (`main.py:2017`, `agent_tick.py:806`).
- **Symptom:** the unattended agent (daemon, campaigns, scheduled tasks) records **no episodes, builds no procedures, consolidates nothing, has no working memory** — only persistent/user-profile/assumptions/source-registry. All episodic/procedural learning, fast-path, and re-ask exist **only** in the interactive REPL/`--ask` path.
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20, commits `6c1977d` → `4020cd6`)** ⬆ from `open`.
- **Resolution (five commits, each with its own fail-before tests):** `6c1977d` separated the four conflated memory permissions — `with_experience` (hold the stores) now defaults to `with_memory` but is independent of it, and the store block moved **out** of the `with_persistent` branch entirely, so experience memory is no longer reachable only through persistent memory. `4a6ba7e`+`f20536b` replaced the single write boolean with a per-sink allowlist under strict priority (audit and dry-run absolute; unknown or unnamed sink denied). `6f4e7f3` added run identity (`run_id` per attempt, `task_id` per logical task, via ContextVar) and bounded idempotency (`EpisodicMemoryStore.save_once`). `1379193` added three-state `usage_eligible`. `4020cd6` gave the unattended profile `durable_writes={"episode"}` plus honest abort outcomes.
- **Resolution evidence:** `tests/test_memory_core_wiring.py` is the first test in the repo to exercise `build_agent` at all — the existing suites construct `AgentLoop` by hand with every store supplied, which is precisely why this gap survived. **Residual, tracked separately:** the queue's `task_id` still does not reach `agent.run` (`RuntimeTask` is reduced to an `AutonomousTask` carrying only kind+description before the call — comment left at the call site); episodes are written `usage_eligible=False`, so the loop closes mechanically but stays inert until the eligibility policy admits them.
- _(SUPERSEDED — see this entry's Status/Resolution; kept as the record of what was considered)_ **Missing tests:** an autonomous tick records an episode / distils a procedure (or an explicit, tested decision that it must not).

### MIR-044 — consolidation is a dead sink (written every cycle, read only by CLI status)
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (M0).
- **Files/functions:** `consolidate_memory` (`core/smart_memory.py:1627`, called from `core/loop_memory_write.py:389`) saves a report every cycle; read **only** by `smart_memory_summary` (`core/loop_memory_read.py:415`, CLI `:smart-memory`). Both call sites lived in `core/loop_methods2.py` (`:382`, `:416`) until the loop split, #312, 2026-08-04 <!-- historical-ref -->. It does not mutate procedures and nothing consumes it for retrieval/planning/pruning.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-22 evening — the 2026-07-19 retirement ruling executed; see above).
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-22, unchanged, and now with the bill attached.** Write side: `core/loop_memory_write.py:411` calls `consolidation_store.save(report)` every cycle. Read side: exactly one, `core/loop_memory_read.py:446` inside `smart_memory_summary()`, whose only production caller is `cli/commands_memory.py:206` — a display command. So the sink is still dead, and it is dead in the specific way MIR-131 names: the write is on the autonomous path and the read is behind a keyboard.
- **The cost, measured.** `data/memory_consolidation.jsonl` holds **255 reports in 738 KB** — about 2.9 KB per row, because each report stores full lists (`active_procedure_ids`, `candidate_procedure_ids`, `linked_episode_ids`, `obsolete_procedure_ids`, …) rather than counts. It is the third-largest state file, written every cycle, for a summary nobody can request during an unattended run. Feeds MIR-125 directly.
- **EXECUTED 2026-08-22 evening, a month and three days after the ruling.** The per-cycle branch is deleted from `core/loop_memory_write.py` (with it the `skip_consolidation` parameter and the cheap-path «skipped» twin — the distinction died with the branch it described); `:memory-consolidate` computes and prints without saving; `smart_memory_summary` derives its consolidation section on demand from the stores it already loads; `app/bootstrap.py` constructs no store; the 738 KB history is archived as `data/memory_consolidation.archive.jsonl`. The `consolidation` durable-sink token stays in the vocabulary with a retirement note so an old config naming it validates rather than failing as a phantom typo. Pinned structurally by `tests/test_the_dead_sink_is_retired.py` (an AST sweep proves no production site saves a report), and six tests that pinned the OLD semantics were repointed to pin the ruling instead — including the one whose assertion message read «the report is persisted, not just printed», which was the dead sink pinning itself.
- **This entry was not awaiting a DECISION — it was awaiting EXECUTION of one.** The ruling below was made 2026-07-19 and prescribes retirement: stop persisting, compute the CLI tally on demand, archive history. A month later the reports are still being written. Recorded plainly because "pending contract approval" and "deferred" read the same in a list and are not the same thing.
- **Operator ruling (2026-07-19):** repurposing ("Option A") **rejected** — re-reading `consolidate_memory` (`smart_memory.py:607-609`) showed the report is a pure tally of statuses procedures already hold; there is nothing in it to "apply." The v2 contract prescribes **retirement** (`MEMORY_LIFECYCLE_CONTRACT.md` §8, phase P6a): stop persisting reports, compute the CLI tally on demand, archive history. Pending contract approval.

- **CLOSURE AUDITED 2026-08-22 — held.** Rule: «compute it when asked degrades at scale». Measured 0.01 ms at 100 episodes to 0.82 ms at 50 000 (350× the live 142), linear — but the stronger argument is that `smart_memory_summary` already loaded both stores for its counts, so the tally is pure computation over lists already in memory: the retirement added ZERO reads. The persisted report was never a cache of anything expensive. **Residue recorded, not patched:** `loop_init` still accepts `consolidation_store` and `loop_memory_write` still names it in an all-None guard, so a reader can take the sink for something switchable-back-on. Nothing saves to it (AST sweep pins that), so the parameter is inert rather than dormant — a map defect, and the audit does not grant itself scope to rewrite constructors.
### MIR-045 — memory hygiene is manual-only (no automatic pruning; unbounded growth)
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** the earlier auto-memory pollution incident. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (M0).
- **Files/functions:** `prune_episodic` (`core/loop_hygiene.py:106`), `archive_low_value_memory` (`core/memory_hygiene.py:478`), `expire_persistent`/`dedupe_persistent`/`summarise_persistent` (`core/loop_hygiene.py:91`, `:96`, `:123`) are invoked only by the `:hygiene` CLI command (`cli/commands_memory.py:81`, `:129`) — never automatically and never on the autonomous path. All five lived at `core/loop_methods.py:339-471` until the loop split, #312, 2026-08-04 <!-- historical-ref -->.
- **Status:** `open` (integration gap) — without an operator running `:hygiene`, episodic/persistent memory grows unbounded; the autonomous path can never prune.
- **CONFIRMED 2026-08-22 BY AN INDEPENDENT ROUTE, and it has now cost something measurable.** This was not re-read to check it; it was re-derived while investigating why self-build kept banking the same episode (`docs/audit/SELF_BUILD_FAILURE_ANALYSIS.md`). `collapse_duplicate_episodes` — the MIR-090 fix, built precisely to collapse repeated gate records — has exactly one production caller, `core/memory_hygiene_commands.py:240`, the `:memory hygiene` sweep. During the unattended run of 2026-08-16 nobody typed a command, so it never ran, and **43 byte-identical `approval_wait` episodes accumulated as one duplicate group**. That is this integration gap with a number attached: manual-only hygiene means the autonomous path is exactly the path that cannot clean up after itself, and it is the path that generates the most repeats. *(Narrowed 2026-08-22 evening: duplicate collapse — the specific mechanism this paragraph is about — now runs from the tick; see MIR-131. The judgement-laden pruners stay manual, and this entry's wider growth concern stands.)*

- **FIELD-CHECKED AND MEASURED 2026-08-23.** External claim: every evaluated agent system accumulates state monotonically by default and needs an explicit forgetting policy, because unbounded growth degrades *recall precision* — noisy retrieval — before it ever costs disk (arXiv 2606.06448 «Agent Memory: Characterization and System Implications of Stateful Long-Horizon Workloads»; arXiv 2605.26302 on agent lifespan). Production accuracy is reported to depend on pruning discipline rather than storage volume.
- **The entry's first half is now STALE and is corrected here.** It reads «never automatically and never on the autonomous path». Since then `agent_tick.py` runs `run_maintenance_pass` on every tick — placed after the work summary so a maintenance fault cannot change the tick's verdict, gated by `AGENT_AUTO_HYGIENE` whose default is **`shadow`**: count and report, remove nothing. The stated ground for shadow is that the thresholds «have only ever been exercised against synthetic data, and the pass deletes».
- **That ground is now answered with real data.** The pass was run in dry-run against a COPY of the live store: persistent memory 125 records → expired **0**, deduped **0**, archived **0**; episodic 142 → pruned **0**. Every deleting threshold touches nothing at the current volume, so switching `AGENT_AUTO_HYGIENE=on` today would delete no memory at all. The fear and the benefit are both currently zero.
- **What the pass WOULD act on is a different store.** `assumptions_archive_compact`: 681 rows scanned, **669 duplicates**, 12 kept. Twelve unique texts repeated for three weeks — «The user expects a Russian-language response» ×254, «The target file is UTF-8 encoded» ×136. Growth ≈31 rows/day, so an unattended week adds ≈217.
- **And that duplication is NOT a precision defect**, which is the half the field claim would have predicted wrongly here: MIR-027 already ruled the assumption store an ARCHIVE, not an active input, and removed the cross-turn auto-restore. Nothing reads these rows into a decision, so the cost is disk only.
- **Status:** `open`, restated. The integration gap named in 2026-07 is closed (the pass is on the autonomous path); what remains open is a **policy** decision — whether `AGENT_AUTO_HYGIENE` should be `on` for the unattended week — and that is an operator's call because the pass deletes and deletion is irreversible (§9). The measurement above is the evidence that decision was missing.

### MIR-046 — the verifier verdict is trust-blind (any resolved citation counts `verified`)
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-042 (chain-injection side) — kept **separate** per operator instruction; MIR-046 is the *verdict* side. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (group 3.4, focused test).
- **Files/functions:** `core/verifier_core.py:87-116` — `if any_matched: verdict="verified"; verified += 1`. The count consults neither the evidence's `kind`, `obtained_via`, `confidence`, nor the record's trust/provenance.
- **Symptom (focused fail-before test `tests/test_verifier_memory_trust.py`):** a `[memory:<id>]` citation to an **agent-auto** record → `verified_chunks=1`; a **user-approved** and an **agent-auto** record cited in one answer both get `['verified','verified']` — identical. Verified iff the citation *resolves to a record in the chain*, never because the content was checked (proved by the same wrong content scoring `verified` in-chain, `cited_but_unmatched` out-of-chain). A working-memory artifact counts verified via the identical path.
- **Trust distinction today:** **none in the verdict.** Trust signals exist but are scattered and unused by the count — evidence `kind`/`confidence` (`DEFAULT_CONFIDENCE`), record `source` (`user-explicit`/`agent-auto`), source `trust_level`, claim `status`, `QUARANTINE_TAGS`. M1 must give the verifier one trust vocabulary.
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20, commit `5105e03`)** ⬆ from `open`.
- **Resolution:** `Evidence` gained a structured `origin` field — the underlying record's provenance. It already existed, but only as prose inside `claim` (`"Memory record X (source=agent-auto)"`), where the verifier could not consult it without parsing English. A memory citation now counts as independent evidence only when `origin == "user-explicit"`; anything else is demoted to topic-only — still shown as supporting context, never counted as proof. Two memory shapes are deliberately kept apart: a **persistent record** (`obtained_via="memory"`) is judged on origin, while a **working-memory artifact** (`obtained_via="working_memory"`) stays `verified` because it is a cached tool observation — real output that was actually fetched. Demoting the latter would discard genuine evidence, which is why the distinction is drawn on `obtained_via`, not on `kind`.
- **Resolution evidence:** `tests/test_verifier_memory_trust.py` — 4 passing, two were fail-before. One baseline test was reseeded (`agent-auto` → `user-explicit`) because agent-auto no longer reaches `verified` at all, which would otherwise mask the property that test demonstrates: the verdict tracks citation *resolution*, not content truth. Its claim is unchanged.

### MIR-047 — conflict_review is advisory-only; a conflicted record stays citable as verified
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-009 (temporal-truth), MIR-042/046. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (group 3.4 ingestion/conflict trace).
- **Files/functions:** `core/conflict_review.py` `ConflictReview.review` (builds a `ConflictReviewReport` of suggestions only); called only by `cli/commands_misc.py:264` (`:conflicts`), result **logged**, not persisted. It does **not** mutate claim status or tag records. Retrieval filters only `QUARANTINE_TAGS` (`core/knowledge_use_policy.py:104`). <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom / path (the operator's "conflicting record later used as evidence" question):** a persistent claim written while `status ∈ {extracted, verified}` (promoted by **source corroboration**, `knowledge_pipeline.py:261` — a *different* notion of "verified" than the answer verifier) can later be contradicted; conflict_review flags it as a **suggestion** but does not tag/quarantine it, so it is **not** filtered on retrieval → it re-enters the chain and is cited as `verified` (MIR-046) until an operator manually acts. Conflict detection is not enforced.
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20)** ⬆ from `open` — for records written from now on; see the legacy limit below.
- **Root cause was NOT where the title suggests.** Live measurement (3105 claims: `extracted` 2528, `unverified` 519, `verified` 34, **`conflicted` 24**) shows detection works and the status IS persisted by the pipeline. The real gap: `memory_tags` returned `["fact", "knowledge", "source-backed", <type>]` — **no link back to the claim**. When a claim later turned conflicted there was no way to know which memory record it produced. Quarantine had no addressee, which is why it stayed advisory.
- **Resolution (three changes, all reusing existing machinery):** (1) `claim_provenance_tag(claim_id)` → records carry `claim:<id>`; a tag rather than a new field, since tags are already persisted and already filtered. (2) `conflicted` joins `QUARANTINE_TAGS`, so `KnowledgeUsePolicy` filters it at every retrieval site with no new code. (3) `quarantine_conflicted_records` tags the linked records, wired into the write path (`_quarantine_conflicted_memory`) where the conflict is detected — the window in which a contradicted claim still reads as ordinary evidence is now as short as the cycle itself.
