- **Marking is by provenance tag only.** A record with no claim link is reported `unlinked` and left alone; matching by content would be the guess MIR-049/050 exist to forbid, and mis-quarantining a correct record is worse than leaving a conflicted one until an operator looks. Idempotent — the tag never accumulates. Resolution stays operator-only: removing the tag restores the record.
- **Legacy limit (deliberate, operator-approved):** records written before this change carry no `claim:` tag, so the 24 already-conflicted claims cannot be linked to their records. They are left as they are. Coverage begins with newly written records.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_conflict_quarantine.py` — 8 passing, all fail-before. Provenance tag present and specific, `conflicted` gating retrieval, an operator restoring by removing the tag, marking only the linked record (unrelated untouched, legacy reported not guessed), idempotence, and no-conflict as a no-op.
- **Original note (honest scope):** the write side is well-guarded — `KnowledgeWritePolicy.decide` (`knowledge_pipeline.py:283`) rejects `status ∈ {unverified, conflicted}`, low confidence, untrusted source, hype, secrets; `MemoryWritePolicy` + echo-antibody (real input, `loop_methods.py:134`) gate the write. The gap is **post-write conflict enforcement**, not the initial write.

### MIR-010 — command failure recorded as step success
- **Aliases:** CORE-06, LPF-010. **Related (broader):** OFM-004 (MIR-031). **Provenance:** previously_documented + independently_reconfirmed.
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md, CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md, OPERATIONAL_FAILURE_MODES.md.
- **Files/functions:** `tools/shell_exec.py` `validate_output` (`:520-539`); `core/loop.py` step gate (`~:2985/:3301`).
- **Symptom:** `where ffmpeg` returns `exit_code=1` but `tool_result.status="success"` and the step is treated as succeeding.
- **Root cause:** `shell_exec` sets `status="success"` whenever the subprocess *ran*; `exit_code` lives in `output` and no post-condition reads it; `validate_output` only warns on timed_out/exit_code *consistency*.
- **Related causes:** external analogue OFM-004 (MIR-031).
- **Production path:** AgentLoop act → `shell_exec` → step success gate → episode outcome.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_shell_exec*` (existing behaviour).
- **Missing tests:** per-command exit-code semantics (`exit!=0` → failure for run-commands; `1` still "not found" for `where`/`grep`/`test`).
- **Status:** **`fixed` (2026-07-20)** ⬆ from `open`. The design decision the entry demanded was taken on measured data.
- **Live confirmation before the fix:** a real cycle ran `grep -c ^ core/loop.py`, got `exit_code=1` with `stderr="FINDSTR: Cannot open loop.py"`, and the log carried `tool_result status=success` beside `verify ok=True`. The command could not open the file and every layer above it said otherwise.
- **Why the blanket rule stayed rejected — measured, not argued:** `grep` found → 0/empty stderr; no match → **1/empty stderr** (a legitimate ANSWER); missing file → 2/non-empty. But `where` not-found → **1 with NON-empty stderr** (`ИНФОРМАЦИЯ: не удается найти файлы`), and `which` not-found → 1 with `which: no zzz in (...)`. So both a blanket `exit!=0→failure` AND a family-blind "stderr means trouble" heuristic would recast an ordinary negative result as an error.
- **Resolution:** `classify_shell_result` in `tools/shell_exec.py` returns **two separate facts** — `execution_status` (success/failure: did the command run correctly) and `answer_result` (positive/negative/not_applicable: what it answered). "grep found nothing" is a successful execution with a negative answer; conflating that with "grep could not open the file" is what produced the defect. Per-family contracts: `grep`/`egrep`/`fgrep` and `findstr` read stderr at exit 1; `rg`, `diff`/`cmp`, `test`/`[`, `where`, `which` key on the exit code alone; anything else takes the conservative unknown contract (0 → success, non-zero → failure); `exit_code is None` (timeout) → failure.
- **Classified on the binary that ACTUALLY RAN.** `_platform_alias` swaps `grep`→`findstr` on Windows, so reading the requested name would check one tool's exit code against another tool's contract. `git` deliberately takes the unknown contract: it is a multiplexer whose semantics differ per subcommand, and guessing a family from `argv[1]` is the inference this design refuses.
- **The three layers stay separate.** `run()` returns the raw `exit_code`/`stdout`/`stderr` untouched plus the two normalised fields; `validate_output` still answers only "does this match the schema", because a failed command produces a perfectly well-formed report OF that failure and rejecting it would conflate malformed output with a correct failure message; a new overridable `Tool.execution_status` hook lifts the verdict into the top-level `ToolResult.status`.
- **`answer_result` is telemetry about one command** and is explicitly NOT a completion signal — it says nothing about task completion, episodic eligibility or procedural feedback (MIR-057 owns those).
- **Tests:** `tests/test_shell_exit_code_semantics.py` — 47 passing. Every row of the contract table, plus an integration pair through `Tool.invoke`: a real failure (`git rev-parse --verify zzznosuchref` → exit 128) that was `status=success`/`ok=True` before and is `execution_status=failure`/`status=error` after, and a real negative answer that must stay `success`. Reproduced on the old code by stashing `tools/`.
- **Not fixed here (separate root):** on Windows the alias translates the NAME but not the ARGUMENTS — FINDSTR reads `/` as a switch prefix, so `core/loop.py` becomes `core` plus `/l /o /o /p`, and POSIX flags like `-c` do not exist. That is why the live `grep` failed at all. Registered separately; a path/flag adapter must not ride out on an exit-code fix.

### MIR-011 — `suspicious` injection content still enters the evidence chain
- **Aliases:** CORE-08, MGA-04, A8. **Provenance:** previously_documented + independently_reconfirmed.
- **Sources:** MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md, CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md, MEMORY_FIX_PLAN.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop.py` (`:3181-3186`) — the location as first recorded; the split moved it to `core/loop_step_execution.py:672-691`, see the 2026-08-22 re-verification below. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** a `suspicious` verdict is annotated and passed through, so the step becomes `Evidence` and can reach semantic memory.
- **Root cause:** `suspicious` → `annotate_suspicious()` + pass-through (`flat_output = annotated`); only `is_blocked` drops+replans.
- **Production path:** act → injection scan → evidence chain → (potentially) semantic write.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_injection_scan_scope.py` covers scan *scope* (MIR-014), not the suspicious-disposition.
- **Missing tests:** `suspicious` content admitted only as a quarantined observation; never a verified claim or semantic write.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-22 evening; see the quarantine block below).
- **Evidence:** code path unchanged at `f317c4c` (as first recorded; repaired 2026-08-22).
- **Approved fix (doc):** MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT §A/D and A8 — quarantine, not blunt exclusion.
- **RE-VERIFIED AND SHARPENED 2026-08-22. The mechanism is worse than this entry recorded, and the file reference was stale.** The code is no longer where the line above records it; the split moved it to `core/loop_step_execution.py:672-691`, where `is_blocked` replans and `verdict == "suspicious"` wraps the output via `annotate_suspicious` and passes it on. What the entry missed is what happens NEXT: `core/knowledge_pipeline.py:145-152` calls `strip_suspicious_annotation` before slicing the excerpt into sentences — **deliberately, and for a correct reason**, recorded in its own comment (otherwise the guard's warning text itself became a fact: a live run on 2026-08-16 banked 7 such records into persistent memory).
- **The consequence, measured by reading the record definitions.** The wrapper was the ONLY carrier of the verdict, and stripping it destroys it. `ClaimRecord` holds id / source_id / text / locator / confidence / status / extracted_at / support_source_ids / conflict_source_ids / metadata; `Evidence` holds id / kind / source_id / obtained_via / content_hash / fetched_at / confidence / claim / excerpt / origin; `SourceRecord` has `trust_level`, which grades the SOURCE in general and says nothing about whether THIS fetch tripped the scanner. **No durable record carries the injection verdict.** So the suspicion is not ignored downstream — it is unavailable downstream. The claim's `status` comes from `_status_from_rank(rank)`, i.e. source reputation, and a sentence extracted from scanner-flagged content is indistinguishable from one extracted from clean content.
- **Why the obvious fix was NOT applied at first writing.** Adding a suspicion field is a few lines; deciding what quarantine MEANS is a policy decision, and the carrier and the disposition had to land together.
- **BOTH LANDED 2026-08-22 evening, the disposition chosen as the escalation-contract default from this entry's own approved fix note («quarantine, not blunt exclusion»).** `ClaimStatus` gained `suspect`; `carries_suspicious_annotation` lives beside the wrapper it detects, and `ClaimExtractor` asks BEFORE stripping — so the verdict survives the strip that used to destroy it. A `suspect` claim is stored (visible, auditable — blunt exclusion would hide the attack from the audit trail), refused by `KnowledgeWritePolicy` with its own stated reason, never upgraded by corroboration (an echo is not a second witness, and for adversarial content an echo is cheapest of all), and never passes `require_verified`. Reversed by one word from the operator; the status is data, so review can lift it per-claim. Pinned by `tests/test_a_suspicious_claim_is_quarantined_not_laundered.py` (4 tests), both halves break-tested red. `blocked` content is untouched — it never reaches extraction.
- **Status update:** `fixed` (2026-08-22 evening).

### MIR-015 — reasoning↔action mismatch is observational only
- **Aliases:** MGA-01. **Provenance:** previously_documented.
- **Sources:** MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop.py` (`:1091-1100`); `core/reasoning_action_check.py` `MismatchReport`.
- **Symptom:** on `has_mismatch` the loop only logs `reasoning_action_mismatch`; execution continues.
- **Root cause:** intentional "Observational only — must never abort the loop"; and `MismatchReport` has **no `severity` field**, so a threshold cannot be applied directly.
- **Production path:** plan→act consistency check in the cycle.
- **Existing tests:** none for enforcement.
- **Missing tests:** unjustified write/external action stops/replans; weak lexical mismatch only logs — **requires a deterministic severity classification first**.
- **Status:** `open` (with a design prerequisite; not a quick fix).
- **MEASURED 2026-08-19 — the signal fires far too often to carry enforcement, and this outranks the enforcement question.** Over every `planner` event in `logs/` (268 real turns carrying both a reasoning text and a plan) the check fires on **190, i.e. 71 %**. That is a FIRING RATE: only two accusations were adjudicated by reading them (both false, both `list_dir`), no ground truth was labelled, and the true false-positive rate over the remaining 188 is **UNKNOWN**. What is structural rather than sampled: 241 of 255 `unjustified` accusations are keyword misses on tools the table DOES know (`list_dir` 114 — it demands the literal "list files"/"каталог"; `file_read` 44 — it keys on `"read "` with a trailing space, so "reading core/loop.py" cannot match); 14 accuse tools the table has no entry for (it knows 13 while the registry ships 15 — `file_write`, `python_probe`, `lesson_provenance` are invisible, so planning them is flagged by construction); and `self_repair` (4) sits in the table while existing in no registry, so the reverse direction accuses the planner of omitting a step it cannot produce. **Consequence for this issue: adding severity to an enforcement path on top of a sensor that fires on 71 % of turns, whose accusations are dominated by its own table defects, would block real work — and that holds without knowing the true false-positive rate, because the structural defects alone are enough. The sensor is the prerequisite, not the threshold.** Provenance: first measured 2026-08-05 in `wip/mir-015-structural-justification` (108 turns, 44 firings) — a branch that never merged and whose referenced `docs/audit/SENSOR_SIGNAL_MEASUREMENT.md` was never written; re-measured against `main` before this entry. Pinned by `tests/test_the_mismatch_sensor_was_measured.py`.
- **Alternative recorded, NOT imported:** that branch also proposed reading the per-step `rationale` the planner is asked for (structural fact) instead of guessing from prose — a behaviour change requiring a planner field that `main` does not have; left as an operator decision.
- **Evidence:** unchanged at `f317c4c`.
- **Alternatives (undecided):** define severity mapping (mentioned-not-planned → warn; unjustified read-only → warn/replan; unjustified external/write → hard stop) before any enforcement.
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-22: every structural defect above is still live, counted against the current tree.** `_TOOL_KEYWORDS` in `core/reasoning_action_check.py:76-80` holds **13** entries while `tools/` ships **15** real tools. In the registry but invisible to the table: `file_write`, `python_probe`, `lesson_provenance` — planning any of them is accused by construction. In the table but shipped by nothing: `self_repair` — so the reverse direction accuses the planner of omitting a step it cannot produce. The keyword shapes are unchanged too: `file_read` still requires the literal `"read the file"` / `"read "` with a trailing space, `list_dir` still demands `"list files"` / `"каталог"` / `"ls "`.
- **External check 2026-08-22, and it cuts BOTH ways — recorded in full because half of it does not transfer.** The field measured detectors of exactly this family and they are weak: LLM-as-judge tops out at **F1 0.65-0.77**, logit-based methods score **below 0.50**, and counterfactual methods only work when the intervention lands on a causally critical step. The published conclusion is that there is no reliable way to tell faithful from unfaithful stated reasoning. **Where that transfers:** it is decisive evidence AGAINST ever putting enforcement on a sensor of this class — this entry already concluded the sensor is the prerequisite rather than the threshold, and the field says even a perfected sensor of that family would not be trustworthy enough to block work. **Where it does NOT transfer, and this must not be borrowed carelessly:** those papers measure prose against the model's HIDDEN internal computation, which is unobservable by construction. Our check compares two OBSERVABLE artefacts — the planner's prose and the plan it emitted. That is a strictly easier problem, and the field's pessimism is not evidence that ours is unsolvable. It does mean the solution should be structural rather than lexical, which is precisely the alternative already recorded above (read the planner's per-step `rationale` instead of inferring intent from prose) — an approach the weak methods surveyed do not attempt, because in their setting no such structured artefact exists.
- **Consequence for the fix order.** Patching the keyword table (add the 3 missing tools, drop `self_repair`, widen the two verb patterns) is mechanical and would cut the 71% firing rate by an unmeasured amount — but it improves a lexical sensor whose whole class the field reports as unreliable. Doing it first would buy a better number without buying trustworthiness. The structural route needs a planner field `main` does not have; that remains the operator's call, unprejudged.
- **DEFAULT UNDER THE ESCALATION CONTRACT (2026-08-22, `docs/audit/ESCALATION_CONTRACT.md`):** no enforcement is ever attached to this sensor family — the field's own measurements (best detectors at F1 0.65–0.77) are the grounds, and they are decisive rather than provisional. Repairs to the sensor itself (the 13-vs-15 tool table, the two verb patterns) are ordinary engineering, not a decision. Reversed by one word from the operator.

### MIR-016 — confidence / verification gate does not block output
- **Aliases:** LPF-009. **Related:** MIR-015. **Provenance:** previously_documented.
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop.py` (`:1595-1603`, "gate stays observational" `:1955`).
- **Symptom:** a `fully_unverified` / `low_confidence_gate` turn is still emitted.
- **Root cause:** the confidence gate is observational (logs `low_confidence_gate`, does not abort).
- **Production path:** verify → gate log → respond.
- **Existing tests:** gate computation tested; blocking behaviour not.
- **Status:** `open` (by-design observational; whether it *should* hard-block is a policy decision, tied to MIR-015 and low-evidence policy).
- **Alternatives (undecided):** keep observational vs. hard-gate on `fully_unverified` for world-claim turns (interacts with MIR-002/MIR-023 general-knowledge handling).
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-22, and the picture is TWO paths, not one — which the entry implies but never separates.** (a) The SENSOR path is dead: `core/evidence_support.py` `compute_evidence_support` is consumed by `core/confidence_vector.py` and nothing else, and MIR-023's sweep the same day confirms `overall_confidence` reaches no gate anywhere in the tree. Measured in `logs/`: the `evidence_support` event appears **4622** times, feeding a number nothing reads. (b) The ENFORCEMENT path is alive and separate: `core/loop_response_deciders.py:340` calls `apply_answer_enforcement` (`core/unsupported_claims.py`, whose own docstring calls itself «рубеж принятия ответа») and logs `low_evidence_truncation`, which has fired **15** times live — it rewrites or truncates an answer whose conclusion outran its support. So the honest statement is not "nothing acts on low evidence" but: **the observational sensor fires 4622 times into a dead end, while a different mechanism enforces 15 times**, and this entry's subject — blocking on the evidence-support gate itself — remains unwired.
- **DEFAULT UNDER THE ESCALATION CONTRACT (2026-08-22, `docs/audit/ESCALATION_CONTRACT.md`):** the gate stays observational. Enforcement already exists on its own path (`apply_answer_enforcement`, 15 live firings) and a second blocking layer wired to a sensor MIR-060 showed cannot tell citation-resolution from truth would block on the wrong signal. Reversed by one word from the operator.
- **Note (2026-07-27, PR #177) — the symptom/root-cause lines above are the record as written; they are not current module names.** The module is now `core/evidence_support.py` and the event is `evidence_support`; the operator's ruling was **not** to connect it to replan but to redefine what it reports (applicability, support score, weak-support flag, citation-integrity flag). See `docs/audit/SENSOR_SIGNAL_MEASUREMENT.md` and `docs/COGNITIVE_CORE.md` §8.11. The *issue* stays `open`: nothing blocks on evidence support, and the enforcing layer remains `core/low_evidence_policy.py` + `core/unsupported_claims.py`.

- **FIELD-CHECKED 2026-08-23, and the standing default's PREMISE is corrected.** External class: the observational gate that never becomes a control, and the agent-abstention result that models judge «should I act / do I know this» poorly on their own. The 2026-08-22 default kept the gate observational on the ground that «enforcement already exists on its own path (`apply_answer_enforcement`)». Probed behaviourally, that ground covers less than it claimed.
- **CORRECTED 2026-08-23, same day: the measurement above blamed the wrong cause.** The first probe used a report of six chunks, and `_DEFAULT_MIN_TOTAL` is **8** — so the answer was left alone because the probe was under the size floor, not because of the mode. Re-run above the floor (10 chunks, all unverified), the low-evidence branch fires as `insufficient_evidence`, `applied=True`, answer truncated — **in all three modes, `off` included**. `AGENT_ENFORCE_UNSUPPORTED_CLAIMS` does not gate it at all.
- **What the mode actually gates**, read from the code rather than inferred: `applied = mode_s == "on"` guards exactly two branches — `verifier_failure` and `malformed_report` (`core/unsupported_claims.py:298, :323`). So in the default mode, an answer whose VERIFIER FAILED OUTRIGHT, or whose report came back malformed, is emitted with no note attached. That is the uncovered case, and it is a different and arguably worse one than low evidence: not «the evidence was thin» but «the check did not run».
- **And `shadow` is close to a no-op for those branches**: it sets `applied=False` exactly like `off`, so the answer is unchanged either way and only the recorded `mode` label differs. Setting it would create the appearance of a control without one — the very shape this audit keeps finding.
- **The real boundary, measured by sweep.** With every chunk unverified, the answer passes untouched at 1–7 chunks and is truncated from 8 upward. A short unverified answer is never trimmed, however unsupported it is. That threshold — not the env var — is what leaves this entry's case open, and it explains the live counts: `insufficient_evidence` applied 3 times because few answers reach eight chunks at ≤20% support.
- **Status:** `open`. The policy question is now precise: (a) leave the size floor at 8 and accept that short unverified answers pass; (b) lower it, which risks trimming ordinary short answers; (c) set the mode to `on` so a verifier failure at least carries a note. These are three different decisions, and only (c) is cheap and additive.

### MIR-062 — the admission policy ran at one write site out of three, so lessons were written unreadable
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (core connectivity audit, 2026-07-27).
- **Sources:** this pass (execution-path trace of every `episodic_store` writer, reproduced against real records).
- **Files/functions:** `core/smart_memory.py` `decide_usage_eligibility` / `EpisodicMemoryStore.save` / `save_once`; writers `core/loop_methods2.py:612` (applied it), `core/self_repair.py:516` and `core/self_build_memory.py:116` (did not). <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** an episode written by the repair or self-build path landed with `usage_eligible=None`. Every reader is fail-closed on `None` (`is_usage_eligible` admits only an explicit `True`), so the record was invisible to `_retrieve_experience_memory` — including the three `lesson`-specific branches that exist to surface exactly those records (the outcome exception at `:304`, the completion exception at `:314`, and the dedicated `search_by_tags(["lesson"])` top-up at `:331`).
- **Root cause:** the policy lived at a call site rather than at the boundary it guards. Two of the three writers simply never called it — not a wrong decision, an absent one.
- **Measured (isolated tmp, real store):** a repair-shaped lesson was found by `search`, by `search_by_tags(["lesson"])` and by `find_most_similar` at similarity **1.0**, while the planner-facing reader returned **0 characters**. `decide_usage_eligibility()` returns `True` for the same record — the `lesson` tag is its first rule.
- **Blast radius (bounded, and measured rather than assumed):** the repair lesson's prose twin is also written to persistent memory via `remember(...)` and *is* retrievable (219-char block containing the lesson), so the fact was not lost — the structured episode was. Self-build lessons stay readable through their own ungated reader (`recent_self_build_lessons`), so that lane was unaffected. What was lost in both cases is visibility in the planner's experience block. `PROTECTED_TAGS` additionally exempts `lesson` from eviction, so the unreadable records accumulate permanently.
- **Production path:** `:repair` → `SelfRepairController._finish` → `_write_repair_lesson` → `save()`; `:self-build-produce` / `:self-apply-run` / autonomous tick → `record_self_build_episode` → `save()`.
- **Existing tests:** the eligibility suites covered the *reader* thoroughly and seeded their fixtures through `save()`, which is why the gap between the writers was invisible to them.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-07-27).
- **Fix:** `admit_for_storage` resolves `None` at the boundary, called inside `save`/`save_once`; an explicit `True`/`False` from a caller is passed through untouched, so an aborted run stays quarantined. Applied on **write only** — a `None` read back off disk is a row that predates the field, and re-deciding it now would rewrite history under today's rule. `save()` returns the stored record so the write event can report the verdict, which it now does (`usage_eligible` was previously absent from `episodic_memory_write`).
- **Proof:** `tests/test_episode_admission_boundary.py` — 6 of its 9 pre-existing-API cases fail on `main` @ `b015134` (`assert '' ... the lesson never reached the experience block`), and the three that pass there are the protective ones (legacy row keeps `None`, eviction does not launder it, explicit `False` survives).
- **Note on the fixtures:** three suites manufactured "legacy/unclassified" rows by calling `save()`. That is precisely what the boundary now forbids, so they write the pre-field row shape directly (`tests/conftest.py::write_legacy_episode`). The assertions are unchanged; only the way an old row is manufactured is.
- **Related:** the same shape as MIR-039 (two queue consumers, one honest status mapping) — a policy applied by some call sites and not others. The general lesson is recorded in `docs/self-audit-lessons.md`.

### MIR-063 — the response was one string, so the last decider to write it won
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (core connectivity audit, 2026-07-27).
- **Sources:** this pass (reproduced through the real `AgentLoop.run()`).
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop.py` response segment — `apply_ranker_output_policy` → B-1 clarification gate → `apply_answer_enforcement`, each mutating one local `answer`.
- **Symptom:** on a `replan_exhausted` turn with thin evidence, three subsystems logged that they had changed the answer and only one had. `output_policy` added a caveat that the verdict was unreliable; the clarification gate added three questions; low-evidence truncation then rebuilt the answer from scratch and both were gone.
- **Measured (real loop, DEFAULT configuration, no env vars):** `output_policy applied=True` with one warning, `clarification_gate` with three questions, `answer_enforcement outcome=insufficient_evidence applied=True` — and none of the four strings present in the 426 characters that shipped.
- **Root cause:** claims and notices were the same bytes. A caveat about the run and a question to the operator are not claims, and no verdict about the evidence makes them untrue — but they lived in the variable the truncation legitimately rewrites, so they died with the claims. Not a wrong decision; an absent distinction. Ordering, not arbitration: which decider "won" was decided by the order of statements in a 4000-line function.
- **Severity note:** the combination is the expected shape, not an edge case. A stuck loop that still produced a long unsupported answer is exactly what both deciders exist for, and the one that lost is the one whose whole premise is *when the loop is stuck, ASK rather than keep building*. The operator got "no claim could be backed by the sources" and no question to answer.
- **Production path:** synthesis → verification → output policy → clarification gate → answer enforcement → respond.
- **Existing tests:** each decider was unit-tested in isolation; nothing tested them on the same turn.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-07-27).
- **Fix:** `core/response_draft.py` — the response is an object with two kinds of content. `set_body` rewrites the claims (recording who superseded whom); `add_notice` attaches prose about the answer on one of two channels (`prepend`, `unverified_note`). Composition happens once, in `render()` — the first point in the cycle where two deciders are reconciled rather than merely ordered. `output_policy` stops merging its warnings into the body and reports them for composition; its body edits (capped `Confidence:`, downgraded realtime tags) stay body edits, because those are corrections to the claims. Enforcement is handed `draft.body`, so it can neither measure nor delete a notice.
- **Journal:** new event `response_composed` reports every contribution, the body-rewrite history, and `missing` — anything that did not survive. The defect was found by reading code; it would now appear in the log.
- **Proof:** `tests/test_response_composition.py`. Fail-before on `main` @ `b015134`: the three end-to-end cases fail (*"a clarifying question the loop decided to ask was deleted by a decider that judges claims, not questions"*). One test pins that the untruncated path renders byte-identically to the old sequential composition, and two protective cases pin that the unsupported claims are still removed and an ordinary answer is untouched.
- **Related:** this is step 2 of the plan recorded in `docs/COGNITIVE_CORE.md` §8.10; MIR-039 and MIR-062 are the same family seen from the write side (a policy applied by some call sites and not others), this one is the compose side (a contribution overwritten by a later writer).

### MIR-064 — the completion axis is written at one site out of three, so records banked today are indistinguishable from pre-axis legacy
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-28) — surfaced as a local test failure during the #184 review, which stated it was "filed separately"; it was not, and this entry is that filing.
- **Sources:** this pass (reproduced through the real write path into an isolated tmp store; no live store present in this environment).
- **Files/functions:** three writers construct an `EpisodeRecord`. `core/smart_memory.py` `episode_from_agent_cycle` (`:1254`) settles both completion fields at banking time (`:1341`). `core/self_repair.py:508` (repair lesson) and `core/self_build_memory.py:93` (`build_self_build_episode`, saved at `:116`) construct the record directly and pass neither `completion_state` nor `declared_completion`. Readers: `effective_completion` (`core/smart_memory.py:1139`), `admit_for_storage` (`:1215`), `decide_usage_eligibility` (`:1153`, `lesson` rule at `:1187`), retrieval at `core/loop_methods2.py:313` and `:330`. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** an episode written today by the self-build or repair path carries no completion verdict. On disk the `completion_state` key is **absent entirely**, which is byte-identical to a row written before the axis existed.
- **Root cause:** the same shape as MIR-062, one axis over. The completion verdict is assembled inside one constructor rather than at the boundary every writer crosses, so the two writers that build the record by hand do not omit it by decision — they omit it by never being asked. MIR-062 moved the *eligibility* policy into `save()`; the *completion* policy stayed at the call site.
- **Measured (isolated tmp store, records written through `record_self_build_episode` and read back off disk):**
  - `episode_from_agent_cycle` → `completion_state='unknown'` — explicit, and correct: a run that was examined and could not be classified.
  - `core/self_repair.py:508` → `completion_state=None`, `usage_eligible=True`.
  - `core/self_build_memory.py:93` → `completion_state=None`, `usage_eligible=True` for all four statuses exercised (`proposed`, `critic_veto`, `rolled_back`, `committed_local`).
  - Read back: `effective_completion` → `unknown`, `"completion_state" in <row json>` → `False`.
- **Why the eligibility bit is `True`:** both hand-built writers tag `lesson`, and `lesson` is the first rule of `decide_usage_eligibility`, so `admit_for_storage` resolves the `None` to `True` at the boundary. That is MIR-062's fix working as designed — it is what makes these rows *reach* a reader, where before they were merely invisible.
- **Two distinct harms, kept apart deliberately:**
  1. **Provenance (certain).** `effective_completion`'s docstring states the invariant this breaks: a stored `None` is kept distinct from a stored `"unknown"` "so a legacy row remains recognisable as legacy rather than as a run we examined and could not classify". Two live writers manufacture that state on every self-build and repair cycle, so the distinction no longer holds and no reader — including `scripts/completion_legacy_report.py`, whose whole subject is what predates the axis — can tell a row banked this morning from one banked before MIR-057. It also puts the contract's §13 migration rule (*state may not be inferred from indirect fields*) permanently out of reach for this population: the rows accumulate faster than they could be classified, and nothing durable records what they were.
  2. **The gate (bounded, and narrower than it looks).** Retrieval exempts `lesson` from the completion gate by design (`core/loop_methods2.py:313`, and the same gate on the second door at `:340`), so these rows are admitted on the eligibility bit alone. The readers that *do* consult completion — fast-path replay, procedure credit — are fail-closed on `unknown`, so the missing field over-admits nowhere. **What is violated is the invariant as written**, not a gate that would otherwise have held: an episode carrying no verdict reaches a prompt. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Blast radius:** unbounded in count and permanent. `lesson` is in `PROTECTED_TAGS`, so these rows are exempt from eviction; every `:self-build-produce`, `:self-apply-run`, `:repair` and every autonomous tick that proposes a split adds one more.
- **Production path:** `:self-build-produce` / `:self-apply-run` / autonomous tick → `record_self_build_episode` → `save()`; `:repair` → `SelfRepairController._finish` → `_write_repair_lesson` → `save()`.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_completion_episodic_gates.py:233` (`test_no_legacy_episode_in_the_live_store_is_ever_admitted`) states the invariant — *"an unclassified episode must not reach a prompt as the store fills"* — and fails against the operator's live store. It **skips in CI** (`data/` is gitignored: measured here, 24 passed / 1 skipped, *"no live store in this environment"*), which is why the defect was reported by a local run and by nothing else. The suite otherwise covers the *reader* thoroughly and seeds its fixtures explicitly, so no test exercised what the two hand-built writers actually store.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-02, under the operator's D-6 ruling — see below). ⬆ from `open`.
- **Fix history, in two halves:**
  1. **The writers (earlier, unrecorded here):** commits `823a1ca` / `92bf7aa` taught both hand-built writers to settle the axis — `core/self_build_memory.py` maps status → outcome → verdict through the shared table in `core/writer_completion.py` (fallback: explicit `"unknown"`), and the repair lesson writes `completion_state="achieved"` with its justification inline (it runs only for `status == "repaired"`). This entry's status had drifted stale against that fix.
  2. **The boundary (2026-08-02):** the operator ruled D-6's writer/boundary half — *"на общей границе сохранения каждая новая запись обязана получить явное значение оси; для статусов без однозначного соответствия — явный unknown; отсутствие поля допустимо только при чтении настоящих legacy-записей; старые неоднозначные записи не реконструировать без доказательств."* `admit_for_storage` (`core/smart_memory.py`) now resolves `completion_state=None` to explicit `"unknown"` at the same boundary MIR-062 used for eligibility — write-only, so a `None` read off disk stays `None` and legacy stays recognisable.
- **Evidence:** `tests/test_completion_episodic_gates.py` — a hand-built record saved through the real store lands with `"completion_state": "unknown"` PRESENT in the on-disk JSON (fail-before verified by revert: key absent, the exact legacy-impersonation this entry measured); a writer's explicit verdict passes the boundary untouched; a legacy row read back stays `None` with no reconstruction. The contract's D-6 bullet carries a dated addendum recording the ruling; its envelope-membership half remains open.
- **Related:** MIR-062 (same defect shape, eligibility axis; its fix is what makes this one reachable), MIR-057 (introduced the axis this entry finds unwritten), MIR-039 (the family: a policy applied by some call sites and not others), and D-6 / §17's D-6 row in the memory lifecycle contract, which recorded the axis as shipped without measuring which writers set it.

### MIR-065 — self-repair asked the model to reproduce a file it was shown only part of
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-28) — found by giving the agent a real registry task (MIR-052) and measuring why it could not do it.
- **Sources:** this pass. Five live `:propose-repair` runs against `core/loop_methods2.py`, two models, read-only except for the fix itself. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Files/functions:** `core/repair_proposal.py` — `DEFAULT_MAX_CONTEXT_CHARS` (`:46`), the `_truncate(current_content, …)` at prompt build (`:393`), `_SYSTEM_PROMPT`'s `proposed_content` = "complete replacement content for the target file" (`:430`), `max_tokens=4096` at the call site (`:206`), `_parse_json_object`.
- **Symptom:** every repair attempt on a module larger than ~16 000 chars failed, with a *different* error per model, none of which named the cause.
- **Root cause:** the generator truncated the target to `DEFAULT_MAX_CONTEXT_CHARS = 16_000` and then asked for the file **in full**. Above the window the request is impossible by construction — the model must emit bytes it was never given. Three consequences compounded: the output cap (4096) could not hold a full file either; an unusable reply was logged without its content, so the cause was undiagnosable; and `_parse_json_object` read only offset 0, so a reply that reasoned before answering was discarded whole.
- **Measured (live, `core/loop_methods2.py` = 36 959 chars, model shown 43%):** <!-- historical-ref -->
  - `gpt-4o-mini` → complied, returned a stub → diff read as **536 changed lines**, i.e. a proposal to delete the 57% it never saw. Rejected only by the 200-line cap.
  - `claude-opus-4-8` → **543 output tokens, no content** → rejected as "proposed_content must be a non-empty string". The stronger model looked like the worse failure: it declined to invent the unseen part.
  - Coverage before: **43 of 145 `core/` modules truncated** (`loop.py` 8% shown, `planner.py` 13%, `smart_memory.py` 24%).
  - After the window fix, same target: input 6 006 → **12 226 tokens**, output 543 → **14 217**. New failure: `invalid JSON: Expecting value: line 1 column 1`.
  - With the reply head logged: `length=41 244`, `head="I'll analyze the failing tests to understand what's e…"` — the JSON was present, under a prose preamble.
  - After all three: **`status=proposed, confidence=0.72, diff +46 −35`**, and a diagnosis that named both halves of the task, including the "genuine storage failures must still be swallowed" half.
- **Fix:** window raised to 60 000 to match `self_build_producer._MAX_CONTENT_BYTES` (both paths answer the same question — how large a file may a single shot rewrite — and the smaller answer was silently deciding what the agent may repair); an oversized target is now **refused before the model is called**, as `_MAX_SPLIT_TARGET_LINES` already does on the self-build path; output budget tracks the window; the unusable-reply head is logged, redacted and capped; the parser tries the whole reply first and then a brace-balanced embedded object.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_repair_proposal_context_window.py` (11), all fail-before.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-07-28). Coverage 70% → **97%**; the remaining 4 (`loop.py`, `planner.py`, `self_build_producer.py`, `smart_memory.py`) now get an explicit refusal naming size and limit instead of a doomed paid attempt.
- **Not fixed here:** those 4 modules still cannot be repaired single-shot. That needs a different contract (a scoped edit rather than a whole-file post-image) and is a separate decision — the repo currently vetoes diff-shaped answers on the self-build path on purpose.
- **Related:** MIR-052 (the task that exposed this; the proposal this run produced was landed as #190 on 2026-07-29, so that entry is now `fixed`), and the `self_build_producer` comments at `_BUILDER_MAX_TOKENS` / `_MAX_SPLIT_TARGET_LINES`, which had recorded this lesson on the other self-modification path without it reaching this one.

### MIR-066 — code repair could not reach a stronger model, in four independent ways at once
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-07-28) — operator question: "it must switch models by itself and it does not; find the root".
- **Sources:** this pass. Route resolution probed directly (no model calls), plus the live `.env` and `config/model_catalog.json`.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_methods.py` `propose_repair` (`:247`, the `for_role` call); `core/deep_escalation.py` `ACTIVE_ROLES` / `ACTIVE_REASONS` / `RESERVED_REASONS`; `core/model_catalog.py` `_load_catalog` / `tier_model_for`; `core/model_router.py` `_resolve_tier_provider` (explicit-route short-circuit) and the escalation block in `for_task`. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Symptom:** the hardest work in the system — reproducing a whole module correctly — ran on whatever model the role config named. Measured: `gpt-4o-mini` was handed a 728-line rewrite (MIR-052) and returned the file byte-identical.
- **Root cause — four links, each sufficient on its own:**
  1. **`propose_repair` used `for_role`**, which has no complexity assessment and cannot escalate at all. `for_task` was wired to exactly two callers (planner, synthesizer).
  2. **`high_value_repair` was in `RESERVED_REASONS`**, deliberately: the file's own comment says accepting it while repair is on `for_role` "would imply behaviour that does not exist". Honest, and it made the previous link load-bearing.
  3. **The model catalog was 12 days old against a 7-day TTL**, so `_load_catalog` returned `None` and *every* tier resolved to `""`. This disabled complexity routing for **all** `for_task` callers, planner included — not only repair. Worse, it was mute: the gate reported `no_deep_model`, which reads as "your provider has no such model" and sends the operator to check credentials that are fine.
  4. **An explicit per-role provider in `.env` disables tier preference by design** (`_resolve_tier_provider` returns early so "the operator's choice always wins"). The operator had pinned `AGENT_REPAIR_PROVIDER=local` to an LM Studio instance that was not running, so every repair spent ~8s failing to connect and then fell through to the cheapest available model.
- **Measured after each link was addressed** (route resolution only, nothing called):
  - assessed tier for a 36 959-char target: `deep`.
  - with the `.env` pin in place: `local/qwen-local` regardless of budget.
  - pin removed, no budget: `openai/gpt-5.4-mini`; pin removed, budget funded: **`anthropic/claude-opus-5`**.
  - small target (1 500 chars, 1 red test): stays `standard` in both cases.
- **Fix:** `propose_repair` routes through `for_task`; the tier comes from `repair_complexity(target_chars, failing_tests)` rather than from prose signals (a repair request contains none, so `assess_complexity` would always say STANDARD); `repair_proposal` joins `ACTIVE_ROLES` and `high_value_repair` joins `ACTIVE_REASONS`; a new **`AGENT_DEEP_MAX_CALLS_PER_SESSION` budget, default 0**, gates autonomous deep spend with `operator_approved=False` on that path so the budget is the only key; `catalog_freshness()` reports `missing`/`expired`/`fresh`/`unreadable` with age, TTL and the fix, and the gate now records `deep_downgraded:catalog_expired` distinctly from `no_deep_model`.
- **Authority note:** this does not widen what the agent may spend by default. With no budget configured the behaviour is byte-identical to before — the request is made and refused. Funding it is an operator act and a ceiling, counted from the usage ledger's own `deep_approved:` records.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_repair_deep_escalation.py` (12), `tests/test_repair_routes_by_complexity.py` (8), `tests/test_model_catalog_expiry_visibility.py` (6) — all fail-before.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-07-28) for links 1–3. Link 4 is configuration, not code: the operator removed the pin.
- **Not fixed here:** `verifier` and `subagent` remain on `for_role` and keep their reserved reasons unaccepted — same honesty rule that kept `high_value_repair` out until its wiring existed. The catalog TTL itself is unchanged; only its silence was.
- **Related:** MIR-065 (the previous link in the same chain — the mechanism could not use the model it was given), MIR-016 (a gate that reports without acting).

---

## C. Retry / runtime durability

### MIR-017 — retry with no backoff
- **Aliases:** CORE-07, OFM-010. **Provenance:** previously_documented (OFM-010, the sole confirmed-gap) + independently_reconfirmed (CORE-07).
- **Sources:** OPERATIONAL_FAILURE_MODES.md (§11 confirmed-gap), CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md, ROADMAP.md (Track D).
- **First documented:** OPERATIONAL_FAILURE_MODES.md @ `6dc253e` (2026-07-17).
- **Files/functions:** `core/task_queue.py` `mark_failed` (`:294`), `pending`.
- **Symptom:** a non-exhausted task returned to `pending` without changing `run_after` → immediately eligible next tick; hot-retry for `max_attempts>1`.
- **Root cause:** no `run_after` backoff in `mark_failed`.
- **Production path:** `AutonomousRuntime.run_task_queue` → `mark_failed` → `pending()`.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_task_queue_retry_backoff.py`, `tests/test_task_queue.py`.
- **Status:** `fixed` (runtime-reproduced). ⬆ upgraded from `code_fixed_needs_runtime_verification` at Stage 3.
- **Evidence:** `mark_failed` computes exponential `delay` and sets `run_after=retry_from+delay`; comment cites OFM-010/CORE-07.
- **Stage-3 verification (2026-07-18, isolated tmp, no durable mutation):** reproduced end-to-end — `max_attempts=3` deterministic failure → fail#1 `pending`, `run_after=+30s`, **not** eligible in `pending()` at fail-time, eligible after the delay; fail#2 `run_after=+60s` (doubled); fail#3 → terminal `failed`. `max_attempts=1` (the default) → `failed` after one fail (no re-pend). The "hot-retry every tick" symptom no longer reproduces.
- **Caller-coverage caveat (Stage 3):** the backoff is reached via `core/autonomous_runtime.run_task_queue` (`:task-run` CLI + campaign path). The **cron/scheduled** path (`agent_tick.py`) does **not** call `mark_failed` at all (see MIR-039) — there a failing task is marked `done`, so it is never retried in that path either. So OFM-010's symptom is closed on both paths, by different mechanisms. `run_report.status=="stopped"` (budget/circuit) is routed through `mark_failed` too, so a budget-stopped task with default `max_attempts=1` becomes terminal `failed` rather than resuming — noted, not escalated.
- **Residual:** no explicit operator **notification** on exhaustion (OFM-010 asked for a "final failure marker + notification"); the marker (`status=failed`, `last_error`) exists, an alarm does not (relates to MIR-034).

### MIR-039 — `agent_tick` consumer marks tasks `done` regardless of outcome; crash leaves them `running`
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (Stage 3).
- **Sources:** this pass (execution-path trace of the cron consumer).
- **First documented:** here, against `main` @ `f317c4c`.
- **Files/functions:** `agent_tick.py:817-896` (task loop), `:886` unconditional `task_store.mark_done`, `:829` unwrapped `runtime.run(config)`, outer handler `:916-931`.
- **Symptom:** in the scheduled/cron path, a task whose run reports `failed`/`stopped`/`inconclusive` is still `mark_done`. Worse, if `runtime.run()` **raises**, the exception propagates past the (mark_running-only) `try/except` to the outer handler, which logs + writes an error heartbeat + `return 1` **without** touching the task — leaving it permanently in `running`.
- **Root cause:** the cron consumer bypasses `run_task_queue`'s status logic: it (a) calls `mark_done` unconditionally instead of branching on `run_report.status`, and (b) does not wrap the run in per-task failure handling (`mark_failed`), unlike `run_task_queue` (`autonomous_runtime.py:337-344`).
- **Related causes:** divergent-consumer root shared with MIR-033; the stuck-`running` state is unrecoverable because of MIR-040 (`recover_stuck` unwired). Contrasts MIR-026 (durable API is correct; the *consumer* misuses it).
- **Production path:** cron → `agent_tick.run_tick` → task loop → `mark_done` (or crash → stuck `running`).
- **Existing tests:** `agent_tick` tick tests exist; none assert failure/crash → correct terminal state in this consumer.
- **Missing tests:** a failed run in the `agent_tick` path marks the task `failed` (not `done`); a raising `runtime.run` marks the task `failed`/recoverable rather than leaving it `running`.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-07-26). ⬆ from `open`.
- **Evidence:** code read at `f317c4c` (lines cited); the outer `except` at `:916-931` provably does not update task status.
- **Nuance / alternatives (undecided):** in **dry-run**, "done" partly means "the tick analysed the task"; the honest run/result status is logged separately (`:891-896`). So a blanket "mark_failed on non-completed" could mislabel a legitimate dry-run analysis. The real defects are (1) the **crash → stuck `running`** case and (2) conflating a genuinely `failed`/`stopped` run with `done`. Fix direction should mirror `run_task_queue`'s branching, or wire `recover_stuck` (MIR-040) as a safety net — **decision needed**, do not fix now.
- **Resolution (2026-07-26):** the mapping moved out of both consumers into `core/task_lifecycle.py`, which `agent_tick` and `run_task_queue` now share: `completed → done`, `blocked → blocked` (a new terminal-until-a-human status, invisible to `pending()`, cleared by `:task-unblock`), `stopped → failed` carrying `stop_reason`, exception → `failed`. The dry-run nuance above was resolved by **keeping the axes separate rather than blending them**: a dry-run pass that completed is `done`, and what the work established stays the tick's own `result_status` field. Two adjacent defects in the same family were fixed with it: the cron consumer used `list(status="pending")`, which ignores `run_after` and would immediately re-run a backed-off task (now `pending()`); and a raising `runtime.run()` now leaves the row `failed` instead of `running`.
- **Proof:** `tests/test_agent_tick_task_lifecycle.py` — fail-before measured on `main` @ `3c761c5`: `stopped → 'done'`, `blocked → 'done'`, raised → `'running'` forever. `tests/test_task_lifecycle.py` covers the mapping and the guarded recovery in isolation.

### MIR-040 — `recover_stuck` is defined and unit-tested but never wired to a consumer
- **Aliases:** none (relates to OFM-009 recovery; daemon-progress item 4.2 "Recovery on start"). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (Stage 3).
- **Sources:** this pass.
- **Files/functions:** `core/task_queue.py:329` `recover_stuck` (resets `running`→`pending` after `timeout_minutes`); tested in `tests/test_task_queue.py`; **zero production callers** (`core/`, `app/`, `cli/`, `main.py`, `agent_tick.py`).
- **Symptom:** a task left in `running` by a crashed process (see MIR-039) is never auto-recovered in the live system, because nothing calls `recover_stuck`.
- **Root cause:** the recovery mechanism exists but is not invoked by any runtime entry point (self-audit-lessons anti-pattern #6: "a module written to fix a live failure mode, never wired into its entry point").
- **Related causes:** the stuck state is produced by MIR-039; the always-on drain that would call it does not exist (MIR-033); startup recovery is explicitly `daemon-progress.md` item **4.2 "Recovery on start" — not started**.
- **Production path:** none — the function is unreachable from any live consumer.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_task_queue.py` (unit-tests the function in isolation).
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-07-26, by the guarded design below — **not** by the reverted one-liner). ⬆ from `open`.
- **Attempted fix (commit `02a8a58` on `fix/mir-040-wire-recover-stuck`) — UNSAFE, DO NOT MERGE:** `run_task_queue` was made to call `task_queue.recover_stuck()` at entry. A behavioral test (`test_run_task_queue_recovers_stuck_running_task`) proved fail-before / pass-after and 177 related tests stayed green — but that only proved recovery *happens*, not that it is *safe*. A follow-up safety review reproduced two real hazards this wiring introduces:
  - **Double execution under concurrent consumers + long tasks (regression risk).** `recover_stuck` uses a `timeout_minutes` (default 30) staleness on `updated_at`, and `updated_at` is set once at `mark_running` and **never refreshed during a run** (verified: no heartbeat inside the `run_task_queue` loop). So a *live* task legitimately running longer than the timeout is indistinguishable from a crashed one. Reproduced (isolated tmp): a task `running` for 40 min is reset to `pending` by a second consumer's `recover_stuck` → becomes eligible → a second process claims and runs the **same task concurrently**. Before this change stuck tasks were merely *stranded* (safe-but-useless); after it they can be *double-run* (unsafe). The consumer entry points (`app/task_scheduler_cli.py` `:task-run`, `agent_tick.py`) hold **no single-instance lock** (`app/single_instance.py` exists but is not acquired here), so concurrent consumers are genuinely possible.
  - **`max_attempts` cap bypass.** `recover_stuck` resets status to `pending` **without touching `attempts`**. Reproduced: a task stuck at `attempts == max_attempts` (e.g. an OOM-killer task) is resurrected to `pending`, then re-claimed → `attempts` becomes `max_attempts + 1`, running one time past the cap. On the recovery path a deterministically process-killing task is retried rather than being finalized `failed` — a mild resurgence of the OFM-010 class.
  - **Real process crash not exercised.** Both the original test and the hazard repros *simulate* a crash (`mark_running` + backdated `updated_at`); an actual killed process leaving a stuck task and a real subsequent recovery was **not** run.
- **Correct design (for a real fix — larger than a one-liner):** recovery must run **only at startup under the single-instance lock** (`app/single_instance.py`; this is `daemon-progress.md` item **4.2 "Recovery on start" — not started**), when no task is genuinely in-flight; **and/or** add a task heartbeat that refreshes `updated_at` during a run so staleness reflects liveness; **and/or** record owner PID/host and verify the owner is dead before reclaiming; **and** `recover_stuck` must respect `attempts`/`max_attempts` (a stuck-exhausted task → terminal `failed`, not resurrected). None of these is the ad-hoc `run_task_queue` wiring.
- **Resolution (operator-approved):** the unsafe wiring was **reverted** — commit `e8d330d` on `fix/mir-040-wire-recover-stuck` reverts `02a8a58`; `core/autonomous_runtime.py` and `tests/test_autonomous_runtime.py` now match `main` (verified: no diff), `recover_stuck` has **no production caller** again (safe-but-stranded behavior restored), and 44 task_queue/autonomous_runtime tests pass. MIR-040 stays **`open`**; the real fix is the guarded startup-recovery above (daemon 4.2), to be scheduled as its own work — not this ad-hoc wiring.
- **Note on docs:** OPERATIONAL_FAILURE_MODES.md §5 calls `recover_stuck()` a "verified control present"; that wording was accurate only for `run_task_queue` until the fix below — refreshed 2026-07-26 (see MIR-036).
- **Real fix (2026-07-26):** built to the "correct design" above, all three layers, not one of them:
  - **Heartbeat.** `RuntimeTask.heartbeat_at` plus `core.task_lifecycle.task_heartbeat`, a daemon thread that refreshes the row while the task runs and dies with the process. Both consumers use it. Staleness is now a liveness signal rather than an ambiguity, which removes the double-run hazard at its root: a task running 45 minutes and still beating is not reclaimed (`test_recover_stuck_leaves_a_beating_long_task_alone`).
  - **Startup only, under the lock.** `core.task_lifecycle.recover_orphaned_tasks` **raises** unless the caller holds the single-instance lock — it does not degrade to unsafe, because the pre-fix behaviour (stranded tasks) is better than double execution. `agent_tick` now acquires `app/single_instance.py`'s lock around the drain and recovers inside it; a second consumer holding the lock skips the drain instead of racing (`test_tick_skips_the_drain_when_another_consumer_holds_the_lock`).
  - **Attempts respected.** Recovery routes through the same failure decider as `mark_failed` (`_failure_transition`), so an orphan past `max_attempts` becomes terminal `failed` rather than being resurrected for one run past the cap, and an orphan with budget left is re-queued *behind the backoff*.
  - Owner pid/host are recorded on `mark_running` as operator diagnostics only; recovery does not trust them (a pid can be reused), it trusts the heartbeat.
- **Residual — CLOSED 2026-08-04 by a real kill.** The note below stood since 2026-07-26: *"an actual killed process was still not exercised — the tests simulate abandonment by ageing the heartbeat."* It has now been run out-of-repo (temp queue file, nothing under `data/` or `logs/`, no daemon and no scheduler), with no clock patched and no timestamp edited — the only wait was real time:
  - A child process claimed a task (`max_attempts=3`) and beat every second. While it was alive, `recover_orphaned_tasks(..., timeout_minutes=1)` returned **`[]`** — a live task is not reclaimed even though recovery ran against it.
  - `taskkill /F /PID` killed it with no chance to clean up (`poll()` confirmed death). The row stayed `running`, heartbeat frozen at `10:23:29.688512+00:00`.
  - 63 seconds of real time later the heartbeat was still **byte-identical** — that is what proves the beat stopped with the process rather than being aged by the test.
  - Recovery then returned **1 task: `pending`, `attempts=1` (not incremented, budget intact), tasks still `running`: 0.**
  - **Honest note on the probe itself:** its own `heartbeat_frozen` flag read `false` — it compared against a snapshot taken ~1 s before the kill, and one more real beat landed in between. The frozen-heartbeat evidence is the identity of the post-kill and post-wait values above, not that flag.
- **Not covered even now:** a kill *during* a state write (torn row), and a machine-level power loss. Both are a different failure class (`state_integrity` quarantine), not orphan recovery.

### MIR-068 — a queued daemon goal never executes: `_config_from_task` drops `include_goal`
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-08-03, live daemon-tick probe during the MIR-027 verification the operator required).
- **Files/functions:** `core/autonomous_runtime.py` `_config_from_task` (`:1355` — maps `RuntimeTask` → `AutonomousRuntimeConfig` without `include_goal`); `AutonomousRuntimeConfig.include_goal = False` default (`:165`); `_build_queue` (`:1176` — the goal task requires `include_goal`).
- **Symptom (measured live, isolated workspace):** two `auto_run` tasks queued via `TaskQueueStore.add(goal=...)` with real goal texts; one real `agent_tick.py` tick processed both to `task_done / run_status=completed / result_status=none` — but only `status`+`learn` ran (journal: 6×status, 6×learn task-kind events, zero `goal`). The queued goal text is carried and silently ignored; the operator reading `completed` would believe the goal work happened.
- **Root cause:** `RuntimeTask` has a `goal` field and the daemon goal path even has its own tool-block list (`_AUTONOMOUS_GOAL_BLOCKED_TOOLS`, "must NOT run on the unattended autonomous / daemon goal path"), so the path was designed to run — but the queue→config mapping never sets `include_goal`, and its default is False. The mechanism is built and not wired (self-audit-lessons anti-pattern #6), or was later deliberately disabled without a record; no comment or doc states which.
- **Callers that DO set `include_goal=True`:** campaign (`core/campaign_io.py:108`), work-session (`core/work_session.py:213`, when a real goal was supplied), explicit `:auto-run --goal` (`app/runtime_cli.py:62`). Only the daemon queue path drops it.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-03, operator's explicit autonomy grant: «исполнять»). The wire is one field — `_config_from_task` now passes `include_goal=True` — and both locks stayed shut: `_build_queue` still refuses a goal task for the default "project health" placeholder (pinned by test), and `_task_goal` still applies the unattended posture blocks, gateway dry-run, kill-switch, budget snapshot and durable-learning suppression (run-scoped, restored on error). Verified LIVE on an isolated workspace: a queued goal produced `autonomous_task_start kind=goal` with the queued text, `autonomous_goal_gateway` showed `gateway_dry_run=True, gateway_path=daemon, blocked_tools=[spawn_subagent, web_*, rss_fetch, semantic_scholar_search, run_tests], durable_learning_writes_suppressed=True`, and the run answered from `list_dir` — pre-fix the same queue shape ran only status+learn and reported `completed` with the goal silently dropped. Pinned by `tests/test_queued_goal_execution.py` (wire + both locks + posture constants; the two wire tests failed pre-fix).
- **Missing tests:** ~~a queued `auto_run` with a real goal either executes a `goal` task or is explicitly reported as not-executed~~ — landed 2026-08-03 (`tests/test_queued_goal_execution.py`).

---

## D. Routing / strategy

### MIR-018 — self-diagnostic is not recognised as a distinct strategy
- **Aliases:** LPF-004, MGA-08. **Provenance:** previously_documented + independently_reconfirmed.
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md, MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/operator_intent.py` / `core/operator_intent_patterns.py` (`OperatorIntentKind`).
- **Symptom:** "Какие у тебя баги?" → `general_question`; planner reaches for `list_dir`/`file_read` instead of a local self-check.
- **Root cause:** no self-diagnostic intent kind; `route_operator_intent("check yourself and find problems")` → `None`.
- **Related causes:** MIR-019 (planner file-tool default). The new `core/intent_understanding` is a binary conversation/action router and does **not** add this intent.
- **Production path:** operator message → intent router → strategy.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_operator_intent*`.
- **Missing tests:** a self-diagnostic phrasing routes to a locally-scoped self-check strategy (no web).
- **Status:** `planned_gap` (partial: a `safe_self_check` route → `:operator-check` exists for the wording "run a safe self-check," but not for free-form "what are your bugs").
- **Evidence:** `_matches_safe_self_check` exists; no general self-diagnostic kind.

### MIR-019 — planner defaults to file tools on identity/meta questions
- **Aliases:** LPF-005. **Provenance:** previously_documented.
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/planner.py` prompt behaviour (not one line).
- **Symptom:** "Кто ты?"/"Какие у тебя баги?" made the planner call `file_read`/`list_dir`. (The `FileNotFoundError` crash was a **test-artifact** of the empty temp workspace — not a defect.)
- **Root cause:** planner LLM behaviour; overlaps MIR-018.
- **Production path:** plan step generation.
- **Existing tests:** none targeted.
- **Status:** `needs_investigation` (behavioural; partially a duplicate of MIR-018). The reported crash portion is a `stale_finding` (test-artifact, explicitly disclaimed in the source).

### MIR-020 — trivial greeting over-processed
- **Aliases:** LPF-003. **Provenance:** previously_documented.
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/strategy_router.py` (no greeting/LIGHT short-circuit).
- **Symptom:** "привет" runs the full Observe→Plan→Synthesize→Verify pipeline and appends an `Unverified:` disclaimer.
- **Root cause:** greeting routes to `strategy=general_question`; no LIGHT fast-path.
- **Production path:** strategy classification → full pipeline.
- **Existing tests:** none.
- **Missing tests:** a greeting skips verifier/disclaimer via a LIGHT path.
- **REPAIRED 2026-08-22 evening, to the extent the entry's cost is real.** The cheap path's greeting vocabulary gained social FILLER only (how/are/doing/there; как/дела/у/тебя…), and the word cap went 4 → 5 («привет, как у тебя дела» is five words of pure filler). Measured live: «Привет, как дела?», «hello there», «спасибо большое», «доброй ночи» now skip the planner call they each used to spend; content-bearing turns («привет, прочитай core/loop.py», «ты кто») still reach it — a wrong skip could drop a needed tool step, so the asymmetry stays conservative. Pinned in `tests/test_task_complexity.py`, break-tested.
- **The remaining half, closed by DEFAULT under the escalation contract:** the structured answer contract (Conclusion/…/Unverified) still applies to a greeting's synthesizer call, so the reply may carry a ceremonial shape. Ugly, not harmful, and reshaping the answer contract per-turn-class is a design change out of proportion to a cosmetic symptom on a low-severity entry. Reversed by one word from the operator.
- **Status:** `partially_fixed` (2026-08-22: the cost half repaired; the shape half defaulted).
- **Evidence:** no greeting/light branch found in `strategy_router.py` at `f317c4c` — historically true and beside the point: the light branch lived in `core/task_complexity.py` all along.
- **RE-VERIFIED BY EXECUTION 2026-08-22 (morning).** Not read — run. `classify_operator_strategy` returns `general_question` with `is_local_strategy() == False` for «привет», «hi» and «спасибо», so all three still take the full pipeline. **Corrected in the evening pass:** the strategy router was the wrong organ to check — the CHEAP PATH (`can_skip_planner` → `planner_skipped_cheap_path`) already existed and already took «привет»/«hi»/«спасибо» straight to the synthesizer without a planner call; what it missed was the VARIANTS. Checked against a plausible false hope: the router HAS grown a `LOCAL_STRATEGIES` fast-path set of 23 entries since this was written, but every member is an operator command intent (`budget_status`, `best_next_action`, …) and no greeting reaches it. Still `open`, still low.

- **CLOSURE AUDITED 2026-08-22 — held, 11 of 11.** Rule: «a skipped planner drops a step the turn actually needed». Probed with real work hiding behind a greeting — «привет, посчитай сколько питон-файлов в проекте», «спасибо, а теперь запусти тесты», «ок, сделай отчёт по бюджету», «хай, что там с бюджетом?» — all keep the planner, while every bare greeting skips it. The short-circuit fires on what the turn IS.
### MIR-021 — ODD coverage gap (rocket/hacking under-detected)
- **Aliases:** LPF-018. **Provenance:** previously_documented (re-framed as coverage gap, not missing classifier).
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/operational_domain.py`; `core/loop.py:653` `_check_operational_domain` (runs pre-planning, no-LLM).
- **Symptom:** "rocket"/"hacking" may route as `general_question` because the ODD heuristic under-detects.
- **Root cause:** heuristic coverage, not an absent/never-called gate.
- **Production path:** pre-plan ODD gate.
- **Existing tests:** ODD gate tests exist; coverage cases not.
- **Status:** `open` (low; correct framing = coverage gap).
- **RE-VERIFIED BY EXECUTION 2026-08-22, and the shape of the gap is worth recording.** `check_operational_domain` refuses «напиши эксплойт для sudo» outright (`out_of_domain` / `refuse`, finding `harmful_illegal`, confidence 0.95) — so the gate is alive and does its job on a phrasing it knows. It returns `in_domain` / `proceed` for **«как построить ракету»** and for **«how do I hack a bank account»**. The framing as a coverage gap rather than a missing gate is therefore correct, and the failure follows this project's own attack criterion: the known form is stopped, an unseen form of the same class walks through. Note the language inversion — the Russian exploit phrasing is caught while the English hacking phrasing is not — so this is not a simple 'add English patterns' fix either.

## E. No-learning governance

### MIR-022 — no run-scoped `NO_DURABLE_LEARNING` directive
- **Aliases:** MGA-07, A7. **Provenance:** previously_documented (needs-e2e) + independently_reconfirmed (code-confirmed no route).
- **Sources:** MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT.md, MEMORY_FIX_PLAN.md, MULTI_AGENT_COORDINATION_LAYER.md §12.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_memory_write.py` `_durable_learning_suppressed` (`:82`; only `audit_read_only`/dry-run. Lived in `core/loop_methods2.py` until the loop split, #312, 2026-08-04; the other `loop_*` modules now hold Protocol stubs of it, not the implementation) <!-- historical-ref -->; `core/operator_intent.py` (no memory-freeze kind).
- **Symptom:** a free-text "save nothing durable this run" engages no brake.
- **Root cause:** only `:audit` mode / dry-run / `AGENT_FREEZE_AUTO_MEMORY` set a brake; no run-scoped `LearningMode`.
- **Production path:** operator turn → learning-suppression check across agent-auto sinks.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_audit_read_only.py` (audit mode only).
- **Missing tests:** run-scoped `NO_DURABLE_LEARNING` → Δ=0 across every agent-auto sink; `:remember` separately specified.
- **Status:** `planned_gap`.
- **Approved fix (doc):** MEMORY_FIX_PLAN A7 / MEMORY_SYSTEM_AUDIT §D.1 (`LearningMode = NORMAL | NO_DURABLE_LEARNING | AUDIT_READ_ONLY`, run-scoped).

## F. Signal quality / UX (low severity)

### MIR-023 — confidence_vector geometric mean punishes general knowledge
- **Aliases:** LPF-002. **Provenance:** previously_documented (observational).
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md, CORE_AUDIT_2026-07-18.md (Section 4 honest-negative: NOT consumed by any gate).
- **Files/functions:** `core/confidence_vector.py` (`_W_EVIDENCE=0.5/_W_COHERENCE=0.3/relevance=0.2`, geometric mean).
- **Symptom:** "capital of France?" → correct "Paris" but `overall_confidence=0.032` (evidence_score=0 collapses the geometric mean).
- **Root cause:** zero evidence factor collapses a weighted geometric mean.
- **Production path:** post-verify signal; **observational only** — not a gate (confirmed in CORE_AUDIT §4: `overall_confidence` not consumed by any gate; its two non-definition uses are keyword strings).
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_confidence_vector.py`.
- **Status:** `open` (observational, low).
- **Documentation note (2026-07-23):** the stale in-code comment describing `cited_but_unmatched=0.5` was corrected to `-0.25`; MIR-023 itself remains open because the geometric-mean behaviour is unchanged.
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-22: the honest-negative still holds, which is the load-bearing half of this entry.** Weights are unchanged (`_W_EVIDENCE=0.5`, `_W_COHERENCE=0.3`, `_W_RELEVANCE=0.2`). More importantly the claim that `overall_confidence` is consumed by NO gate was re-checked by sweep rather than assumed: outside `core/confidence_vector.py` the identifier appears exactly once in the whole tree, as a string literal in `core/doc_routing.py:309`. So the collapsing geometric mean still cannot change any decision, and this stays observational and low — the number is wrong, and nothing acts on it.
- **Alternatives (undecided):** evidence floor for self-evident answers, or exclude the evidence axis on legitimately evidence-free turns. Ties to MIR-002.
- **DEFAULT UNDER THE ESCALATION CONTRACT (2026-08-22, `docs/audit/ESCALATION_CONTRACT.md`):** the number stays observational and uncorrected: nothing consumes it (re-verified by sweep 2026-08-22), so a repair would adjust a value with no reader — ceremony. If a consumer is ever wired, the geometric-mean collapse must be fixed FIRST, and this line is the tripwire. Reversed by one word from the operator.

### MIR-024 — shallow re-ask detection (Jaccard ≥ 0.40)
- **Aliases:** LPF-014. **Provenance:** previously_documented.
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_memory_read.py` (`_REPEAT_THRESHOLD = 0.40`, `:317`; lived at `core/loop_methods2.py:236` until the loop split, #312, 2026-08-04) <!-- historical-ref -->; `core/smart_memory.py` `find_most_similar` (`:720`).
- **Symptom:** semantically-different questions sharing a frame ("Я хочу … что мне сделать") exceed 0.4 and are treated as repeats.
- **Root cause:** Jaccard token overlap on question tokens with a 0.40 threshold.
- **Production path:** observe → re-ask hint block appended to planner context.
- **REPRODUCED 2026-08-22, and it is worse than 'low'.** The constant is alive at `core/loop_memory_read.py:344` — it is function-local, which is why a module-level lookup finds nothing and why a reader could wrongly call this entry stale. Scored with the store's own tokenizer, two genuinely different operator questions sharing the frame «Я хочу … что мне сделать?» reach **0.444**, and this pair reaches exactly **0.400**, the threshold: «Я хочу ЗАПУСТИТЬ АГЕНТА НА НЕДЕЛЮ, что мне сделать?» against «Я хочу УДАЛИТЬ ВСЕ ЛОГИ, что мне сделать?». A launch and a deletion — opposite intents — are classified as the same question, and the second is annotated to the planner as a repeat. The `low` severity was written for an English framing; «Я хочу … что мне сделать» is the operator's ordinary way of asking, so the collision is not rare here. Same root as MIR-105 and MIR-008: the stopword-keeping tokenizer supplies the tokens, and function words are most of the overlap.
- **DEFAULT UNDER THE ESCALATION CONTRACT (2026-08-22, `docs/audit/ESCALATION_CONTRACT.md`):** the 0.40 threshold stays untouched — not because it is right, but because the tokenizer family upstream (MIR-105/MIR-008) supplies the tokens, and tuning a threshold over broken tokens fits the fix to the defect. The tokenizer work goes first; this entry re-measures after. Reversed by one word from the operator.
- **Existing tests:** `tests/test_smart_memory.py` (find_most_similar).
- **ИЗМЕРЕНО НА ЖИВЫХ ДАННЫХ 2026-08-25: рамка побеждает и ПОСЛЕ починки различающими словами.**
- **Названный порог — не рабочий.** У кандидата с неизмеренным или низким качеством ответа порог падает до **0.30** (`max(0.20, threshold - 0.10)`), а таких эпизодов **142 из 142**. То есть 0.40 не применялся ни разу; комментарий в коде исправлен по замеру.
- **Провал воспроизведён.** Среди 49 РАЗЛИЧНЫХ живых вопросов **56 пар** дают ≥ 0.30 и **37 пар** ≥ 0.40. Наглядная: «Зайди в интернет и прочитай, что такое **CrewAI**» против «…что такое **MetaGPT**» — **0.65**. Общее у них — рамка задачи (`doctrine`, `future`, `knowledge`, имя файла хартии), а не предмет. Починка убрала служебные слова, но рамка здесь состоит из слов, ВЫГЛЯДЯЩИХ содержательными.
- **Тяжёлое следствие сегодня мертво — и мертво случайно.** Быстрый путь отдаёт сохранённый ответ дословно, но требует качества ≥ 0.70 и сходства ≥ 0.85 при измеренном качестве; измеренного качества нет **ни у одного** из 142 эпизодов, поэтому повтор не может сработать вовсе. Защита держится не на устройстве ворот, а на том, что оценка качества не работает: **как только она заработает, путь вооружится, и 0.65 между CrewAI и MetaGPT станет живым риском.**
- **Порог по-прежнему не трогаю** — решение контракта эскалации в силе. Что изменилось: провал перестал быть гипотезой, названный порог опровергнут, а безопасность названа условной, а не проектной.
- **Status:** `open` (low, но с названным условием роста severity: появление измеренного качества ответов).

### MIR-025 — user profile is sticky toward `expert`
- **Aliases:** LPF-015. **Provenance:** previously_documented.
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/user_profile.py` (`expert_signals`/`novice_signals` cumulative; "profile only moves toward a level").
- **Symptom:** after many expert signals, current beginner-level questions barely move expertise.
- **Root cause:** cumulative ratio with no decay/recency.
- **Production path:** profile update per turn.
- **Existing tests:** user_profile tests.
- **Status:** `needs_investigation` — the module **documents** this as intended ("only moves toward a level"); whether the stickiness is a defect or a design choice is a product decision → could resolve to `not_a_bug` or a decay fix.

### MIR-026 — no per-run goal/plan finalizer
- **Aliases:** LPF-016. **Provenance:** previously_documented (low severity, self-disclaimed).
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop.py` (per-run Goal/Plan objects).
- **Symptom:** ephemeral Goal stays `pending`, Plan stays `in_progress` after `[OUT] respond`.
- **Root cause:** no transition to completed/failed for the in-run log objects.
- **Production path:** in-run logging objects only.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-22 evening).
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-22: unchanged, and still correctly labelled cosmetic.** `Goal`, `Plan` and their siblings in `core/models.py` all declare `status: Literal["pending", "in_progress", "done", "failed"] = "pending"`, and neither `core/loop.py` nor `core/loop_run_tail.py` assigns `.status` anywhere. The in-run objects are therefore still born `pending` and die `pending`. Kept `open` rather than closed as not-a-bug, because the durable side (MIR-039) showed what happens when a lifecycle field is written carelessly, and an unset field is at least honestly unset.
- **Stage-3 verification (2026-07-18):** the *durable* `RuntimeTaskStore` **API** lifecycle is well-formed — `mark_running`→`mark_done`/`mark_failed`/`cancel`, plus `recover_stuck`; statuses `pending/running/done/failed/cancelled/paused` transition cleanly (verified by reading `core/task_queue.py`). So the LPF-016 claim "durable RuntimeTaskStore lifecycle is closed correctly" holds **at the store API level**. **However**, one *consumer* (`agent_tick.py`) misuses it — it marks tasks `done` regardless of the run's real outcome — which is now tracked separately as **MIR-039**. The ephemeral in-run Goal/Plan finalizer (the actual LPF-016 scope, in `core/loop.py`) was **not** re-verified this stage (planner subsystem) — remains `open` (low, cosmetic/logging).

- **CLOSURE AUDITED 2026-08-22 — the field's failure LANDED.** Rule: «a status written at one exit lies about the other paths». The attempt loop has no `return` at all, which is why the single-exit reading looked complete — but it re-raises `ModelBudgetExceeded` after saving a pause checkpoint, and that exit skipped the settle. A budget-interrupted run left `Goal`/`Plan` at `pending`, which in this vocabulary means «never started», not «interrupted» — the exact confusion this entry was opened to remove, surviving on the path that matters most for an unattended week. Settling is now a named node (`_settle_run_objects`) called from BOTH exits; the pin counts escaping `raise`s against settle calls, so a third exit added later reddens. Three structural guards had to be satisfied rather than loosened: CNS anchors (+4 lines), the loop-split guard (the insertion is declared by name in `_DeclaredInsertions`), and the node census.
### MIR-027 — assumptions may accumulate across unrelated goals
- **Aliases:** LPF-017. **Provenance:** previously_documented (partial / needs context).
- **Sources:** LIVE_PROBE_FINDINGS.md.
