N sections omitted ...]` (`core/evidence_budget.py:196, 214, 217, 242`), and nothing on the extraction path knows about any of them.
- **Why no conflict is reported today, and why that is luck rather than safety.** `conflict_source_ids` is empty on all 4347 claims, and `tests/test_conflict_source_scope.py` passes. The reason is scope: the detector compares across SOURCES, and today's truncated `If a command is not here` carries `source_id = file:knowledge/maps/COMMANDS_MAP.md`, the same source as one of its clean twins. The 08-15 red test happened because the twins then sat under *different* sources (`docs/COMMANDS_MAP.md`, `knowledge/maps/COMMANDS_MAP.md`, `memory:mem_aabd6c65…`). Both a clean and a truncated variant exist right now for two separate sentences, so the precondition is fully restored; only which copy the budget happened to trim keeps the conflict from re-forming. A green test here means "the coincidence has not recurred", not "the defect is contained".
- **Recorded against the measurement itself, because two successive searches returned a confident false zero.** The first searched for `[truncated` — but the trimmer's current markers are the `INTENT-BUDGET` family, so the string was wrong. The second searched the right strings at the wrong depth: `data/source_registry.jsonl` rows are nested **twice** (`{_integrity, payload:{kind, payload:{claim}}}`), so `row["payload"]["text"]` is `None` for every row and every pattern scored 0 of 5155. Only the third pass, at the correct nesting and with the correct markers, found the four. **An absence measured through a broken probe reads exactly like a clean store** — the same failure mode this registry records for the MIR-058 report, met here from the other side.
- **REPAIRED 2026-08-22 evening.** The grammar now lives beside the writers: `core/evidence_budget.py` `carries_framework_notice` recognises the SHAPE — an ellipsis butted against a bracket, including a trailing unclosed notice (`...[tr`, which the budget once produced by cutting its own marker in half) — and `ClaimExtractor._accept_sentence` refuses any sentence carrying it. Refused, not cleaned: a sentence interrupted by our own notice was never fully written by the source. All three closure criteria hold and are pinned by `tests/test_a_trim_notice_never_becomes_a_claim.py` (16 tests): (1) no claim from a trimmed excerpt carries a marker; (2) the live registry was cleaned of the 4 standing corrupted claims (backup `source_registry.jsonl.pre-marker-cleanup-20260822T160228Z.bak`) and this cleanup can hold because the extractor is fixed; (3) an unseen marker of the same shape — `...[SELF-DOC NOTE: 3 of 9]`, `…[CACHE: stale copy]` — is refused, verified by narrowing the grammar to a blacklist and watching 7 tests go red. Ordinary prose with ellipses and brackets (`a[i]`, «see [4]», «Он замолчал... потом продолжил») survives, and clean sentences beside a marker still extract.
- **Missing tests:** claim text is identical for the same source whether or not the turn's budget trimmed it. *(The stronger property shipped instead: a trimmed excerpt yields only clean-sentence claims, and the notice itself can never be one.)*

### MIR-098 — the input layer decides by vocabulary, and answered for the agent three times out of five
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-094 / MIR-095 (the same word-table disease inside the memory path), the R6 intercepts pinned in `tests/test_a_task_is_not_an_order_for_a_plan.py`. **Provenance:** measured live 2026-08-15 while trying to hand the agent a repair task, operator standing instruction «пусть чинит сам».
- **Files/functions:** `core/operator_intent_patterns.py:458` `_matches_implementation_plan`; `core/referent_resolver.py` (local-critique eligibility); `core/doc_routing.py:87` `_SELF_REPO_INTROSPECTION_TERMS` + `_drop_web_lookup_for_introspection`.
- **Five measured routings of one assignment, three of which never reached the agent:**

  | input | what happened | events |
  |---|---|---|
  | abstract: «разбери свою систему, найди дефекты» | resolved the referent to the OPERATOR'S OWN TEXT (`kind=user_text`, the only candidate), local critique fired, planner skipped | 0 tools |
  | concrete with a path: «в core/loop.py:554 не передан experience_block… почини» | `operator_intent -> :implementation-plan`, reason "implementation planning wording" | **8 events total, no model call** |
  | same, reworded without the word «планировщик» | reached the agent, which read both files and confirmed the defect exactly | `outcome=success` |
  | «напиши падающий тест» | planner chose `web_search 'SynthesisState tests site:tests/'` — its own tests, on the internet — **three identical times**, replan budget exhausted | file never written |
  | `:propose-repair core/loop.py` | refused correctly: "baseline tests are already green; refusing to invent a repair" | — |

- **The one-word proof.** `"Открой core/loop.py и посмотри на планировщика."` routes to `:implementation-plan`; `"Открой core/loop.py и почини строку 554."` reaches the agent. The trigger is `_has_any_loose(text, (".py",".md",".txt","\\","/")) and _has_any_loose(text, ("план","implementation","менять","тесты"))` — a path plus the substring «план», which lives inside «планировщик», the name of the agent's own node and the commonest word in any conversation about its architecture. **The more precisely the operator describes the system, the more certainly the input layer intercepts.**
- **A patch on the same line proves the class was known.** `core/operator_intent_patterns.py:471` carries: «R6 (2026-08-13, живой d322a875/Q2): „в текущей реализации“ — обстоятельство места, не заказ плана; стем „реализац“ изъят из мягкой ветки». One stem was removed thirteen days ago and «план» was left, which now catches «планировщик». Fitted to its sample; the class stayed open — the operator's unseen-form rule again.
- **The introspection guard has the same shape and the same hole.** `_drop_web_lookup_for_introspection` exists precisely to stop the agent web-searching itself, and its table holds **51 terms** («your repository», «your code», «своей памяти», «твоей архитектур»…). The assignment named `core/loop.py`, `SynthesisState`, `tests/` and «твоя машина починки» — **zero matches**. A path inside the workspace is a structural fact and needs no vocabulary; it was available and unused.
- **The failure channel reached the model and changed nothing.** `replan_context_chars` grew 0 -> 523 -> 919 across the three web_search attempts, so `<failure_context>` DID carry the prior failures into the prompt, and the planner emitted a byte-identical plan each time. Recorded because it qualifies the neighbouring diagnosis: the problem is not only that local state fails to reach the model (MIR-096's ledger) — where it does reach, it is not acted on.
- **Status:** `open`. Not repaired by the assistant: the operator's instruction of 2026-08-15 is that the agent repairs and the assistant directs. Shape of the fix stated for whoever takes it — decide routing on structural facts available in the turn (does the text name a path inside the workspace? does it name a symbol that exists in the repo?) before consulting any vocabulary, and treat the word tables as a fallback rather than the decision.
- **Re-measured 2026-08-20, tracing the input path end to end rather than one phrasing.** The order today is: a deterministic `:command` parser (correct — governance must not be probabilistic), then `decide_activity`, then `route_operator_intent`, and only then the agent's own model. Two things the original entry could not know:
  - **The reported case is fixed in code.** The soft branch now matches WHOLE WORDS (`core/operator_intent_patterns.py`), so «планировщик» no longer contains a request for a plan. Eight realistic utterances — including the exact one this entry recorded, plus «посмотри, эта память реально участвует в решении», «докажи что урок реально доехал до подсказки», «расколи commands_ingest по четырём швам» — were probed: **0 of 8 intercepted**, all fall through to the model.
  - **The layer is not a pure keyword sieve.** Of 24 intent kinds, **13 are subject to a model VETO** (`_model_says_conversation`): a lexical match must survive the model calling it conversation. Live traffic: the lexical route fired 10 times and the model overturned it 3 — the semantic check is load-bearing, not decorative.
- **What remains, stated as measurement rather than accusation.** **11 of 24 kinds dispatch on a lexical match alone**, with no semantic step — the explicit imperatives (`implementation_plan`, `patch_proposal`, `self_build_request`, `source_review_plan`, `capability_request`, `architecture_audit`…). In live traffic **5 of the 10 dispatches were those ungated kinds**, so the model never saw those utterances. Whether any of the five was wrong is **UNKNOWN**: no ground truth was labelled, and the MIR-015 distinction applies here too — a firing count is not an error count.
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-22 AGAINST A GENUINELY UNSEEN SAMPLE, AND MOST OF THIS ENTRY IS NOW REPAIRED — the first entry in this sweep to have moved forward rather than back.** The sample was chosen so it could not have been fitted to: **twelve of the operator's own real utterances from the session of 2026-08-22**, none of which existed when any of this code was written («разберись почему self-build постоянно падает», «запиши эти три дыры в реестр», «разбирай класс AutonomousRuntime», …). **One of twelve was intercepted** — «окно одобрения» routed to `approval_status`, which in context was an assignment to work on the approval window rather than a request for its status. The two probes this entry was opened on now fall through: «посмотри на планировщика в core/loop.py» and «в core/loop.py:554 не передан experience_block, почини».
- **The "11 of 24 ungated kinds" remainder is CLOSED.** Commit `6011afa` ("lexis proposes, the model adjudicates — the last 11 free-text intents gated") landed after this entry was re-measured. Counted today: `cli/intent_bridge.py` `_VERIFY_INTENTS` holds **23 of the 24 kinds the router can emit**. The single exception is `shell_command_hint`, which dispatches no work at all — it prints an advisory that the text looks like a shell command. `approval_status` is inside the gated set, so even the one interception above is subject to the model's veto rather than final.
- **The introspection guard now decides on a STRUCTURAL fact, which is exactly the fix shape this entry prescribed.** Probed directly: the entry's own failing assignment — the one naming `SynthesisState`, `tests/` and `core/loop.py`, which matched **zero** of the 51 terms when this was written — is **guarded today**, as are a bare `посмотри core/smart_memory.py` and a path carrying a repair verb. The vocabulary controls («расскажи про свою архитектуру», «что у тебя в своей памяти») still match, so the term table was extended rather than replaced.
- **What is still open, stated exactly.** The fix shape named two structural facts: *does the text name a path inside the workspace*, and *does it name a symbol that exists in the repo*. **The path half is done; the symbol half is not.** Measured: «что делает `AutonomousQueueRunReport`» — a real class in this repository, named without any path — is **not guarded**, so a plan for it may still reach for the public web. Closure criterion 2 is therefore half-satisfied. Criteria 1 and 3 are satisfied for the routing layer on the unseen sample above; criterion 2's planner half (a question naming a repo path never PLANS `web_search`) was not re-run live here, because that needs a model call and the operator's money.
- **Closure criterion:** 1. an assignment naming a workspace path is never routed to a plan command or to local critique; 2. a question naming a repo path or symbol never plans `web_search`; 3. an unseen phrasing of each is stopped — the fix may not be fitted to «планировщик» or to the 51 terms.
- **Missing tests:** «посмотри на планировщика» + a path stays a task; a self-question naming a repo path does not reach web_search.
- **Half 3, measured and NOT shipped: the agent cannot cite a line at all.** `file_read` returns bare text, so every line number it gives is counted by eye — live, it answered 164 and 382 where the truth was 385 and 450, and nothing catches it because the `[file:<path>]` citation resolves whatever number rides on it (MIR-060). `core.answer_format.number_lines` was built to fix this, including the hard half (an excerpt keeps its addresses in the FILE, not in the excerpt). Wiring it was tried three ways — before the budget, after it with recovered indices, and only for untrimmed files — and each attempt turned the same integration tests red, always with long-term memory losing what it had: the gutter costs ~8.6% on `core/loop.py` (35342 -> 38386) and memory is spent first by design (MIR-092, measured 822 -> 0). **Left unwired on purpose:** buying an address column with the agent's own recollection is the wrong trade while MIR-096 is open. Pinned by `tests/test_the_model_can_see_line_numbers.py`, whose third test asserts the NON-wiring so the decision is a test rather than a memory.
### MIR-099 — the oversized-module sensor measures file length, and 5 of its 11 current verdicts rest on prose
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-015 (the other sensor whose output was read as a defect count before anyone measured the sensor). **Provenance:** measured 2026-08-20 after the agent's first self-chosen engineering proposal — a split of `core/self_task_producer.py` — and the operator's observation that «в каждом файле дохрена писанины».
- **Files/functions:** `core/backlog_signals.py` `oversized_module_candidates()` — `line_count = content.count("\n") + 1`, compared against `_OVERSIZED_MODULE_MIN_LINES = 800`. No parsing, no distinction between code, comment, docstring or blank.
- **The measurement, in the two quantities it actually has** (the first draft of this entry conflated them, and the operator caught it on 2026-08-20 — the correction is kept here because the conflation is the same flattery this entry is about). Census over 270 modules in `core/`, `tools/`, `app/`, `cli/`, counting a docstring line or a PURE comment line as prose and a line carrying both code and a trailing comment as CODE (a looser rule inflates prose and was rejected for that reason). Repo-wide prose is 17621 of 89739 lines, 19 %. Eleven files are at or over the limit today.
  - **Under code-token lines** (prose AND blanks removed): **5 of 11 fall below 800** — `doc_routing` 970→727, `shell_exec` 964→533, `loop_step_execution` 955→673, `knowledge_pipeline` 932→601, `step_sanitizer` 837→678. This is the firm result: the sensor's verdict changes for five files when you count what executes.
  - **Under `total − prose` with blank lines LEFT IN** — the counterfactual that isolates prose alone — **4 of 11 fall below**: `shell_exec` 620, `loop_step_execution` 744, `knowledge_pipeline` 712, `step_sanitizer` 728. `doc_routing` is the discriminating case: 970 − 159 = **811, still over**. Its verdict is decided by blank lines, not by prose.
  - Saying «5 of 11 rest on prose» would therefore be one file too generous. Prose alone is decisive for four; a fifth needs whitespace counted too. The six survivors are genuinely large — `smart_memory` is 1929 lines, 1244 without prose, 1028 of code.
- **The specimen proves it on itself, and here prose alone IS decisive.** `core/self_task_producer.py`, the target the agent picked unprompted, was 870 lines with 165 of prose: `870 − 165 = 705`, below the threshold with blanks still counted, and **602 lines of code**. Either way it was never oversized. The 2026-08-20 trim removed 79 physical lines — **63 of prose and 16 blank, and zero of code: 602 before, 602 after.** (The first telling of this said «79 lines of prose», which is wrong by the same sloppiness this entry documents.) The signal that produced the proposal was measuring explanations written in the margins.
- **The proposal it produced was denied on 2026-08-20T04:06Z**, not left to expire: with the file now at 791 physical lines the grounding condition no longer holds even under the unmodified sensor, and a pending item blocks every later self-build attempt (`self_build_proposal_skipped: pending proposal exists`, measured on the 00:31Z tick). The specimen survives in `logs/`, the episodic journal and this entry; the denial reason is the first negative verdict to reach `data/approval_outcomes.jsonl`.
- **What this does NOT establish.** That the six survivors are correctly flagged: `code_lines > 800` is a second proxy, not a measure of architectural complexity, and nothing here says a 1028-line module needs splitting. Nor does it establish a replacement threshold — the honest reading is that the sensor's proxy is lossy in a direction that was never checked, in about half of its live verdicts.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-22 evening). The ruling's precondition — establish the correlation first — was satisfied by the 275-module census the same day (r = 0.971, and the wrong-statistic finding), and the repair honours its other half: the threshold NUMBER is untouched (800), and no comment was deleted anywhere — the sensor changed what it counts, not what the code contains.
- **THE OPERATOR'S RULED QUESTION IS NOW ANSWERED — measured 2026-08-22, and the answer is two-sided.** His ruling was to establish "насколько total LOC действительно коррелирует с тем, что вы хотите считать переросшим модулем" before touching anything. Census re-run over **275 modules** in `core/`, `tools/`, `app/`, `cli/` under the same rule as 2026-08-20 (docstring or pure-comment line is prose; a line with code and a trailing comment is code):
  - **Pearson r(total, code) = 0.971.** The correlation is strong. Total LOC is, globally, a good predictor of code LOC — the sensor's proxy is not arbitrary.
  - **And that does not rescue it. Of the 10 modules flagged today, 5 fall below 800 when only code is counted** — `loop_step_execution` 960→674, `doc_routing` 933→727, `knowledge_pipeline` 894→601, `shell_exec` 848→533, `step_sanitizer` 820→678. Half the live verdicts still flip, at r = 0.971.
  - **Why both are true at once, and this is the part worth keeping.** A correlation describes the whole cloud; this sensor makes a **threshold cut**, and what governs a cut is the spread of the residual AT the cut, not the global fit. Prose share among modules of 500+ lines ranges from **1% to 38%** (median 15%), so near the boundary the residual is worth several hundred lines — more than enough to move a verdict. **A high correlation is the wrong statistic for a threshold instrument**, and this entry now records that as its finding rather than as an opinion.
- **A new fact the earlier census did not report: the errors are one-directional.** Zero modules have `total < 800` while `code >= 800`. The sensor therefore has **no false negatives** — it over-flags and never under-flags. That materially changes what a repair must protect: nothing is hiding from it, so any fix is about suppressing noise, not about widening coverage, and a fix that lowered the threshold to "catch more" would be solving a problem that does not exist.
- **Population drift since 2026-08-20, recorded so the two censuses can be compared rather than confused:** 11 flagged then, **10 now**; repo-wide prose 19% then, **17%** now over 275 modules (was 270). `smart_memory` has come down 1929 → 1664 (1036 of code) and is still over. The three largest are now `model_router` 1777 (1281 code), `self_build_producer` 1743 (1272), `smart_memory` 1664 (1036) — the same three that `SPLIT_ONE_SHOT_MAX_LINES = 1500` puts permanently beyond self-build's reach (`docs/audit/SELF_BUILD_FAILURE_ANALYSIS.md` §6). `core/autonomous_runtime.py` remains flagged at 1073 (824 code) even after its split.
- **CLOSED 2026-08-22 evening — the ruled precondition was met by the census, and the closure criterion is implemented verbatim.** `oversized_module_candidates` now counts CODE lines (`_code_line_count`: the census rule — docstring or pure-comment line is prose, code plus trailing comment is code), the quote names BOTH quantities («NNN code lines of NNN total … docstrings and comments excluded»), and an unparseable module falls back to total lines with «unparseable» stated in its quote — the sensor must not go blind. Live effect: **10 flagged → 5**, exactly the five the census called genuinely large; the one-directional-error measurement guarantees nothing is newly missed. The pin file that held the OLD behaviour on the operator's ruling (`tests/test_the_size_sensor_counts_lines_not_code.py`) is repointed to the new rule with the ruling's history kept in its docstring; six tests, break-tested by restoring the total-lines cut.
- **Closure criterion (met, kept for the record):** the sensor states which quantity it measures and why that quantity is the one that matters, and a candidate that is large only in prose is either not emitted or emitted with its composition named in the quote.
- **Missing tests:** a module of 900 lines of which 400 are docstrings does not produce the same signal as a module of 900 lines of code.
- **Reproduce:** the census script lives outside the tree on purpose (a measurement, not an organ); its rule is one function — docstring ranges from `ast`, comment lines from `tokenize`, minus any line that also carries a code token.

### MIR-100 — a signal becomes work without anything asking whether it is true
- **Provenance:** the operator named this metaclass on 2026-08-20 after reading the registry entry by entry (stated in the Aliases line below; moved into the field so the record answers "where did this come from" mechanically).
- **Aliases:** the metaclass the operator named on 2026-08-20 after reading the registry entry by entry: «существует механизм → его output принимают за знание о мире → позже выясняется, что сам измеритель смотрит не на ту величину». **Related, as members of that class rather than as neighbours:** MIR-015 (`reasoning keyword miss ≠ reasoning/action mismatch`), MIR-050 (`same tool sequence ≠ same procedure`), MIR-058 (`standing increment ≠ earned reputation`), MIR-060 (`citation resolves ≠ claim follows`), MIR-074 (`memory injected ≠ memory helped`), MIR-096 (`episode stored ≠ lesson recallable`), MIR-098 (`failure_context present ≠ planner reacts`), MIR-099 (`LOC > 800 ≠ architecture is bad`).
- **The question this entry answers.** Not «can the agent notice something about itself» — 2026-08-19 it did, unprompted. The next one: does anything stand between what it noticed and the work it proposes? Measured structurally by enumerating every gate on both roads from a backlog signal to an approval item.
- **Files/functions:** `core/self_build_producer.py` (docstring gate list, `_default_grounded_selector`, `critic_veto`, `value_veto`); `core/self_task_producer.py` (`task_veto`, the AST sieves); `core/campaign_io.py:451` `_propose_failing_test_from_diagnosis`, `_propose_repair_from_diagnosis`.
- **Every gate that exists falls into three kinds, and none of them is the missing one.**
  1. *Authority and safety* — budget kill-switch, hour budget, one approval in flight, dirty working tree, `CRITICAL_DENY`, path hygiene (config/, secrets, lockfiles). These ask «may I act», never «is this true».
  2. *Grounding* — the signal's quote must appear verbatim in the source text it claims to come from, and the target must resolve to a real file. This is an anti-hallucination check: it proves **the evidence exists**, not that the evidence **means** a problem.
  3. *Product quality* — `critic_veto`, `value_veto` (a patch with no effect), and Stage A's three AST sieves (tautological assert, phantom kwarg, a test that names no code token of the diagnosis). Every one of these judges the artefact the hands produced, downstream of the premise being accepted.
- **There is exactly ONE premise gate in the whole system, and it is built on the instrument of MIR-060.** `_propose_repair_from_diagnosis` refuses unless `verified_chunks == examined_chunks` on the answer's own verification — the diagnosis must be fully verified before it may become work. That counter is precisely the one MIR-060 measured on live traffic within a single hour: a FALSE claim about `_run_synthesizer_ladder` scored `3 of 3 verified` at confidence 0.836 because its quoted strings existed in the file, while a TRUE claim that nothing would make the agent read `knowledge/corrections.md` scored `0 of 5` at 0.028 because an absence has no line to cite.
- **The consequence is worse than a gap, because the asymmetry is aimed at self-knowledge.** The single premise gate systematically rewards claims with convenient citations and punishes true statements about absences — and «there is no mechanism that does X» is the exact shape of an agent's most valuable finding about itself. MIR-096 is that shape. So the one road that does check its premise cannot promote the class of premise that matters most, and every other road does not check at all.
- **MIR-099 is this entry's live specimen.** The evidence existed verbatim (`870 lines >= 800`), grounding passed by construction, and the premise — that the module is oversized — was never tested by anything. It was false: 602 of those lines were code.
- **Status:** `open`. Nothing repaired: naming the missing edge is not the same as knowing where it belongs, and the operator's standing method is that a measurement is banked before a fix is designed.
- **Deliberately NOT pinned by a failing test, and this is the interesting part.** Every invariant available to me goes green for the wrong reason: «the producer gains a status meaning the premise was rejected» is satisfied by any new status at all, and «some road consults a premise check» is satisfied by a check that consults the MIR-060 verifier and inherits its asymmetry. A bank that can be closed by an unrelated change is worse than no bank. What IS pinned, in `tests/test_a_signal_becomes_work_without_a_premise_check.py`, is the single gate that does exist, so it cannot quietly disappear or be widened while this entry is open.
- **Closure criterion:** a signal that survives grounding is subjected to a check that can call it false on grounds other than a resolvable citation, and a true claim about an absence can pass that check.

- **MEASURED 2026-08-23 on the live approval inbox — the class now has a number.** The entry established structurally that no gate asks «is this signal true». The consequence is visible in what happened to work that signals produced, because the operator's refusals ARE that missing gate, applied by a human after the fact:

  | operation | requests | denied | executed | denial rate |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | `self_build_task.approve` — the agent's own chosen build | 5 | 3 | **0** | **60%** (the other 2 expired) |
  | `self_apply_lane.run` — the agent's own patch | 27 | 15 | 10 | **55%** |
  | `autonomous_runtime.allow_effects` — permission for a human-directed run | 104 | 20 | 82 | **19%** |

- **The three-fold gap is the finding.** Work the agent chose for itself is refused at 55–60%; permission for work a human directed is refused at 19%. Whatever the missing gate would have caught, a human is catching roughly half the time — and **not one self-chosen build proposal has ever been executed**.
- **Second finding, and it closes the loop the wrong way: all 38 denials carry no reason.** `decision_reason` is empty on every one. The single highest-signal feedback the agent receives — the operator saying no — is recorded as a status change with no content, so nothing can learn the shape of what gets refused. MIR-096's «failure → procedure» closure and this are the same wound seen from two sides.
- **What this means for an unattended week.** Nobody will deny anything, so the 55–60% filter is simply absent; proposals will accumulate and expire on the inbox TTL (3 days). The week will not produce bad work — it will produce unfiltered proposals, and no record of why any of them was wrong.
- **Status:** `open`, unchanged in kind, now carrying the measurement the entry was missing. The fix is not a new gate invented here: the cheapest true step is to make a denial carry its reason, which turns the human filter into data the agent can learn from — and that is a prerequisite for any automatic gate, not a substitute for one.

### MIR-132 — the router has no notion of provider health, and retried a dead provider 391 times
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-124 (model retirement — the other way a provider stops answering), MIR-120 (money walls), MIR-131 (the same shape: nothing observes its own state and adapts). **Provenance:** found 2026-08-22 while measuring context growth for MIR-122; the provider column was read on the way past. Measured on `data/model_usage.jsonl`, not reasoned about.
- **The finding.** Anthropic calls returned `BadRequestError 400` with the literal message *"Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API"* **391 consecutive times across four days** (08-14: 19, 08-15: 226, 08-16: 97, 08-17: 49), with **zero successes** in that window. On 08-15 that was 226 failures against 239 successes — nearly half of all model attempts went to a provider that had already told us, 225 times, that it could not answer. Nothing adapted. `grep` for a circuit breaker, consecutive-failure counter, provider cooldown or health flag across `core/model_router.py` and `core/llm.py` returns nothing.
- **The severity is LOW, and saying so is part of the finding.** Three measurements bound it, and each one cuts against the alarming headline: (1) **the fallback works** — of 215 runs containing an Anthropic failure, **214** had the same role succeed later on OpenAI; exactly one lost its work; (2) **no money burned** — `cost_units` charged on the 393 failures totals **0**; (3) **the time cost is 291 seconds total**, 4.8 minutes across three weeks, because a 400 comes back fast (median 332 ms). A raw count of 391 failures reads like a catastrophe and is not one.
- **What is nevertheless wrong, and why it should not be dismissed.** The cheapness is a property of HOW this provider fails, not of our design. The same 393 calls include one that took **152 seconds**. A provider that hangs or times out instead of refusing quickly would cost ~150 s per attempt under identical code, because there is nothing in the router that could notice 200 consecutive failures and stop asking. The router selects on model capability and role defaults; it has no input at all from what happened last time. **Presence of an API key is treated as availability of the provider** — the key exists, the balance does not, and only the provider knows the difference.
- **Live status of the three configured providers, since it bears on any unattended run:** OpenAI answers (funded). Anthropic answers only with the credit-balance refusal. DeepSeek is configured and, on the operator's standing instruction, must not be enabled until he says the balance is topped up. So two of three routes are decorative, and the router cannot tell.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-22 evening, on the operator's «берись за ключи» ordering). The shape this entry deferred is built with the §9 boundary kept: `ModelUsageLedger.provider_unhealthy` derives health from the ledger the router already writes (bounded tail read — the MIR-125 lesson applied at birth), and `UsageTrackedLLM` consults it before the FIRST call of a fresh proxy, switching pre-emptively with the grounds recorded in `route_reason`. A cooldown, never a ban: past the window the provider is probed again; one success heals; only the durable key-class demotes (rate limits fail over per-call but never demote — the switch vocabulary was split into transient and durable, the durable tuple owned beside the ledger so the two rules cannot drift). Fail open on missing history. Pinned by `tests/test_the_router_remembers_a_dead_provider.py` (9 tests), both halves break-tested red. Full account: docs/CODE_NOTES.md, «A key is not a balance».

### MIR-131 — the agent owns its maintenance organs and only a human can move them
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-044 and MIR-045 (two instances of this class, filed separately before the class was seen), MIR-090 (whose fix is one of the unreachable ones), MIR-125 (the growth these would have contained). **Provenance:** swept 2026-08-22 at the operator's framing — that leaving these deferred yields "не автономный, а полуавтономный инвалид". Measured by AST over `core/`, `cli/`, `tools/`, then hand-checked, because the first sweep was wrong (see below).
- **The measurement.** Of the maintenance actions in `core/` (verb-prefixed: prune / collapse / dedupe / archive / expire / summarise / consolidate / compact / rotate / recover / reconcile), **13 have no caller anywhere except a command module**, and one more (`summarise_ledger`) has no production caller at all:

      archive_low_value_memory   archive_persistent      cleanup_backups
      collapse_duplicate_episodes  dedupe_episodic       dedupe_persistent
      expire_memory              expire_persistent       prune_episodic
      prune_stale                reconcile_value_reviews summarise_memory
      summarise_persistent

- **The subtlety that hides it, and it is the reason this was filed twice as separate issues rather than once as a class.** Most of these are methods of the agent loop: `core/loop.py:52` composes `AgentLoopHygiene` in as a mixin, so they READ as part of the cycle. They are not. Nothing in the loop's own path invokes any of them — verified by searching the cycle for calls to its own hygiene methods and finding none. The call comes from outside: `cli/commands_memory.py:81` reaches into the object and invokes `agent.prune_episodic(...)`. **Belonging to the loop is not the same as being on the loop's path**, and the mixin makes the two indistinguishable to a reader.
- **What it has already cost, measured rather than projected.** On the unattended run of 2026-08-16 the episodic store accumulated 43 byte-identical `approval_wait` episodes as a single duplicate group. `collapse_duplicate_episodes` — written for exactly that, under MIR-090 — is in the list above. Nobody typed a command, so it never ran. The store now sits at its 200-row ceiling with 63% of rows protected and unevictable, and the one mechanism that could relieve it is reachable only from a keyboard. **The autonomous path is the path that generates the most repeats and the only path that cannot clean up after itself.** *(2026-08-22 evening: the 43-group itself was drained — writer fixed, rows demoted and collapsed via `scripts/demote_gate_wait_lessons.py`, store 200 → 142. That closes the INSTANCE. The CLASS this entry records — thirteen maintenance actions reachable only from a keyboard — is untouched and stays open.)*
- **A counter-example in the same codebase, which is why this is a wiring defect and not a design opinion.** `ApprovalInbox.expire_stale()` enforces a 24-hour TTL and is called **on every read of the inbox** (`core/approval_inbox.py:225`), with its own comment stating the purpose: to keep the inbox from accumulating stale items "when the operator goes offline". It fired 6 times on the live data, including the item created 2026-08-16T17:30 that aborted exactly 24h later. So this repository already contains a proven, in-house pattern for autonomous maintenance — an action invoked on every read of the thing it maintains. It was applied to approvals and not to memory. The fix shape does not need to be imported from anywhere.
- **A correction to this entry's own first draft, recorded because the error is instructive.** The initial sweep reported 6 actions "called by nothing at all". Five of those were an artefact of the method: it excluded same-file call sites, so `expire_stale`, `compact_summary`, `summarise_incidents`, `archive_record` and `compact_assumptions` were all wrongly labelled orphans while having real callers. Had that number been reported it would have been confidently false, and it would have accused the correctly-wired TTL of being dead code. Only `summarise_ledger` is genuinely uncalled in production.
- **Status:** `open`, narrowed 2026-08-22 evening: **one of the thirteen is wired** — `collapse_duplicate_episodes` now runs from the tick's startup slot (`agent_tick._sweep_episodic_duplicates`, journaled, best-effort, pinned by `tests/test_the_tick_sweeps_its_own_duplicates.py`). The line was drawn on judgement: duplicate collapse is mechanical (byte-identical only, keeps newest, loses nothing), while the other twelve decide which memories are WORTH keeping — the MIR-128 resolver-seat hazard — and those remain the operator's, exactly as this entry said. An automatic pruner that exercises judgement unattended is still the §9 class; a sweep that removes exact copies is not.

- **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-23, and the original count was wrong in two directions.**
- **Four of the thirteen are not organs but IMPLEMENTATIONS behind their own command wrapper:** `expire_memory`, `prune_stale`, `summarise_memory` and `archive_low_value_memory` are called by `core/memory_hygiene_commands.py`, which is the layer that exposes them. «Only a command module calls it» is correct layering there, not a severed nerve. Counting a wrapped implementation as an unreachable organ inflated the finding.
- **Five are now ON the autonomous path**, which they were not when this was written: `run_maintenance_pass` (invoked every tick, gated by `AGENT_AUTO_HYGIENE`) drives `expire_persistent`, `dedupe_persistent`, `prune_episodic`, `archive_persistent` and `compact_assumptions`; and `collapse_duplicate_episodes` — **the one with the measured cost, the 43 identical `approval_wait` episodes of 2026-08-16** — is now called from `agent_tick.py` directly. The class this entry names has been shrinking, not standing still.
- **Genuinely keyboard-only still:** `dedupe_episodic`, `cleanup_backups`, `reconcile_value_reviews`, `summarise_ledger`, and `summarise_persistent` — the last deliberately, because it needs an LLM call and `memory_hygiene_commands` says so in its own docstring.
- **The subtlety the entry identified stands and is worth keeping:** the hygiene mixin makes «belongs to the loop» and «is on the loop's path» indistinguishable to a reader. That is what made the first count wrong in both directions at once.
- **Status:** `open`, restated with the corrected measurement. Field frame: the always-on survey (arXiv 2606.30306) puts it exactly — the literature covers accumulating and retrieving state and not **governing, recovering or relinquishing** it, and an organ only a human can move is state the agent cannot relinquish.

### MIR-130 — nothing checks whether the agent answers the same question the same way twice
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-115/121 (what enters memory), MIR-119 (self-judgement). **Provenance:** gap named by the operator 2026-08-22; the field's approaches searched at his instruction rather than guessed at.
- **The gap.** Nothing here compares the agent's answer to the same question across contexts or samples. That matters because the two failure modes the operator asked about are **different objects and are caught differently**: a hallucination is knowledge the model does not have and states anyway — it is CONSISTENTLY wrong; a lie is knowledge the model has and does not express under some objective — it is a CONTEXT-DEPENDENT INCONSISTENCY, right in one framing and wrong in another. Source comparison catches the first. Only cross-context comparison catches the second, and we do neither for consistency.
- **What the field does, so this is not invented here.** The SelfCheckGPT family samples the same query several times under varied decoding and measures agreement — by NLI entailment/contradiction, QA consistency, BERTScore, or n-gram probability — on the premise that factual answers stay stable under randomness while fabricated ones vary. A question-based variant paraphrases the question instead of resampling, and checks whether the answer survives rephrasing.
- **The limit that must be recorded with the method, or we would trust it too far.** Self-consistent errors: models repeatedly produce the SAME wrong answer across samples, and the literature reports that all four detector families struggle badly on exactly those. So a consistency check catches variable invention and misses confident wrongness — which is this repository's own "same answer, different cause" rule wearing new clothes. Any such detector must be scoped accordingly on the day it is built.
- **Status:** `open`. Gap and known approaches recorded; nothing built.

### MIR-129 — memory stores facts and not the connections between them
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-128 (the same store), MIR-122 (context growth). **Provenance:** gap named by the operator 2026-08-22; approaches searched, not invented.
- **The gap.** Episodes are records; nothing represents the relation BETWEEN two records. So an old episode and a new one that concern the same file, the same defect or the same decision are connected only by whatever a similarity search happens to surface, and a contradiction between them is never resolved — both simply sit there.
- **What the field does.** A temporal knowledge graph (Zep/Graphiti is the reference implementation) extracts entities and relations from each episode and stores them as graph edges with **bi-temporal** validity: four timestamps per edge — when the system learned it and when it stopped believing it (`t_created`, `t_expired`), and when it was true in the world (`t_valid`, `t_invalid`). On a contradiction the old edge is **invalidated, not deleted**, by setting its `t_invalid` to the new edge's `t_valid`. History stays readable; the current belief is unambiguous.
- **The convergence worth noting.** That design also insists on **episode-level provenance** for every edge — which is the axis this project built on 2026-08-22 (`grounds`: operator_goal / observed_state / retained_record). We arrived at the same requirement from the WHO DECIDES side rather than the memory side.
- **Status:** `open`. Recorded with its known shape; nothing built. Whether a graph belongs here at all is an operator decision — it is a new capability, not a repair.

### MIR-128 — memory has a window but no consolidation
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-115/121 (what may enter), MIR-129 (connections), the 200-row FIFO measured earlier. **Provenance:** gap named by the operator 2026-08-22 in his own terms — old records, new records, and records "just lying there, unclear what for, but maybe useful later". Field approaches searched at his instruction.
- **The gap.** Admission is guarded (usage eligibility, the lesson axes, MIR-115/121) and capacity is bounded (FIFO at 200, protected tags exempt). Between those two there is **nothing**: a new episode never updates an older one, a contradiction between two episodes is never resolved, and no record is ever strengthened by being useful. The store grows and truncates; it does not consolidate.
- **The named consequence, from the field:** *an agent that remembers everything remembers nothing useful.*
- **What the field does.** A two-phase pipeline — extraction, then update. The update phase retrieves semantically similar existing memories and an LLM resolver decides one of a small set of operations per incoming fact: add it, update an existing record, mark an old one superseded, or do nothing. Conflicts are marked invalid rather than erased so the history stays inspectable. Separately, retrieval is used as a signal: a memory that gets retrieved and used has its relevance boosted and its decay timer reset, so usefulness is discovered by USE rather than predicted at write time.
- **That last mechanism is the direct answer to the operator's question** about a record "lying there, unclear what for, but perhaps useful later": it neither lives forever nor is discarded on a guess — it fades unless something reaches for it, and reaching for it makes it stronger.
- **The caution to carry.** Every consolidation design in the field puts an LLM in the resolver seat, deciding add/update/supersede. Under C0.P that is a judgement about the agent's own experience — interior territory — but under MIR-119's anti-requirements it is also the shape most vulnerable to self-preference. A resolver that decides which of its own memories survive is the same hazard as a judge scoring its own work, and would need the same discipline: pre-registered expectation, independent differential, never the author's opinion of its own record.
- **MEASURED THE SAME DAY, because the field's premise had to be checked here before anything was built on it (`docs/audit/MEMORY_CONSOLIDATION_MEASUREMENT.md`).** The live store sits at **exactly 200 of 200** — eviction is happening now, not hypothetically. **76 of the 200 rows (38%) add no new `(question, summary)` pair**, which is the measured ceiling of what consolidation could recover. And the measurement killed the obvious implementation: 151 rows (75%) repeat a QUESTION, but one group holds ten rows with ten different answers, so deduplicating by question label would have destroyed real experience while reporting a large win. Consolidation here must key on content, never on the label.
- **Side finding, possibly larger than this entry** *(partly addressed 2026-08-22 evening: the wait-state share of this population was demoted and deduplicated, `ae7134a`; the genuine-attempt rows remain)*. 83 rows — 42% of the whole store — are `self-build-produce`, carrying 19 distinct summaries and outcomes of 65 `partial` / 10 `failed` / 8 `success`. The store is not filling from general growth: one loop that mostly fails is consuming it, and distinct lessons elsewhere are evicted to hold another copy of its failure. Consolidation would compress that symptom; it would not touch the cause.
- **ЦЕНА ПРОБЕЛА ИЗМЕРЕНА 2026-08-25, и она уже уплачена.** «Между допуском и потолком нет ничего» — сегодня это стоило улики: уборка вынесла эпизоды, на которые опираются счётчики процедур. Счёт из отчёта MIR-058: **47 ссылок на эпизоды, которых больше нет**, и `tools:file_read` держит уверенность **0,962** на 31 исчезнувшем эпизоде.
- **Связь ВЫЧИСЛЯЕТСЯ и никем не используется.** `consolidate_memory` собирает `linked_episode_ids` — ровно те эпизоды, что подпирают процедуры (`core/smart_memory.py:1637`), — и это значение существует только в отчёте: потребителя вне отчёта нет ни одного. А уборка о процедурах не знает вовсе: слова «procedur» нет ни в `core/memory_hygiene.py`, ни в `core/loop_hygiene.py`.
- **Значит убыло не по окну.** Хранилище опустилось с 200 до 142 строк, то есть сработал не FIFO (он держит 200), а проход обслуживания. Пробел этой записи проявился не там, где его ждали: не переполнением, а вычищением подпирающего.
- **Цена очевидного шага, посчитанная заранее.** Защитить цитируемые эпизоды от уборки значит удержать сегодня 47 строк, которые она сочла лишними, — то есть обменять место на прослеживаемость. Это решение о политике памяти и остаётся за оператором, как и вся эта запись.
- **Status:** `open`. Gap, field approaches, and a measurement of what the gap actually costs here. Nothing built and no design chosen — under the freeze that is an operator decision.

### MIR-127 — the source-worth table is a utility ranking, and utility ranking is ratified as the agent's
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-119 (its downstream twin), MIR-114/WHO DECIDES, C0 quoted half, C0.P. **Provenance:** decision-authority census run 1, family A, 2026-08-22 — `docs/audit/DECISION_AUTHORITY_CENSUS_RUN_1.md`.
- **The finding.** `core/source_ranker.py:53` `_TIER_SCORES` assigns epistemic worth by source kind — authoritative 1.00, primary 0.95, reputable 0.82, general web 0.68, memory 0.55, blog 0.50, search pointer 0.30, **llm 0.20**, unknown 0.10 — and `:65` `_FRESHNESS_SCORES` does the same by age (fresh 1.00, dated 0.75, stale 0.45, undated 0.35). Kin at `core/backlog_selector.py:43` and `core/source_connectors.py:361`.
- **Why it is a vacancy candidate rather than an observation.** The operator-ratified quoted half of C0 says hardcoded code must not prescribe **utility ranking**; this is a utility ranking in its plainest form. Under ratified C0.P the agent decides what to attend to and what to study, and how much a kind of source is worth *to its own learning* is a judgement inside that interior territory. So it stands to MIR-119 as the upstream twin: MIR-119 is the missing verdict AFTER a change, this is the missing valuation BEFORE a study.
- **Not claimed.** That the numbers are wrong (they look sensible), that they should be removed, or that removal is safe: the table is a defence against learning from junk, and a bad replacement is worse than a developer's reasonable defaults. Only that the authorship is a developer's where a ratified criterion places it with the agent. Causal measurement not yet run — this is a family-A hand classification, a hypothesis until perturbed.
- **Counter-finding from the same run, recorded here so the census is not read as a removal list:** `core/instruction_conflict_gate.py:56` `AUTHORITY_RANK` (`operator: 1` above task contract, tests, repo invariants, conventions, advisors) fired on the same detector and was refused by the classifier as **Constitution** — it is the machinery keeping the human's word above the agent's. A careless pass hunting for tables to strip would take it. It stays, and the reason is written down.
- **DEFAULT UNDER THE ESCALATION CONTRACT (2026-08-22, `docs/audit/ESCALATION_CONTRACT.md`):** the developer's table STANDS as the floor — it is a defence against learning from junk, and the entry itself records that a bad replacement is worse than reasonable defaults. The vacancy (whose valuation is it) is NOT filled by this default: under the contract a vacancy the ratified criterion assigns to the agent's interior is earned through the C0.P machinery, not decided by the assistant or extracted from the operator. Reversed by one word from the operator.
- **Status:** `open`. Measurement and classification only; what to do with the vacancy belongs to the operator.

### MIR-126 — nine swallowed exceptions, individually reasonable, collectively unaudited
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-117 (a cousin: state that hides what happened). **Provenance:** full-codebase sweep 2026-08-22 against the field's documented builder-error classes; grep verified, sites listed.
- **The class.** "Silent failure": `except: pass` makes a subsystem degrade without a trace, and the operator learns about it from consequences instead of logs. The field treats unaudited swallowing as an observability black hole.
- **Measured here.** Nine sites where a broad `except` ends in `pass`: `app/budget_guard.py:136,:230,:269`, `app/daemon_notice.py:51`, `cli/commands_approval.py:430`, `cli/commands_ingest.py:51`, `cli/commands_self_apply.py:89`, `cli/commands_value_review.py:106`, `cli/repl.py:167`, `core/approval_inbox.py:258`. At least one is deliberate and documented (`_record_outcome`: a write failure must not block a verdict, and says so). **Three sit in `app/budget_guard.py` — a money-adjacent module is the worst place for silent degradation.** The gap is not any single site; it is that no census says which are deliberate-and-documented and which are accidents.
- **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-22 BY AST, AND THIS ENTRY IS WRONG ON BOTH HALVES — the count and the claim that no census exists.**
  - **The count.** A grep for `except … pass` misses any handler written across two lines. Parsed instead: **68 handlers whose only body is `pass`** across `core/`, `cli/`, `app/`, `tools/`, `api/`, `scripts/` and the repo root — not 9. A further **54** have a body of only `continue`, which is the same silence with a different keyword. Of the 9 sites this entry lists, 8 still match; `core/approval_inbox.py:258` no longer does (that file now has silent handlers at `:216`, `:278`, `:347`).
  - **The claim.** This entry says "no census says which are deliberate-and-documented and which are accidents". **That census exists and has existed since MIR-077: `scripts/except_audit.py`.** It classifies every broad handler six ways — `journaled`, `log_guard`, `reraise`, `silent_default_return`, `silent_noop`, `silent_other` — and reports today: **190 broad handlers, 28 `silent_noop`, 39 `silent_default_return`, 35 `silent_other`, and 0 silent WITHOUT a stated reason.** It is pinned by `tests/test_except_audit_ratchet.py`, 12 tests, all passing, so the target class can only shrink.
- **THE REAL GAP, which is neither of the two things this entry said.** `scripts/except_audit.py:322` walks `(REPO / "core").rglob("*.py")` — **`core/` only**. Of the nine sites this entry raises, exactly **one** is in `core/`. The other eight are in `app/` and `cli/`, and that includes **all three `app/budget_guard.py` handlers** — the money-adjacent ones this entry itself calls the worst place for silent degradation. So the classification instrument exists, works, reports zero unexplained, and does not look where the concern is. The instrument's clean bill of health is scoped, and nothing states the scope at the point where the number is read.
- **DONE, 2026-08-22 evening — and the answer to this entry's original question is now measured.** `scripts/except_audit.py` walks `core/`, `cli/`, `app/`, `api/`, `tools/`; over the full scope it classifies **247 broad handlers** and the target class — silent WITHOUT a stated reason — is **0**. So the per-site classification this entry called for is complete, and the verdict on the nine sites (including all three in `app/budget_guard.py`) is: deliberate-and-documented, every one carrying a nearby comment naming its reason. **No re-baseline was needed: the ratchet held at zero over the widened scope.** The scope itself is pinned (`test_the_audit_looks_where_the_concern_is`), so narrowing it back would fail loudly instead of keeping the ratchet green for the worst reason; break-tested by setting the roots to core-only.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-22 evening). The classification exists, covers the production roots, reports zero unexplained, and its scope is ratchet-pinned. Which silent-but-documented sites should additionally get a voice remains open as a preference, not a defect — every one now provably states its reason.

### MIR-125 — append-only state grows forever, and the budget gate reads all of it per call
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-116 (the gate involved), MIR-123 (the long-run family). **Provenance:** full-codebase sweep 2026-08-22; sizes measured on the live workspace.
- **The class.** Unbounded operational state: append-only files with no rotation or compaction. A slow-burn failure the field documents well — nothing breaks today, everything is slower next month, and a long-lived agent inherits the bill.
- **Measured here.** `data/` totals 17 MB after ~1 month: `source_registry.jsonl` 3.8 MB, `approval_inbox.jsonl` 1.8 MB, `tool_receipts.jsonl` 1.2 MB, `budget_ledger.jsonl` 1.1 MB — none with a rotation or compaction policy (the episodic store's cap is the sole exception, and protected tags can exceed even that). **The sharper half:** `BudgetLedger.reserve()` calls `load_records()`, which re-reads the ENTIRE ledger file on every reservation — and MIR-116 wired a reservation into the path of every model call. The cost gate is O(file size) per call on a file that only grows: the protection added for a long-lived agent carries a seed of its own slowdown.
- **THE SHARPER HALF IS FIXED, 2026-08-22 evening — and it was not slow "today", it was already 77.5 ms per model call.** `reserve()` and `check()` now read a BOUNDED TAIL (`_records_for_windows`) instead of the whole ledger. The design is shaped by an asymmetry: windows are time-bounded so only recent records can count, but an under-read UNDERCOUNTS usage and lets the agent spend past its limit — so the read PROVES its coverage, doubling until the oldest record it has seen is strictly older than the longest window's cutoff, with the full read as the fallback on any failure. **Measured on the live 1085 KB ledger: 77.5 ms → 0.8 ms per call, 95× — and the old cost grew with every future record.** `snapshot()` keeps the full read: it is an operator diagnostic reporting over all time, pinned as such.
- **Recorded against the witness:** removing the coverage proof left all five first tests GREEN, because their fixtures fit inside one tail span — caught by break-testing, and answered with a sixth test whose in-window records deliberately exceed a span (with a guard asserting the fixture still does). The re-break then reddened it. This is the day's «ломка не покраснела — выясни почему» applied to the one property whose failure would cost money.
- **Status:** `partially_fixed` (2026-08-22). The per-call read cost is closed. The other half of this entry — unbounded GROWTH of the append-only files (rotation/compaction policy) — stays open, and its default under the escalation contract is: no rotation is built until a file's SIZE, not its read cost, is shown to hurt; the read cost was the part that hurt and it is gone. Reversed by one word from the operator.

### MIR-124 — model retirement drifts behaviour silently, and nothing here is ready for it
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** the model router's pinned specs. **Provenance:** external check 2026-08-22 against provider deprecation practice; internal state read from the router config. Gap, not defect: nothing is broken today.
- **The external fact — split 2026-08-22 into its two distinct mechanisms, which the first version conflated.** (a) **Retirement** of a named snapshot: announced with dates (a quarter to a year of notice; preview models as little as two weeks), and ends in hard failure at the deadline — loud, schedulable, survivable by a dependency register. (b) **Alias/migration drift**: the silent one — an alias re-pointed to a successor, or a recommended migration, keeps the API returning 200 while tool-call formatting, JSON adherence, refusal boundaries and verbosity shift, so parsers break while every log looks healthy. The defences differ: (a) needs a register and calendar; (b) needs a frozen golden set re-validated per model change and pinning to snapshots rather than aliases. Narrowed against OpenAI's own docs 2026-08-22: `gpt-5.6` is a **confirmed family alias** routing to `gpt-5.6-sol`; `gpt-5.6-sol` and `gpt-5.6-terra` are **canonical model IDs**, not that alias — so we are not pinned to the rolling family name. But canonical-ID is a third class, not a proof of immutability: no dated snapshot identifiers (`gpt-5.6-sol-2026-xx-xx`) are shown publicly, so whether sol/terra are version-pinned or can themselves be re-pointed remains not established — and that residue is what determines which failure mode reaches us first.
- **Our state.** The router pins named models (`gpt-5.6-sol`, `gpt-5.6-terra` seen live). There is **no dependency register, no golden set, no re-validation procedure, no fallback policy** for a retired or drifted model. This risk was never once discussed in the project before 2026-08-22.
- **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-22. The core claim is confirmed and made exact; the exposure analysis is corrected, because it describes 19% of the traffic.**
  - **Confirmed, and sharper.** `config/model_catalog.json` exists and carries **81 model ids** across providers — so there IS an inventory of what models exist. It contains **zero** occurrences of `retire`, `deprecat`, `sunset`, `valid_until`, `eol`, `replacement` or `successor`. An inventory of capability is not a dependency register: the catalog knows which models exist and nothing about when any of them stops existing. That is the gap this entry names, now pinned to a file and a count.
  - **Corrected: the models this entry analyses are not the models we mostly use.** Successful calls since 2026-08-15, from `data/model_usage.jsonl`: **`gpt-5.4-nano-2026-03-17` 281 (59%)**, **`gpt-4o-mini` 93 (20%)**, `gpt-5.6-terra` 62, `gpt-5.6-sol` 26 (19% together), `Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct` 12. So the sol/terra alias question this entry works through governs about a fifth of live traffic.
  - **And the two dominant models sit at OPPOSITE ends of the risk the entry splits in two.** `gpt-5.4-nano-2026-03-17` is a **dated snapshot** — immune to the silent alias-drift failure (b), fully exposed to the loud dated-retirement failure (a). `gpt-4o-mini` is an **undated alias to an older generation** — the exact shape most exposed to (b), and the likeliest of all 81 to be retired first. Both are also hardcoded as fallbacks outside the catalog (`core/llm.py:20` returns the literal `"gpt-4o-mini"`, `core/model_router.py:962` sets `model="gpt-4o-mini"`), so the retirement of that one id would take the fallback path with it. That is the concrete first casualty a dependency register would have to name.
- **DEFAULT UNDER THE ESCALATION CONTRACT (2026-08-22, `docs/audit/ESCALATION_CONTRACT.md`):** no register is built yet; the default is the two facts already banked here (the catalog inventory and the named first casualty `gpt-4o-mini` with its two hardcoded fallback sites) plus this tripwire: the FIRST retirement-shaped failure of any model id converts this entry into the register work, immediately and without a new decision. Reversed by one word from the operator.
- **Status:** `open`. Recorded as a gap; what to build, if anything, is the operator's decision under the freeze rules.

### MIR-123 — the mathematics of long runs is against the marathon shape
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-116 (bounded campaigns), the freeze-cost note (no consequence accumulation). **Provenance:** external check 2026-08-22; field measurements, not ours.
- **The external facts — contested 2026-08-22 and re-verified at source; the specific formulas did not survive.** The first version carried epsilon(t) = epsilon0 + alpha*log t and "doubling duration quadruples failure" from secondary syntheses; the primary abstracts (arXiv 2603.29231, 2604.11978) contain **neither** — both are now UNPROVEN and must not be quoted from this registry. What the primaries DO say: long-horizon degradation is systematic and domain-stratified (software-engineering reliability dropping 0.90 -> 0.44 in one metric), frontier models show "meltdown" rates up to 19%, and agents that are strong at short horizons break on extended interdependent sequences. METR 2026 stands and cuts both ways: a 50% time horizon is task difficulty rather than unattended endurance, one point of task messiness costs about 8% success — and horizons keep doubling every few months, so any fixed "under 10% beyond 4 hours" figure is a snapshot, not a law.
- **What it means here — unchanged in direction, weakened in force.** Long continuous runs degrade for reasons the field measures but has not reduced to one formula. Chained short segments with verified checkpoints remain the reasonable engineering default; whether our cycles constitute real verification boundaries has not been assessed. This entry no longer claims the marathon shape is "measured to fail" — it claims the marathon shape carries a measured, systematic, and currently unmodelled degradation risk.
- **Status:** `open`. Recorded so the next long-run design argues against the measured mathematics rather than against hope.

### MIR-122 — context rot: every frontier model degrades with input length, and we never measured ours
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-123 (same long-run family). **Provenance:** external check 2026-08-22 (Chroma technical report, 18 frontier models).
- **The external facts — re-verified at the source 2026-08-22 after the falsification pass initially skipped this entry.** What the Chroma report actually supports: across 18 tested models, increasing context length produces **systematic but strongly model- and task-dependent, non-uniform** reliability degradation. STRUCK from the first version, as stronger than the source: the universal "drops of 30–50%" (a result of some configurations, not all 18 models), and "middle of the context attended worst" — Chroma's own NIAH experiment reports **no notable variation** by needle position, and their repeated-words experiment shows position effects that often favour the START; "lost in the middle" is a term from other literature, not this report's finding. Reliability at short context still says nothing about the same task at long context — that part stands.
- **Our state.** Campaign cycles, planning prompts and memory injection all grow context over a run's life; the project has **no measurement** of how its own loops behave as context lengthens, and no context-budgeting policy informed by one.
- **MEASURED 2026-08-22 — and the entry's own premise did not survive.** This said campaign cycles and memory injection "grow context over a run's life". Measured on `data/model_usage.jsonl` (984 calls carrying `input_tokens`): of **461 runs, exactly ONE has 8 or more model calls**, and across that run input grew 813 -> 883 tokens, **1.1x**. Runs here are short, so there is no within-run context accumulation to rot. The real distribution is a wide spread, not a ramp: planner median **8 659** tokens, synthesizer median **6 594**, with a 2% tail at or above 40 000 (26 of 984 calls) and a single maximum of **115 442**. Twenty-five of those 26 large calls went to Anthropic models, which have since stopped answering (MIR-132), so even that tail may not be reachable on the current provider set.
- **What this does and does not settle.** It settles the LOCAL question — our loops do not lengthen their own context as a run proceeds, so the marathon-shaped rot this entry feared is not the mechanism here. It settles NOTHING about degradation itself: whether a 115k-token synthesizer call answers worse than an 8k one is unmeasured and cannot be measured from logs, because it needs paired model calls and therefore the operator's money. That half stays open and is now precisely scoped: probe the synthesizer at the observed extremes, not the planner, and not the run length.
- **Status:** `open` (narrowed). The growth half is measured and negative; the degradation half is unmeasured and needs a funded probe.
- **DEFAULT UNDER THE ESCALATION CONTRACT (2026-08-22, `docs/audit/ESCALATION_CONTRACT.md`):** the funded probe waits until a week of real unattended traffic exists (post-launch), then measures the SYNTHESIZER at the observed extremes (8k vs 40k+ input), not run length — the shape the measurement above scoped. Probing now would spend money on synthetic load. Reversed by one word from the operator.

- **RE-CHECKED 2026-08-23, and the 2026-08-22 conclusion needs one correction and one addition.**
- **A field number was checked at the source and REFUSED.** A 2026 summary claims reasoning accuracy falling «from 0.92 to 0.68 as inputs grew from a few hundred to three thousand tokens». Fetched, the article does not support it: the only concrete measured figures it carries are Adobe's (Feb 2025) at a **32 000-token** prompt — GPT-4o 99% → 70%, Claude 3.5 Sonnet 88% → 30%, Gemini 2.5 Flash 94% → 48%, Llama 4 Scout 82% → 22%. Degradation «starting at 3k» is not established; degradation measured at 32k is. The threshold used below is the supported one.
- **Our distribution against the supported threshold.** Of 984 calls carrying `input_tokens`: median **8 492**, p90 **13 652**, p99 **53 008**, max **115 442**. **44 calls (4%) are at or above 32 000** — the region where the field's numbers are measured rather than extrapolated. By role, 39 of those 44 are the **synthesizer**.
- **The addition, and it is the week-relevant part: the tail did not disappear, it MOVED.** Of the 44 large calls, **43 went to anthropic** and one to openai, all between 2026-07-29 and 2026-08-14. Anthropic has since been demoted for an empty balance (MIR-132/134), so those same calls now route to openai. Both `synthesizer` and `planner` currently resolve to `openai/gpt-4o-mini`, whose window is 128 000 — so the largest observed input would occupy **90% of the window**, and the field's own summary of this literature is that models become unreliable **30–40% before** their stated limit.
- **And nothing bounds it.** `context_window` is carried per model spec but used ONLY as a tie-breaker inside `_model_score` / `_model_score_for_policy` when choosing a model — it is never compared against what is about to be sent. The one real budget is `core/evidence_budget`, capped at 32 000 characters (≈8 000 tokens), which explains the MEDIAN and not the tail: on the largest calls the bulk comes from somewhere the budget does not reach.
- **Status:** `open`, sharpened. What was «we never measured ours» is now measured twice; what is still missing is a **bound**: no check compares the assembled prompt against the chosen model's window before the call, so the failure mode is either silent degradation (openai accepts it) or a hard provider error at the worst moment of an unattended week.

### MIR-121 — the memory's `lesson` bypass is a mature attack class's front door
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-115 (the bypass itself, measured), MIR-118 injection axis (lethal trifecta). **Provenance:** external check 2026-08-22 against the memory-poisoning literature; internal state already measured in MIR-115.
- **The external facts.** Memory injection attacks (MINJA and successors) achieve **~95% injection success through ordinary queries**, need no elevated privileges, and the poison **survives session restarts, context resets and even model updates**. LLM-as-judge sanitisation is bypassed: models are socially engineered into assigning perfect trust scores to malicious entries. Poisoned records then ride retrieval into future decisions.
- **Why it lands on us — narrowed 2026-08-22 by a falsification pass.** Two corrections to the first version. (1) "Unconditionally" is dead: `decide_usage_eligibility` now runs `_answer_disqualified` BEFORE the lesson exception (`core/smart_memory.py:1222`), so one disqualification check precedes the bypass; what remains true is that a `lesson` tag skips every OTHER verification axis (completion, outcome, verified chunks, replay), and the machinery that produces the content still mints the exempting tag. (2) The chain **web -> lesson-tagged episode -> later action is UNPROVEN**: `lesson` tags are minted by the self-build/self-repair machinery (`core/self_build_memory.py:122`, `core/self_repair.py:504`), not by web ingestion; whether web-derived text can reach a lesson-tagged episode indirectly has not been traced. The external attack class is real and mature; its reachability in THIS system is a hypothesis awaiting a trace, not a measured path.
- **THE UNPROVEN CHAIN WAS TRACED 2026-08-22, and the answer is that it CANNOT be traced from the record — which is a worse result than either yes or no.** This entry left open whether web-derived text can reach a `lesson`-tagged episode indirectly. Measured on the live store:
  - 22 episodes carry a web-ish `source_labels` entry (`web_`, `http`, `search`, `fetch`, `rss`, `scholar`). **Zero of them are tagged `lesson`.** So the DIRECT chain is not realised.
  - **All 127 `lesson`-tagged episodes carry no `source_labels` at all — not a web label, not a file label, none.** (63 after the 2026-08-22 gate-wait demotion; the no-labels fact is unchanged for the survivors, so the untraceability stands.) They are minted by `core/self_build_memory.py` from self-build reports, and that writer records no provenance.
  - Web-derived content does exist upstream: `data/persistent_memory.jsonl` holds 125 rows of which 10 carry a web trace.
- **What that means, stated carefully.** The absence of a web label on the lesson population is **absence of evidence, not evidence of absence** — the question cannot be answered in either direction from what is stored, because the exact records the attack class would target are the ones that record nothing about where their content came from. The `memory:` label that `_lesson_provenance_disqualified` now refuses appears on **zero** lesson episodes, so that guard has nothing to bite on here either: it filters a label the writer never writes.
- **THE WRITE-SIDE CHANGE IS BUILT, 2026-08-22 evening.** `ProducerReport` carries `sources` recorded at the moment of reading — `file:<target>` always, `memory:self-build-lessons` exactly when recalled lessons were injected into the Builder prompt — and `build_self_build_episode` carries them into `source_labels` (older callers fall back to the target and changed files, so no episode is label-less again). The `memory:` label is the point: injected lessons come FROM episodic memory, so a lesson minted from a run that consumed lessons is memory-derived content — the laundering channel this entry's attack class uses — and `_lesson_provenance_disqualified` finally has the label it was written to refuse. Pinned end-to-end in `tests/test_self_build_producer.py::test_the_report_names_what_the_run_read` (with the boundary that the label means memory was ACTUALLY read, or the guard would quarantine clean lessons) plus five writer tests; the first break-test of the producer half stayed green because the unit tests hand-built the dict — the end-to-end witness was added and the re-break reddened it. From the next produced episode on, an absent label is evidence of absence. The 63 standing label-less lessons stay unanswerable — history is not rewritten.
- **Consequently the closure of this entry needed a WRITE-side change before any measurement could settle it (as recorded before the fix):** `core/self_build_memory.py` must record what the run actually read, the way chat and campaign episodes already do. Until then, "no web-derived lesson was found" is a statement about the instrument, not about the store — the same false-zero class this audit hit four times on 2026-08-22 (`docs/audit/SELF_BUILD_FAILURE_ANALYSIS.md`, Addendum 2).
- **ИЗМЕРЕНО 2026-08-25: это не совпадение чисел, а РАЗБИЕНИЕ.** Из 142 эпизодов 72 несут метки источника и 72 пригодны к подбору — но пересечение всего **9**. И эти девять ТОЧНО СОВПАДАЮТ с теми, у кого `verified_chunks > 0` (множества равны, проверено сравнением идентификаторов). Оставшиеся **63 пригодных не имеют ни источника, ни проверки** — и это ровно `lesson`-популяция: пересечение «источники ∩ lesson» равно **нулю**, как и было 22 августа.
- **Что это значит одной фразой.** Память, способная рулить будущим ответом, делится надвое без остатка: **9 из 72** что-то достали, проверились и записали происхождение; **63 из 72** не проверены ничем и происхождения не имеют вовсе. То есть семь восьмых рулящей памяти непрослеживаемы по построению, а не по недосмотру.
- **Правка на стороне записи не произвела НИ ОДНОЙ строки.** `ProducerReport.sources` построен вечером 2026-08-22, а новейший эпизод в хранилище — **2026-08-20**: агент остановился за двое суток до починки. Она не опровергнута и не подтверждена — она ни разу не работала (см. «починка не готова, пока не прошла живым путём»).
- **Цена очевидного шага измерена заранее.** Потребовать происхождение для пригодности к подбору значит замолчать **63 эпизода из 72**. Это не побочный эффект, а весь эффект; поэтому шаг остаётся решением оператора, а не экспертным дефолтом.
- **Та же дверь закрывает первый критерий MIR-096.** Исключение `lesson` в `decide_usage_eligibility` — единственное, чем урок из ПРОВАЛИВШЕГОСЯ прогона доходит до канала, который читают позже (17 из 20 таких эпизодов пригодны к подбору). Одна механика: здесь она вход для атаки, там — единственный путь обучения на ошибках. Закрывать её, не построив второй путь, значит закрыть и обучение.
- **Метку выдаёт исход, который не удался.** Сужение 2026-08-22 отняло `lesson` ТОЛЬКО у статусов ожидания ворот (`core/self_build_memory.py:137`); всё остальное её получает. Живой счёт по 63 «урокам»: успешных **26**, частичных **20**, провалившихся **17** — то есть **37 из 63 (59 %) не удались**, и все они несут пригодность к подбору, защиту от вытеснения и приоритет выдачи. Десять из них — вето критика.
- **Обе починки 2026-08-22 не проверены жизнью.** И запись источников, и сужение метки построены 22 августа, а хранилище стоит с 20-го: ни одна не произвела ни строки. Нынешние 63 — прошлое, но правило, их породившее, действует и сейчас, кроме отнятого куска.
- **Виды меток у тех, кто их имеет** (23 `read_logs:latest`, 16 и 15 — чтения файлов доктрины, 15 `list_dir:core/`): происхождение сегодня фиксируется у ЧТЕНИЙ, а не у выводов. Урок, рождённый из чтения, метку теряет.
- **Status:** `open`. The bypass stays banked as in MIR-115; this entry upgrades its threat model from internal hygiene to external attack class. Repair choice belongs to the operator.

### MIR-120 — the containment walls are one class below the field's own answer
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-118 (whose confinement caveat this sharpens), C0.P viability class. **Provenance:** measured in this codebase 2026-08-22 (grep for OS isolation primitives: zero uses), against externally verified practice — full check in `docs/audit/WALL_CLASS_AND_EXTERNAL_PRACTICE.md`, every claim taken to a primary source.
- **The finding.** Every wall here is **in-process Python** — the `file_write` resolver, the `shell_exec` whitelist, `SafeVCS`'s missing remote verbs, the egress policy, the MIR-116 cost envelope — running in the same interpreter as the agent, under the operator's own OS user, with API keys in the same process environment. The ladder is textual → in-process → OS/infra, and this codebase stands on the middle rung. The middle rung holds an agent acting through tools; it does not hold arbitrary code in the process, and a self-editing agent is a machine for approaching arbitrary code in the process. The field's own trajectory (Anthropic's 93%-approval fatigue answered by an OS sandbox and an 84% prompt drop; OpenAI's keyring-isolated credentials and egress policy; DeepMind's insider-threat framing) is precisely the lesson that the middle rung plus human prompts was insufficient.
- **Split 2026-08-22 into what is established and what is not.** ESTABLISHED, from code: the wall-class gap itself — zero OS isolation primitives, every wall in-process, API keys in the agent's process environment, no principal separation. UNPROVEN, and to be treated as hypothesis until traced: that the effects path can in practice rewrite the walls themselves and that the per-tick restart then animates the rewritten wall **without any fresh authority**. Structurally suggestive facts on each side: `file_write` is bounded only by the workspace (which contains `core/` and `tools/`), while the self-apply lane — the path DESIGNED for self-editing — carries a critical-organ denylist the raw tool lacks; but whether the unattended effects path actually reaches a wall file, keeps the change (nothing on the raw planner->policy->gateway->file_write path runs pytest automatically — tests run there only if the PLAN chose to run them; the full-pytest insurance belongs to the self-apply lane, a different path, and must not be assumed for this one), and has it imported by the next process has not been demonstrated. Viability class under C0.P; the differential experiment is the measurement candidate that should precede any long unattended effects run.
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-22 by primitive-name sweep, and the finding holds exactly.** Searched `core/`, `app/`, `cli/`, `tools/`, `scripts/` for every OS-level containment primitive by name — `seccomp`, `AppContainer`, Job Object, `CreateProcessAsUser`, restricted token, low-integrity, `chroot`, `setrlimit`, `firejail`, `bubblewrap`, Seatbelt, `docker run`: **zero hits**.
