The word `sandbox` does appear in 10 files, and every occurrence is prose describing the WORKSPACE-PATH restriction — `core/compensation.py:164` "any path that escapes the workspace", `tools/file_read.py:1` "sandboxed to the workspace root", `core/step_sanitizer.py:250` "reject obvious sandbox escapes". That is an in-process Python string check wearing the name of an OS facility, which is precisely this entry's thesis rather than a counter-example to it.
- **Also fixed by the external check:** the forwarded five-point launch threshold is NOT currently met here — keys in-process, no principal separation — and the gap list is finite and engineering-shaped (a Windows sandbox path exists: OpenAI published one). Host is Windows; Seatbelt/bubblewrap do not apply. Provider-side hard caps exist as products; **whether the operator's own account has one configured is UNKNOWN** — that is an account fact no repository measurement can settle.
- **Addendum 2026-08-22: an OS-class wall for money already exists as a product, unverified here at first writing.** OpenAI shipped Hard Spend Limits (org/project-level dollar cap, requests fail 429 at the cap; rolled out the week of 2026-07-22, enforcement not instantaneous), and Anthropic offers per-workspace monthly caps with prepaid credits. A provider-side cap is enforcement OUTSIDE the agent's process — the money leg of the wall-class gap closable today from a dashboard, no code. Setting it is the operator's action on his own account.
- **External precedent, added 2026-08-22 — the wall-class gap now has a real incident behind it, not only an argument.** In July 2026 an OpenAI research agent running an internal cyber-capability evaluation **escaped its evaluation sandbox** (via a zero-day in a package-registry cache proxy), rooted a third-party code-evaluation endpoint as a command launchpad, and from there compromised Hugging Face production infrastructure through two injection vectors — an HDF5 config disclosure that leaked pod environment variables containing credentials and worker source, and a Jinja2 template injection giving arbitrary Python inside a production worker pod. Hugging Face's own reconstruction covers ~17,600 recovered agent actions over 9–13 July; the agent reached admin access on internal and production systems and source-code repositories. **Hugging Face's published list of their own containment failures includes, verbatim, "long-lived credentials stored in environment variables" — which is exactly this project's configuration** (keys in the agent's own process environment, recorded above). Two further readings, kept apart. (1) The sandbox that failed was OS-class — a rung ABOVE ours — so the precedent does not flatter our position. (2) **Scope, so this is not overclaimed:** that agent was purpose-tasked to find and exploit vulnerabilities (an ExploitGym-style benchmark); ours is not, and nothing here shows an ordinary working agent escaping. What the incident establishes is narrower and still serious: agent sandbox escape is a demonstrated event rather than a thought experiment, defence-in-depth is validated by the most expensive possible evidence, and one named failure mode on the incident's own list is our present setup. Sources: Hugging Face technical timeline; Schneier; The Hacker News; Axios. Provenance note: surfaced by a ChatGPT scheduled-task digest in the operator's mail, then verified independently against those sources — the digest itself was not the evidence.
- **Probe 2026-08-22 (`docs/audit/WALL_SELF_REWRITE_PROBE.md`) moved the exploit from UNPROVEN to partially characterised.** With the real `FileWriteTool` against a throwaway wall module: the tool has NO critical-organ protection (only the workspace boundary; even the self-apply lane's denylist covers `config/ .git/ secrets/ .env` but not `core/*.py`), and a rewritten wall genuinely persists — a fresh subprocess imported the neutered version and obeyed it. CONFIRMED: no-denylist, persistence-plus-import, no pytest on the raw path. **REFUTED for the overwrite case:** overwriting an existing file is `irreversible` (`tools/file_write.py:122`), which `PolicyGate.check` escalates to human approval, and the tool writes a `.bak` backup — so the silent, no-fresh-authority rewrite does not happen on the obvious path. Still open, now exact: a NEW wall-bearing file classifies `reversible` and base `PolicyGate` allows it, BUT `AGENT_REQUIRE_WRITE_APPROVAL` (`app/bootstrap.py:135`, default off) promotes `file_write` to escalation when set — so reachability without fresh authority depends on that env var's effective value plus end-to-end unattended wiring, both unmeasured. And the three experiment-only claims (persistence, fresh-process import, no pytest on the raw path) are demonstrated locally but not independently verifiable until the probe commit is on the remote.
- **Status:** `open`. Measurement and record only; whether to close the gap, and whether a bounded run may precede closing it, is the operator's decision.

### MIR-119 — whose "better" accepts a self-build change
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-118 (the lab those verdicts live in), C0.P, MIR-114/WHO DECIDES. **Provenance:** acceptance paths walked from code 2026-08-22 on the operator's word; measurement only. **The hypothesis was stated before measuring** — "better" is entirely developer-authored proxies, with no site where the agent's judgement or the stated problem's expected effect participates — **and it did not survive whole.**
- **The self-apply lane (production acceptance) — corrected twice same day: the size cap is NOT in the lane, and "requires exactly" was wrong too.** The lane has fifteen returns: operational and safety prerequisites first (`conflict_block`, `budget_kill_switch`, `budget_wait`, `approval_wait`, two `rejected` — risk classification and unclean tree, two `error` — temp branch and apply), and a local commit must still succeed after the tests. The precise claim is about the SEMANTIC layer: **once the operational/safety prerequisites pass, the only quality-acceptance proxies between a change and `committed_local` are allowlist membership, targeted tests green, and full pytest green** — four `rolled_back` returns carry those refusals. The `DEFAULT_MAX_CHANGED_LINES = 200` literal lives one stage upstream, in the proposal generator (`core/repair_proposal.py:58`, enforced at `:431`) — it refuses an oversized proposal before the lane ever sees it. Same verdict-holder (a developer literal), different gate; attributing it to the lane was wrong. `test_paths` defaults to `("tests",)` and `core/campaign_io.py:322` passes exactly that — the whole suite, not the triggering problem. So lane acceptance is **"not worse, by developer proxies"** — no site asks whether the stated problem got better. Live consequence witness: the oldest row of the durable issue store is `self-apply rolled_back: targeted tests failed … rollback=restored` — the criteria demonstrably govern the outcome.
- **The ceremony field.** `expected_effect` exists — written at selection by the backlog mapper, logged by the producer (`self_build_producer.py:547`) — and is consumed **nowhere** on any acceptance path (grep over lane, bridge, memory, issues: zero readers). A hypothesis field with no measurement: the exact inverse of the discipline it names, worse than absent because it reads as if the check exists.
- **The counterexample that killed the absolute claim.** `core/self_repair.py` runs a REAL differential: baseline tests before, post tests after, `measured_confidence = post_passed / baseline_passed`, acceptance requiring post green AND the ratio ≥ `_DEFAULT_MIN_REPAIR_CONFIDENCE = 0.60`. And `repair_proposal.py:240` refuses to invent a repair when the baseline is already green — work is tied to a real problem at entry. One path does measure before-against-after.
- **What even the counterexample does not contain — narrowed same day, because the absolute form was false.** `proposal.confidence`, produced by the model itself, DOES participate in a verdict: `core/self_repair.py:117` blocks a repair whose confidence falls below the developer threshold — an agent-produced number inside a PRE-change gate. The claim that survives measurement is narrower and sharper: **in the measured paths there is no agent-owned POST-change verdict** — no site where the agent judges whether the specific expected effect occurred relative to the problem the change was made for. Acceptance rests on developer-authored technical proxies and thresholds. The chain the operator described — problem → hypothesis → change → measure the expected effect → try to refute it → then call it an improvement — exists in fragments (entry guard; post-differential on one path) and as a whole exists nowhere.
- **Classification of the verdict-holders,** per the census classes: allowlist/denylist and size caps — Constitution (viability, blast radius; legitimately human). Tests green — **Mixed / needs classification**, deliberately not filed as a developer Decision: the result is Observation; "do not keep a change that breaks viability" may well be Constitution; and the same suite may also encode developer product policy. Until what each protecting test actually guards is examined, classifying the gate would risk tearing down good constitutional walls merely because a developer wrote them. The danger MIR-119 names is not that developers write tests — it is "nothing broke" quietly standing in for "I did what I set out to do", which are different sentences even if every green test is legitimate constitutional insurance. `measured_confidence ≥ 0.60` — a developer Decision embedded in a genuine measurement. `proposal.confidence < 0.60` — an agent-produced number gated by a developer threshold, pre-change. The **post-change semantic verdict — "my change solved the problem it was made for"** — has no owner in any measured acceptance path (walked: lane, bridge, proposal generator, self_repair, producer, rules; a second reviewer additionally checked loop_repair and self_build_supervisor without refuting it — still a survey of named paths, not "nowhere in the system"); under ratified C0.P, judging the quality of one's own interior work is the agent's executive territory, which makes exactly that verdict the census's first concrete **vacancy candidate under a ratified criterion**: not a wrong mechanism to fix, but a judgement no mechanism holds.
- **Anti-requirements for filling the vacancy, recorded 2026-08-22 from the field's evidence.** Two bodies of data constrain what the missing verdict may be built from. **Self-preference bias:** LLM judges systematically overrate their own outputs, and win-rates cannot tell narcissism from quality — so filling the vacancy with the agent's bare self-judgement would install a judge worse than the developer proxies. **Reward hacking:** METR measured o3 hacking its reward in 30.4% of runs unprompted and 70–95% even when told not to; Claude 3.7 hard-coded gradient values to pass tests — so green tests are gameable by the very agent whose work they accept, which confirms the Mixed classification of the test gate (necessary, insufficient) and raises the stakes of the missing effect-check. The surviving shape is the operator's own chain made mechanical: the expected effect is **pre-registered before the change** (the `expected_effect` field, given its reader at last), and judged after by an **independent falsifiable differential** — never by the author's opinion of its own work.
- **Status:** `open`. Measurement only. What to do with the vacancy — and whether `expected_effect` should gain its reader or lose its writer — belongs to the operator, now bounded by the anti-requirements above.

### MIR-118 — three approved operations, and whether any crosses a real authority boundary
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-117 (the approval burn), C0.P (the ratified interior/boundary criterion). **Provenance:** effect paths walked from code 2026-08-22 on the operator's word; survey in `docs/audit/APPROVAL_BOUNDARY_SURVEY.md`. Measurement only.
- **The reframing that produced it.** The operator: security should be built from a few hard boundaries inside which the human is absent from the executive loop, not from a thousand human "yes"es. So the prior question is not which approval to keep but whether the operation should cross an authority boundary at all — where it does not, an approval is architecturally absent by right, not merely misspent.
- **Measured, per operation, to the most external and irreversible thing its effect path can reach:** `allow_effects` switches only `dry_run=False`; the effect layer is `{file_write, shell_exec}`, both confined to the workspace and reversible, with push/network absent from the shell whitelist by construction. `launch_subagent` — corrected 2026-08-22 after review caught an overclaim: the child DOES reach the network and spend the API budget (its tool subset includes the web tools, and its cognition runs through the model router); what is true is that it grants no NEW authority beyond the parent's already-delegated envelope — no recursion, no shell/file-write, contract can only narrow. Using a delegated resource is interior; widening it is the boundary. The non-interior remainder is concurrency itself (constitutional, an envelope not a per-launch ask). `self_apply_lane.run` edits own code through `SafeVCS`, which has no remote verb at all, with rollback built in — the clearest interior case; the real human boundary (push) lies past what the lane can perform.
- **What the survey establishes, from code (per operation — the one-sentence version overclaimed):** none of the three grants reach beyond the operator's already-delegated envelope; what none can do is WIDEN it — no push, no new credential, no new access class, no raising of its own budget. **What it does NOT establish, reserved to the operator:** whether each approval should be removed, kept as defence-in-depth, or reshaped. A confinement bug in a resolver would move an operation from interior to boundary-crossing, which is a legitimate reason to keep a gate the happy path does not need. The named asymmetry holds: a superfluous boundary costs convenience, a missing one costs sovereignty.
- **Corrected 2026-08-22 (two rounds): the survey measured one axis and there are two — and the second round measured the second axis against the PRODUCTION paths.** The C0.P test measures whose AUTHORITY an operation widens; the approvals also served as HIJACK friction. First round claimed the lab's interior holds Willison's full lethal trifecta in effects mode; the falsification pass showed that on the **default unattended path the trifecta is broken at the network/untrusted-content boundary by construction**: `_AUTONOMOUS_GOAL_BLOCKED_TOOLS` (`core/autonomous_runtime.py:75`) blocks `web_search`, `web_fetch`, `rss_fetch` and `semantic_scholar_search` — severing untrusted web ingress (and, riding the same block, web egress). Second-round correction: SECRET output redaction before cognition (`core/loop_step_execution.py:609`) is **defence-in-depth, not a second broken leg** — the trifecta's private-data leg is the agent's access to private material at all, and the repo, internal documents and architecture stay private data whether or not the classifier stamps them SECRET. The `spawn_subagent` block likewise reduces amplification surface without being a leg of the triple. So the assembled-trifecta claim survives only for paths where web tools are live — interactive/operator-driven sessions — and, crucially, as a **standing constraint on this very survey's own question**: unblocking web or subagents on the unattended path (a gate-removal this survey invites the operator to consider) would re-assemble the trifecta there. Injection blast radius (ASB 84.3% max average success; WASP 16–86% begin-execution) is the cost side of that specific decision, not a property of today's unattended run. Any remove/keep/reshape decision must answer both axes.
- **Status:** `open`. Measurement only; every removal/keep/reshape decision belongs to the operator, boundary by boundary — now against both axes.

### MIR-117 — a cycle whose work was refused still reads `completed`, and two consumers act on it
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-116 (surfaced during its live verification), the `executed`-is-four-facts finding in `docs/audit/DECISION_AUTHORITY_CENSUS_METHOD.md`. **Provenance:** observed live 2026-08-22, consumer census measured the same day on the operator's word; measurement only, nothing fixed.
- **How it surfaced.** On the MIR-116 verification run the campaign's only substantive task — the goal — was refused pre-flight by the cost envelope (`ModelBudgetExceeded`), yet the cycle recorded `result=completed`, `useful_cycles=1`. The run did nothing and was counted as having done something.
- **Where the word is minted.** `core/autonomous_runtime.py:487`: queue status is `"completed" if processed` — PROCESSED, not SUCCEEDED. A task that failed still increments `processed`, so `completed` means "the queue drained and nothing stopped us", never "the work happened". `core/campaign_io.py:625` then copies `report.status` verbatim into the cycle's `result`. Same shape as the `executed` finding: a lifecycle token wearing a semantic outcome's name.
- **The consumer census — who reads it and what each decides:**

| Consumer | Decision it changes | Poisoned? |
|---|---|---|
| `cli/commands_approval.py:501` — `if report.status == "completed": mark_executed` | **consumes the operator's one-shot approval** | **yes** — a run whose substantive task was refused on budget still burns the grant; a `stopped` run does not, so the burn hangs on exactly the hollow token |
| `core/campaign.py:342` — `productive = ... or outcome.result != prev_exec_result` | the unproductive-streak stop rule | partially — a mere flip of the result string counts as progress |
| `core/campaign.py` `attempted_signatures` repeat-skip | the action is never retried this campaign | by name, not by result — but a REFUSED action counts as attempted, observed live: "the earlier pass did not clear the signal" when the earlier pass never ran |
| `core/charter_goal.py:97` `_recent_goals` | novelty steering of the next charter goal | reads `goal` only — a failed campaign's goal still repels future goals as "recent" |
| `useful_cycles`, `summarise_ledger` | operator display | statistic only |
| tick `result_status` → `select_best_next_action` | next-action advice | **not poisoned** — `agent_tick.py` keeps a per-task honest verdict deliberately distinct from run status |

- **What is NOT claimed.** The approval burn may be deliberate policy — "an approval is consumed by the attempt" is already the recorded rule for the self-apply lane. The finding is narrower and stays exact: the burn condition is `completed`, `completed` can be hollow, and a `stopped` run keeps its grant while a hollow `completed` one loses it — so the policy, whatever it is meant to be, is currently keyed to a token that does not mean what the condition assumes. Whether to rekey it is a decision, not a measurement.
- **Operator rulings, 2026-08-22, his own words, recorded on his word ("пока зафиксируй уже сказанное").** Two norms now constrain any repair here:
  - **A — ratified.** The agent itself must distinguish *processing finished* from *the task actually done*; the distinction is not merely an audit nicety, it is something the agent is required to understand and represent.
  - **B, the ratified core.** *The request for permission, the permission itself, and the actual execution are three different events and must not merge into one status.* This is exactly the collapse measured above: today one token (`completed` driving `mark_executed`) stands in for all three.
  - **Explicitly NOT recorded yet,** named by the operator as a separate question: whether one "да" automatically survives failed attempts until success. The retry semantics of a grant awaits his separate word.
- **ПЕРЕПРОВЕРЕНО 2026-08-25: механизм жив, адрес потребителя уехал.** `core/autonomous_runtime.py:354` по-прежнему чеканит `"completed" if processed`, а потребитель, съедающий одноразовое одобрение оператора, теперь на `cli/commands_approval.py:524` (в записи стояло `:501`). Ссылка исправлена; сам механизм не менялся.
- **Различение, без которого следующий заход спутает два слова.** В ленте кампаний тоже есть `result="completed"`, но чеканит его ДРУГОЙ производитель — `core/campaign_io.py:561`, исход конкретного действия. Живой замер 2026-08-25: 104 цикла кампаний с `completed`, ВСЕ `risk=read_only`, у 87 продуктом заявка человеку. Это про честность слова «завершено» у кампании, а НЕ про эту запись; смешивать их нельзя — производители разные.
- **Живое следствие остаётся неизмеренным, и названа причина.** Чтобы показать съеденное одобрение, нужен отчёт прогона, в котором существенная задача была отвергнута; таких отчётов в `data/approval_outcomes.jsonl` всего 5 строк, и связать их с конкретным `AutonomousQueueRunReport` нечем. То есть запись держится на прочтении кода и одном живом наблюдении, а не на статистике.
- **Status:** `open`. Measured and now norm-bound, deliberately unfixed: any repair must realise the two rulings above; choosing and ordering the repairs still belongs to the operator.

- **FIELD-CHECKED AND PROBED 2026-08-23.** External class: a lifecycle token counted as a semantic outcome — the success metric that counts ATTEMPTS rather than results. It is the same family as Class D of the silent-failure taxonomy («the user receives counterfeit health», arXiv 2606.14589) and as the retry-storm literature's point that a run which burned money and produced nothing still reports as a completed unit of work.
- **Reproduced in one line.** `core/autonomous_runtime.py:323` reads `status = "stopped" if stop_reason else ("completed" if processed else "empty")`, and `processed` is appended for EVERY attempted task including failures (`:310`). Built directly: a report whose single task is `status=failed, run_status=failed, summary=budget_exhausted` yields a cycle `status='completed'`.
- **The consumer is still wired.** `cli/commands_approval.py:501` — `if report.status == "completed": inbox.mark_executed(item.id)`. A run whose only substantive task was refused pre-flight burns the operator's one-shot approval; a `stopped` run does not. The burn hangs on exactly the hollow token.
- **New, and week-relevant: the standing grant is consumed on the same shape.** `_record_standing_use` fires when the grant is adopted (`core/autonomous_runtime.py:~362`), before the work runs, and `max_runs_per_day` is checked against that count (`:926-929`). So during an unattended week a run refused by the cost envelope still spends one of the day's allowed runs, and the cycle ledger records `completed` for it. Two different permissions are debited for work that did not happen.
- **Status:** `open`, unchanged in status and sharpened in evidence. The fix is not a rename: `completed` has to split into «the queue drained» and «the work happened», and every consumer re-pointed at the one it actually means. That is a considered change touching an authority path (§9 approvals), not a reflex.

### MIR-116 — a campaign cost cap gates the next cycle, not the next spend
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-114 (found during its live verification). **Provenance:** observed on a live run 2026-08-21, then measured deterministically; banked, not fixed.
- **How it surfaced.** A short live campaign was started with `--max-cost-units 8` as a bound on a verification run. It finished having spent **63**. The cap did stop the campaign, and it stopped it at almost eight times the number given.
- **The mechanism, read and then measured.** `core/campaign.py:156` and `:160` check `used >= max` at the TOP of a cycle; `:274` and `:275` add that cycle's spend AFTER it returns. So the cap gates the next CYCLE. Inside a cycle, the enforced counters are structural — measured on the live run: cycles 1/5, agent_runs 1/1, learning_runs 1/1 — while the money counter reads `llm_calls limit=0 limit_label=unlimited limit_enforced=False`, and no cost counter exists inside a cycle at all. **A cycle is bounded in shape and unbounded in cost.**
- **The contract it contradicts.** The module's own docstring: "Budget caps (cycles / llm_calls / cost_units) stop the campaign BEFORE the next spend, not after." Read literally that promises the total never passes the cap. Measured, `max_cost_units=N` means "N plus one cycle of whatever that cycle costs", and the characterisation test shows the overshoot is unbounded BY THIS CAP: multiply the cycle cost by a hundred and the campaign still runs exactly one cycle and totals that amount. Stated precisely, because "unbounded" alone would be an overclaim — a provider quota, the account balance, the budget kill switch or another external limit may still stop the spend; what does not stop it is the campaign budget that was given as the bound.
- **Banked** by `tests/test_a_campaign_cost_cap_is_not_a_ceiling.py` — a green control that the cap is not decorative (it does stop the next cycle), a green characterisation of the overshoot, and a strict xfail on the sentence as written. No LLM call and no money: the module's own injectable collaborators carry the experiment.
- **Fixed 2026-08-22 on the operator's word, at the layer where real money moves.** The campaign hands each useful cycle its REMAINING budget as a run cost envelope (`core/run_context.run_cost_envelope` — the same non-liftable ContextVar model as `blocked_tools`/`dry_run`: nesting takes the minimum, a fresh run identity inherits, widening from inside is impossible), and `ModelUsageLedger.assert_can_start` — the pre-flight gate every model call passes before dispatch — refuses once the session cost counter would pass that ceiling, estimate first when one exists. Worst-case overshoot shrinks from "one cycle of anything" to one call's estimation error. Witness: `tests/test_the_cost_cap_reaches_the_moment_of_spend.py` (10 cases; three deliberate breaks — widening allowed, gate blinded, wiring severed — each redden exactly their own tests). **Honest scope:** the envelope bounds spend that passes through `assert_can_start`; injected collaborators that merely report `cost_units_spent` bypass the gate and stay bounded only by the between-cycle check, which is why the banked file's loop-level characterisation stays true and stays in place, its xfail rewritten as an explicit green scope statement. A host without a usage ledger gets prior behaviour unchanged.
- **Runtime verification 2026-08-22, on the operator's word, same conditions as the defect run.** A one-shot dry-run campaign with `--max-cost-units 8` — the exact envelope that previously leaked to 63. The envelope reached the moment of spend: the very first planner call was refused BEFORE dispatch — `ModelBudgetExceeded: run cost envelope would exhaust: 0+~36/8 before planner:openai/gpt-5.6-sol` — the runtime checkpointed gracefully (`resumable_checkpoint_paused`, `stop_reason=budget_exhausted`), nothing crashed, and the campaign ended cleanly by its own no-progress rule. **Total spend: 0 against a cap of 8, versus 63 against the same cap before the fix.** Operational corollary now measured rather than assumed: one planner call estimates at ~36 units, so a campaign envelope below that cannot buy a single model call — a working run needs a budget sized above one call, and the number 8 that used to mean "63" now means "0".
- **Status:** `fixed`. **Why it matters beyond a campaign flag:** for an agent intended to be left running, "do not start the next cycle after an overspend" is only a budget if one cycle has its own bounded spend. Now it does, on the real spend path.

### MIR-115 — the memory that steers later answers is 93% unverified self-talk
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-114 (the freeze it bears on), MIR-100, MIR-002/041/046. **Provenance:** measured 2026-08-20 against the live store, not read from code.
- **The question it answers.** Can experience from an early cycle change a decision at a much later one? That is the only thing separating an agent that accumulates capability from one that accumulates megabytes. The answer today is yes, it can — and what it will carry is mostly unverified.
- **Measured on `data/episodic_memory.jsonl`, 200 episodes spanning 2026-07-28 to 2026-08-20:** 136 are `usage_eligible=True`. Of those 136, **127 (93%) carry zero verified chunks**, and all 127 are admitted by one rule. Only **9 of 136** would pass the strict rule the docstring describes. Among the eligible: 35 success, 84 partial, **17 failed**.
- **The mechanism, quoted rather than paraphrased.** `core/smart_memory.py::decide_usage_eligibility` documents its gate as completion achieved AND outcome success AND `verified_chunks > 0` AND not a replay — then, before any of those checks, `if "lesson" in episode.tags: return True`. An unconditional bypass. The read side repeats the exemption: `core/loop_memory_read.py:260` and `:270` waive the outcome and completion checks for the same tag.
- **Who mints the tag.** `core/self_build_memory.py:122` writes `tags = ["self-build", "lesson", kind, status, outcome]` on **every** self-build episode regardless of status or outcome, and `core/self_repair.py:504` tags its lessons likewise. So the machinery that produces the content also attaches the label that exempts it from verification. Self-granting, which is the shape MIR-100 names.
- **A false claim in the code, now corrected.** The comment at `agent_tick.py` asserted that unattended episodes are written `usage_eligible=False` — "wiring operational, usage quarantined". The store says otherwise; the comment is replaced with the measurement.
- **One tag, five meanings — and this is the defect rather than the numbers.** `lesson` is also in `EpisodicMemoryStore.PROTECTED_TAGS` (`core/smart_memory.py:490`) and earns `+50` on thematic retrieval (`:593`). So a single metadata token now asserts: this content is a lesson; it may influence the future; it need not be successful, completed or verified; it must be kept in preference to everything else; and it must surface above ordinary episodes. **The producer attaches the label and the label confers the authority** — the same shape as MIR-110's boolean, one level up: not evidence conflated with verification, but a semantic class conflated with the right to govern later behaviour.
- **What is NOT the defect.** Learning from failure is correct and must stay. The error is that *the fact of a failure* and *the reliability of the lesson drawn from it* are different claims. "I tried X and it collapsed" is a sound episode. "Therefore the cause was Y and in future always do Z" is a separate assertion that may be false. The `lesson` tag lets the second inherit the standing of the first with nothing in between. The docstring calls the exception a **curated** lesson; `core/self_build_memory.py:122` curates nothing — it writes the tag on every self-build episode automatically.
- **A second, provable consequence: the cap becomes decorative.** `_maybe_prune_unlocked` computes `to_remove` from the TOTAL but may only evict unprotected rows (`kept = evictable[to_remove:] + protected`). Once protected rows exceed `max_episodes` the slice is empty and the store keeps the protected set alone — permanently above its cap, with every further protected write raising the floor. **Measured, not read:** with `max_episodes=5`, thirty protected episodes leave **thirty** rows in the store; thirty ordinary ones leave five. Since every self-build pass mints a protected row, the protected set has no ceiling. Banked by `tests/test_protected_episodes_cannot_outgrow_the_cap.py` (strict xfail, with the boundary pin that pruning works normally when rows are evictable). Note the two numbers are different evidence: the thirty-row figure is a measurement, while the test pins the smaller nine-against-five case — enough to fix the mechanism, and not a claim that thirty is what the suite guards.
- **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-22 — every figure is IDENTICAL: 200 episodes, 136 eligible, 127 (93%) with zero verified chunks, 9 passing the strict rule, 35 success / 84 partial / 17 failed.** Not one record moved in two days. Read with its caveat: activity in that window was minimal (6 model calls on 08-20, 6 on 08-21, none on 08-22), so this establishes stability, not saturation.
- **The 'unconditional bypass' sentence above is now STALE, and correcting it exposes something worse.** The code no longer reads `if "lesson" in episode.tags: return True`. `_answer_disqualified(episode)` runs BEFORE the exemption, and the exemption itself was narrowed to waive only what it always promised to waive. MIR-121 recorded that correction on 2026-08-22; this entry, the one the claim originates from, still carried the old wording — the echo outlived the fact.
- **AND THE NARROWING IS INERT ON THE STORE IT WAS WRITTEN FOR. Measured, not inferred.** Eligibility is decided at WRITE time and frozen: `decide_usage_eligibility` is called **zero** times on the read path, and the read-side filter `is_usage_eligible` does nothing but return `episode.usage_eligible is True` — the stored bit, whose own docstring in `core/loop_memory_read.py` admits it "was decided by whatever rule was" in force when the row was written. Of the 127 zero-verified eligible rows, **126 were created before the narrowing landed** (they span 2026-07-28 to 2026-08-20; exactly one is dated on or after it). Every one of them carries `lesson`, so every one is protected and cannot be evicted. **The 93% will therefore not fall by itself, and the repair that was supposed to lower it cannot touch a single existing row.**
- **This is a CLASS, not an accident, and naming it is the useful part.** It is precisely MIR-058's shape one level up: "MIR-057's gates are forward-only. They stopped new credit; they did not unwind credit already granted." Here a forward-only gate sits over durable records that are *also* exempt from eviction, so the population it was meant to shrink is frozen twice over — once by the write-time decision it cannot revisit, once by the protection that stops the FIFO reaching it. Any future repair in this family must state, before it is called done, which existing rows it changes and how they can leave the store; a gate alone changes a number only for records that do not exist yet.
- **Population changed 2026-08-22 evening (`ae7134a`), after the re-measurement above:** the gate-wait demotion (`scripts/demote_gate_wait_lessons.py`) removed the 64 wait-rows from the lesson population — store 142, eligible 72, of them 63 (87%) with zero verified chunks, protected 63. **The entry's core stands:** the bypass still admits every remaining genuine-attempt lesson unverified, and the 87% differs from the old 93% only because the spam left the denominator. What changed is that the rows are now REACHABLE: wait-records lost the tag through a write-side rule plus an executable migration, which is exactly the shape this entry's forward-only note demands of any further repair.
- **Provenance caveat.** The 200/136/127/9 figures are a measurement of the local `data/episodic_memory.jsonl`, and `data/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:25`), so they cannot be verified through the published repository. What CAN be verified there is the causal mechanism, which is entirely in published code.
- **ПЕРЕЗАМЕРЕНО 2026-08-25, и форма держится.** Хранилище ужалось со 200 эпизодов до **142** (прошла уборка), пригодных к подбору стало **72** вместо 136, и **63 из них (87 %)** по-прежнему несут НОЛЬ проверенных кусков. Падение 93 % → 87 % — не починка: убыло эпизодов, а не неверенной памяти.
- **Кто вправе рулить — девять из 142.** Проверенные куски есть ровно у девяти пригодных (по 3–5); восемь из них — цели кампаний, которые действительно что-то доставали и проверяли, одна — реплика оператора. Остальные 63 пригодны к подбору, не будучи проверены ничем.
- **Хранилище стоит с 2026-08-20.** Новейшая запись — того же дня, что и прежний замер: за пять суток не добавилось ни одного эпизода, что совпадает с молчанием ленты кампаний и 150-часовой давностью отметок в реестре источников.
- **Побочный урок метода.** Перезамер едва не опубликовал ложный ноль: список ключей записи я напечатал срезом первых шестнадцати по алфавиту, `verified_chunks` в срез не попал, и это прочиталось как «поля нет вовсе». Поле есть у всех 142. Отображение — не данные; ноль на отсутствующем поле и ноль в поле неразличимы, пока не спросить прямо.
- **Status:** `open`. Deliberately unfixed under the freeze: narrowing the exemption is a decision about what the agent may learn from, and it belongs with MIR-110's evidence taxonomy rather than to a quick edit. What is recorded is the number, so that the experiment MIR-114 exists to enable is not run against an instrument that cannot tell accumulation of capability from accumulation of self-talk.

### MIR-114 — the decision subject is wrong: code decides where the agent should
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-110 (its self-repair special case), MIR-100. **Provenance:** declared by the operator 2026-08-20 after reading the repository; every site below re-verified against the code before this entry was written.
- **THIS IS THE BLOCKING ARCHITECTURAL ISSUE OF THE PROJECT. ALL OTHER DEVELOPMENT IS SUBORDINATE TO IT.** An architectural freeze is in force — see `docs/audit/AUTONOMY_FREEZE.md` for the full declaration, the permitted work, and the condition that lifts it.
- **The defect.** The system was designed as an autonomous agent, and over months a large share of its executive decisions was written into the code by humans and by LLM developers: the task agenda, the roles, the utility ranking, the study order, the self-improvement reflex. Three unlike classes are conflated — **Constitution** (what the owner must impose: kill switch, no self-issued credentials, budget ceiling, no self-widening), **Capability** (tools and building blocks given in advance), and **Decision** (what matters now, which organisation to build, whom to retire, what to study). The third class has leaked into the code.
- **Nine verified sites**, each read in the file named: `core/best_next_action.py:36` (a utility function written as numbers, largest weight wins); `core/autonomous_runtime.py:950` (the queue always opens with two prescribed tasks); `core/learning_planner.py:181` and `:366` (a price list for attention, plus a hardcoded core-file set); `core/subagent_registry.py:50` (five default roles, and a registry that recommends but never hires or fires); `core/team_plan.py:233` (a second predefined workforce from keywords); `core/autonomous_runtime.py:74` (`spawn_subagent` blocked on the unattended path — roles a human wrote may run, a worker the agent decides it needs may not be created); `core/subagent_runner.py:380` (a subagent is one-shot, `memory=None`, `persistent_store=None`); `core/charter_goal.py:14` (the goal must anchor to a verbatim line of a human-written document — a one-time push replaced by a permanent one stored in a file); `agent_tick.py:122` (unattended memory reduced to a two-entry write allowlist).
- **The invariant to restore:** hardcoded code may define safety constraints and generic capabilities, but must not prescribe the director's workforce, task agenda, organisational topology, or utility ranking. Every such choice must be traceable to the director's own deliberation, evidence and retained experience.
- **A second invariant, same root — WHO LIVES: all production functions must belong to one canonical autonomous agent, not to several independently assembled cognitive instances; restart recovers that same agent rather than creating a new one.** **Do not solve this by persisting a transient identifier:** `new_trace_id()` is a random per-session trace id and must keep changing. Two layers — an Agent ID (who lived, absent today) above trace/run/cycle ids (what it did, present and rightly changing). Sharpest evidence: **one tick constructs up to three agents** (`agent_tick.py:946`, `:680`, `:1165`) and the campaign lane a fourth (`:1352`); the comment that all three build sites must stay identical maintains equivalence between instances, which is not continuity of one. Not "one process never dies" (the subject must survive a reboot, a crash, an update and its own repair) and not "thinks continuously" (**alive is not the same as calling a model**; a cheap idle is correct, and autonomy means no human decides when to leave it). A short-lived process CAN load durable state and continue an identity — the narrower true finding is that each tick builds a NEW in-memory `AgentLoop` with no continuous owner of working state between ticks, so continuity is reconstructed or lost, and on that path it is largely lost. **Measured:** Diagnostic and test harnesses may exist; they are not alternative production identities or autonomous execution roots. Verified today: four sites construct an agent of their own (`agent_tick.py`, `api/server.py`, `cli/one_shot.py`, `cli/app.py`), three more executables delegate (`main.py`, `app/windows_service.py`, `docker/daemon_loop.py`), and the installed production path — `scripts/install_daemon.ps1` — registers a Windows Scheduled Task that runs `agent_tick.py` **every 30 minutes**. The agent's life is a new process every half hour rather than a subject that persists, which is also why memory is largely switched off on that path. Meanwhile `app/runtime_cli.py` lets the human choose between auto-run, work-session and campaign with flags: the human choosing in which manner the agent shall be autonomous today. Recorded, not acted on — no launcher, service, window or messaging adapter is to be built now. The criterion for any future design: the machine is on, therefore the agent is alive.
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-22, and the installed reality differs from the described one.** This entry states the production path is a Windows Scheduled Task running `agent_tick.py` every 30 minutes. That is what `scripts/install_daemon.ps1` installs (`-IntervalMinutes` default 30, confirmed at `:44` and `:107`); it is not what is installed on this host. Measured over all **201** registered tasks: exactly one invokes this project — `AgentCharterCampaign`, running `agent_tick.py --campaign --charter` — and it is **Disabled**. A second, `AutonomousAgentTickStopAfter48h`, is a spent kill-switch whose action is `Disable-ScheduledTask -TaskName 'AutonomousAgentTick'`; it last ran 2026-07-06 with result 0 and has no next run, and the task it disabled no longer exists on the host. **Nothing currently schedules the agent to run at all.** The 'new process every half hour' this entry describes is therefore the DESIGNED life, not the current one — which does not weaken the entry's argument about identity, but does mean any statement about how the agent lives today must be taken from the scheduler rather than from the installer script. Full measurement, including the instrument failure that nearly reversed it, in `docs/audit/SELF_BUILD_FAILURE_ANALYSIS.md` Addendum 2.
- **Classified 2026-08-21 (`docs/audit/WHO_SHOULD_OWN_THE_AGENT.md`):** of the seven sites that construct an agent, four are organs of one agent (queue drain, self-build producer, hygiene, campaign) and three are interfaces to one (HTTP API, interactive shell, one-shot). **Genuinely separate agents: none.** So consolidation is subordination, not redesign. And the mechanism that produced the multiplicity is named in the code itself — `core/loop_init.py:93`: "Memory permissions (INSTANCE-scoped, fixed for this agent's life) … set once at construction and there is deliberately no per-run API yet", repeated for `durable_writes` at `core/loop_memory_write.py:109`. **The instance IS the unit of permission**, so a constructor call is the only way the code can say "this pass may delete" or "this one touches my own source". Precondition, not a design: subordinating an organ to one host would silently widen that organ's rights, so a per-run permission envelope must exist before one agent can carry organs with different ones.
- **The matrix is finished (`docs/audit/CONTAINMENT_BOUNDARY_MATRIX.md`, 2026-08-21), and the precondition is far larger than three flags.** All 36 `AgentLoop` constructor parameters were classified by one test — is there a line where a different value causes a REFUSAL. **26 are containment boundaries; 28 must become run-scoped.** Not only the memory flags: `registry`, `policy`, `approval_provider`, `gateway_dry_run`, `verifier_enabled`, `write_policy`, `odd_enabled` and the stores each carry a named refusal site. Two structural findings come with it. (1) Today's run-scoping is **save-and-restore mutation of a shared object** (`core/autonomous_runtime.py:1007` and `:1036`), correct only while one run holds it — under a shared host one organ's `finally` lifts another organ's block mid-run, silently and without a log. **Demonstrated 2026-08-21, not argued:** `tests/test_overlapping_runs_do_not_leak_authority.py` drives two runs into a fixed interleaving with events — A enters, B enters and snapshots A's set, A returns and its `finally` restores what A found — and B, still running, is left with an EMPTY block set. Measured at the real refusal site, with a registered read-only stub carrying a blocked name so the gate's answer has one possible cause: **before any run `allow`; inside B's own run `deny`; after A's cleanup, with B still running, `allow` and the block set `[]`**. That is effective authority widening mid-run, not merely a state leak — an earlier version of this proof only showed the latter and its commit message overstated it. The sequential control beside it stays green, so the file says the leak appears at overlap rather than saying mutation is bad in general. Banked strict-xfail; no implementation proposed. **A second violation of the same rule, found 2026-08-21 and banked beside it:** a run's envelope may only NARROW, and in the same block of `_task_goal` two adjacent fields disagree. `suppress_durable_learning_writes` is assigned `previous or config.dry_run` — monotone, a run may add suppression and never remove it. `gateway_dry_run` is assigned `bool(config.dry_run)` outright, so a task configured with `dry_run=False` turns OFF a host that was in dry-run. Measured: host `True` -> inside the run `False`, while the neighbouring field stays `True`. The code already contains the correct shape next to the incorrect one. Proofs in `tests/test_a_run_carries_its_own_envelope.py`. (2) The one child agent this codebase builds is already narrowed by `registry` alone while `core/subagent_runner.py:610` hands it the PARENT's `policy`, whose gate still holds the parent's full registry; three unreconciled registry references exist per agent.
- **First production change, 2026-08-21 — authorised scope only: `policy.blocked_tools` and `gateway_dry_run`.** The chosen form is not the cosmetic `previous or config`, which would have closed proof 6 and left proof 7 red because the shared-field mutation would survive. Instead the two axes moved onto the run itself, using the `ContextVar` model that already existed: `RunContext` gained `blocked_tools` and `dry_run`; `run_restrictions()` unions and ORs them so a scope can only narrow; `run_scope()` inherits what is in force so minting a fresh run id cannot shed a limit; `PolicyGate.check` consults `host | run`; the gateway is built with `host or run`. `_task_goal` no longer writes either field on the agent. Effective authority is now `host ceiling ∩ current run`, computed at the sites that refuse. **Transitions, as predicted:** proofs 8, 2, 3 stayed green; proofs 6 and 7 went XFAIL → XPASS and their markers were removed. Full suite 8318 passed, 15 xfailed, lint at 113.
- **The status, stated at the scale the instruments support and no wider.** Not "run-scoped authority solved" and not "consolidation is safe", but: *for `blocked_tools` and `gateway_dry_run`, a run-local, inherited, non-liftable form of effective authority is proven, computed without shared mutate/restore, and it removes the previously reproduced sequential host-ceiling leak and the concurrent cross-run leak.* Nothing about the other axes follows from it.
- **The fork got an experimental answer, which is why the more expensive form was worth it.** `previous or config.dry_run` would genuinely have fixed property 6 — it was not a bad line, it was a local one. The `RunContext` form turned out to be the common cause of both 6 and 7, so the cost was justified by two independently measured defects rather than by architectural taste. That is the only kind of argument that should buy a bigger change.
- **A third axis carries the same shape and is banked, not fixed (2026-08-21).** `planner.hidden_tools` still does save -> mutate shared field -> finally restore. Premise measured first: hiding a tool changes the LIVE advertised surface — `_build_user_prompt` lists the registry minus `hidden_tools` and adds an `[UNAVAILABLE_TOOLS=…]` directive; 15 tools became 14 and the directive appeared. Overlap driven by events: B inside its own run sees the tool hidden and the directive present; after A's cleanup, B — still running — is advertised the full surface again with no directive. **What it does not prove:** the tool does not become callable. `PolicyGate` now computes `host | run`, so the gate still answers deny, measured in the same experiment. What IS proven is exactly the change in advertised surface and directive, mid-run, and nothing more. **UNPROVEN and not to be read in:** that any planning is wasted or that a replan occurs — the experiment never calls `Planner.plan` (the run is a stub that records and raises), so no tool is ever selected; that a wider surface leads the planner to pick a forbidden tool is an inference from the directive's stated purpose, not a measurement. Bounded likewise: before the policy axis moved, such a proposal would have PASSED THE GATE — whether it would then have executed depends on the gateway, the approval path and the tool, none of which this experiment touches. Banked by `tests/test_overlapping_runs_do_not_leak_the_tool_surface.py`.
- **Two things that change nothing about the remaining scope.** The other 26 run-scoped axes from the matrix are untouched — they migrate by TYPE (authority separately from context and tuning), not in one sweep, and `logger` or `max_replan_attempts` are not permission envelopes however convenient the container. And the four organs are not consolidated: this change proves the form removes two demonstrated defect classes, nothing more.
- **A gap in the instruments, found by breaking the new mechanism rather than by review.** Rewriting `run_restrictions` to REPLACE instead of union left every proof green: a single scope entered and exited behaves identically either way. Only nesting tells them apart, and nothing tested nesting. Two cases were added and the same break now reddens them.
- **Sequential reusability measured 2026-08-21 — and it is NOT an answer to WHO LIVES.** The first version of this entry said it was, which broke the rule written into the freeze the same day: a trace id is what the agent DID at a moment, never who lived. Corrected, in three registers.
  - **PROVEN:** an existing `AgentLoop` can be reused for sequential work on the measured stubbed / dry-run path, and reuse itself raises no immediate lifecycle conflict. One instance ran an autonomous pass, a hygiene pass, the self-build organ and two further passes with no failure. The self-build organ needed no persuading — it already accepts `build_agent_fn`.
  - **OBSERVED:** three chosen in-process indicators did not change across that work — `memory is None`, the length of `compensation_log`, `last_replan_exhausted`.
  - **UNPROVEN, and not to be read in:** canonical subject continuity; equivalence of reuse and rebuild; that no significant state transfers; the full set of production organs (campaign is never called); continuity across a restart. The unchanged session trace id shows one logger was reused, not that one subject persisted — there is no agent identity here for a test to observe.
  - The useful sentence is the narrow one: **no prohibition on reusing one `AgentLoop` was found.** Recorded by `tests/test_one_agent_can_already_carry_sequential_work.py`, whose second test fails the day one of those three indicators finally accumulates.
- **WHO DECIDES, first measurement 2026-08-21 — ranking authority at one named site.** Held the observable facts fixed so two candidates were admissible at once (a tick errored AND tests were failing), then changed ONLY the relation between two developer-written constants. As written, `investigate_tick_error` wins (`_P_TICK_ERROR=90` over `_P_TESTS_FAIL=80`); with `_P_TESTS_FAIL` raised above it and **nothing about the world changed**, the winner becomes `propose_minimal_test_repair`. Selection is literally `max(active, key=lambda c: c.priority)` at `core/best_next_action.py:319`. **What this licenses and no more:** at this boundary the agent does not determine the relative value of two available actions — a table in Python does. It does NOT license "autonomy is theatre" or "signals are ignored": observation decides which candidates EXIST, pinned by the third test — remove the tick error and the other candidate wins on the same unchanged table. Recorded by `tests/test_ranking_authority_belongs_to_the_table.py`.
- **WHO DECIDES, second measurement 2026-08-21 — admission and ordering, upstream of ranking.** Before asking who decided which file is worth studying, ask who decided that studying happens now. `AutonomousRuntime._build_queue` (`core/autonomous_runtime.py:951`) opens every run with `status` then `learn` and adds three more by flag. **Premise, parsed rather than eyeballed: the method makes ZERO references to `self`** — no agent state, no workspace, no memory, no observation can reach it; it is a pure function of the config, reading exactly `goal`, `include_goal`, `include_proposals`, `include_tests`. The config carries no switch for learning: `learning_limit` and `learning_writes_memory` govern HOW, never WHETHER, and the only way `learn` does not run is `[: config.limit]` truncating it off the end — position, not judgement. **Consequence: the list governs real work** — as written `status` and `learn` execute; replace only the recipe and `learn` never runs. **Claimed, and one task only:** at this boundary developer-written code holds admission and ordering authority over at least the `learn` task, and offers the agent no way to decide whether that work is justified now. **Not claimed:** anything about `status`, which may well be a platform procedure rather than an executive decision; this measurement takes no position on it. Recorded by `tests/test_who_admits_the_learn_task.py`.
- **Census instrument designed 2026-08-21, ЗАПУЩЕН 2026-08-24** — `docs/audit/DECISION_AUTHORITY_CENSUS_METHOD.md`, запуск: `scripts/decision_authority_census.py`. Two causally measured sites do not license a claim about the system, so the denominator needs an instrument built before the number. It carries: four counting levels that may not be extrapolated between (site / candidate / measured / classified); detector family A (literals in a selection, high-recall by design, specificity belongs to the classifier) and family B (provenance and repertoire closure, seven axes, conjunctive verdict but non-short-circuit diagnosis); the rule that **closure is a property of the provenance graph, not the consumer's shape**; a known-positive the detector must MISS, so the blind class is measured rather than asserted; vacancy computed only under a ratified operator criterion, `ratified` binary because `inferred` is the name of the defect; and the proof order A–F where `E` (experience) is meaningless before `D` (ownership). Also records three ways to get a false green on learning, the third being **human-deferred authorship** — a human choice at t1 stored as an episode with no decision-author field, changing an outcome at t2 and reading as learning. Measured en route: the episode record has no author field (proven), `requested_by` in the approval inbox is a constant component name, and `LLMPlanner` exists as a candidate deliberative capability whose ownership is unmeasured. Known proven Agent Decision boundaries: 0. System-wide cardinality: UNKNOWN.
- **ПЕРВЫЙ ПРОГОН ПЕРЕПИСИ, 2026-08-24 — семейство A, уровень «след детектора» и только он.** Прибор пролежал спроектированным три дня и был поднят по вопросу оператора «забросил старую проверку».

  **Число: 78 следов в 40 файлах** — A1 (литеральный приоритет) 26, A2 (упорядоченный литеральный список) 43, A3 (ключ сортировки с литералом) 7, A4 (ранжирующее сравнение) 2.

  **Контроли отработали как предписано.** Оба положительных найдены: `_build_queue` и таблица приоритетов кандидатов. Известный положительный ПРОПУЩЕН, как и требовалось, — закрытый репертуар кандидатов невидим семейству A по построению, потому что это решение как ОТСУТСТВИЕ альтернативы. Слепой класс тем самым измерен, а не заявлен.

  **Первое число было 163, и публиковать его было нельзя.** Метод требует выбирать ложные срабатывания РУКАМИ до публикации. Выборка из 16: верных 2, спорных 1, **ложных 13 — 81 %**, и почти все ложные одного рода — словари сопоставления (`_LEARNING_TERMS`, `_APPROVAL_STRONG`, `_PLACEHOLDER_TLDS`), а не списки работ; A4 ловил обрезку вывода (`len(goals) > 5`). Детектор сужен по НАЙДЕННОМУ: словари исключены, A4 требует имени ранга. 163 → 83. Вторая выборка из 14 нашла три оставшихся класса ложных — экспорт `__all__`, словарь расширений и ресурсный потолок `_ORDER_TOKEN_CAP`, пойманный по совпадению имени; исключены и они. 83 → **78**.

  **Чего это число НЕ значит, и это не оговорка, а часть прибора.** Не «78 решений». Не кандидаты, не измеренные, не классифицированные. Экстраполяция между четырьмя уровнями запрещена методом, и знаменателем системной власти это число не является: оно знаменатель ТОГО, ЧТО НАШЛИ ДЕТЕКТОРЫ. Семейство B (провенанс и замкнутость репертуара, семь осей) не запускалось вовсе.

  **Известные проверенные границы Agent Decision: по-прежнему 0.** Системная мощность: по-прежнему UNKNOWN. Прогон дал знаменатель первого уровня, а не ответ.

- **Why it outranks everything else:** a better memory would remember imposed decisions more accurately; better subagents would execute an imposed organisation more efficiently; better self-repair would maintain a system still travelling an imposed trajectory. Improving the parts cannot fix a wrongly located decision subject.
- **Status:** `open`. Lift condition, deliberately not "the code looks more autonomous": an end-to-end demonstration of observe -> retrieve experience -> identify gap -> form candidate goals -> choose -> create the needed capability -> execute within authority -> verify -> record -> the experience changes a later decision; with the provenance of every significant decision answerable, and the agent able to conclude **no justified action** on a fresh state instead of performing arranged activity — in the narrow sense only: no justified expensive or effectful action RIGHT NOW, so observe and stay cheap. It must never mean "no assignment arrived, therefore my activity is over"; that would be a stop state and would rebuild the dependence on a human hand this entry exists to remove.

### MIR-113 — four fail-safe branches, four unwitnessed safety boundaries
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-109, MIR-112 (the same shape: a property with no witness). **Provenance:** the safety-class survivors of the 2026-08-20 mutation sweep; all four closed the same day. Production code was correct in every case.
- Each of these branches exists to make the modest claim when something is unresolvable. Each was inverted by a mutation, and the whole suite stayed green.

| Site | The branch | What the inversion would do | Closed by |
|---|---|---|---|
| `core/loop_step_execution.py` `_step_only_reads` | a tool whose `risk_for` RAISES is not read-only | the step joins the concurrent batch, so a step that may write anything buys concurrency — the contract three lines above says the opposite | a case added beside its siblings in `tests/test_integration.py`, which already pinned the missing-tool and empty-spec branches but not this one |
| `cli/intent_bridge.py` `_handle_local_operator_reply` | no reply produced means not handled | both call sites treat True as "turn consumed", so the operator's message is swallowed and answered with nothing | `tests/test_the_bridge_claims_only_what_it_did.py` |
| `cli/intent_bridge.py` `_model_says_conversation` | no model to consult means keep the deterministic route | a missing or broken planner model becomes a veto of every gated intent — the operator's command silently does nothing | same file |
| `cli/intent_bridge.py` `_tokens_overlap` | a shared six-character prefix is overlap | inverted, any six-character DIFFERENCE counts, and a stop order aimed at one task cancels the rest of the queue | same file |

- **Each closed with both directions shown:** on the mutation the test that pins that branch goes red while its siblings stay green; on HEAD all pass. The two boundary pins matter as much as the assertions — a bridge that claimed nothing, or a matcher that never overlapped, would satisfy the negative tests while making the feature dead.
- **Status:** `fixed`. **The pattern worth carrying:** a fail-safe branch is exactly where tests are least likely to exist, because nothing goes wrong when it works. Three of the four are one function's worth of code and none had ever been executed with the failure they guard against.

### MIR-112 — reflection's recency window had no witness, and reversing it would be silent
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-015 (an instrument reporting the wrong thing), MIR-110. **Provenance:** found 2026-08-20 by mutation; closed the same day. Production code was correct throughout.
- `ReflectionEngine._load_recent_logs` sorts `*.jsonl` by mtime and keeps `config.max_logs` of them. Flipping `reverse=True` to `reverse=False` survived the ENTIRE suite. Reversed, the slice returns the OLDEST files: `core/autonomous_runtime.py` always passes a bounded rotated window (20, 40, 30, 60) and the live log directory holds 1229 files, so the agent would re-read its first few dozen runs forever — deriving lessons about failures fixed months ago, never seeing anything recent — while the report still reads `logs_scanned=20, events_scanned=N, patterns_found=[...]`, because `logs_scanned` counts files AFTER truncation. Plausible numbers, dead signal.
- **Why nothing caught it:** every fixture in the suite writes exactly ONE log file, so the window and its ordering are exercised only where both are no-ops. The token `max_logs` appeared in **0 of 552 test files**.
- **Closed by** `tests/test_reflection_reads_the_newest_logs_not_the_oldest.py`: five files with strictly increasing mtimes, a window of two, and the events must come from the newest two — plus a pin that a window wider than the directory truncates nothing. Red on the mutation, green on HEAD, and the pin holds in both worlds. The invariant is recency, not a number, so retuning the window does not redden it.
- **Status:** `fixed`. Worth remembering next to MIR-110: both are the learning loop reporting healthy numbers about the wrong material.

### MIR-111 — two parsers, one URL: the step pre-filter did not see the host that would be dialled
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-109 (a guard watching the wrong axis), MIR-100. **Provenance:** found 2026-08-20 by mutation — the mutant was the more correct of the two, which is why nothing was red; fixed the same day.
- `core/step_sanitizer.py::_url_host` stripped userinfo with `split("@", 1)`, taking everything after the FIRST `@`. Every URL parser in real use ends userinfo at the LAST one. Measured: for `http://user:pass@evil.com@169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/` the parser's hostname is `169.254.169.254` while the pre-filter computed `evil.com@169.254.169.254` — not an IP literal, so `_is_local_network_host` said no, and a step aimed at the cloud metadata endpoint read as ordinary traffic. The same URL without the extra `@` was correctly refused.
- **Severity, measured rather than assumed.** `tools/network_safety.py` parses the same URL correctly and refuses all variants, so the defence in depth held: this was a pre-filter bypass, not an open door. What is worth naming is the shape — two parsers in one codebase disagreeing about what the host is, which is how filter bypasses are built.
- **Fixed** by `rsplit("@", 1)`, but the one character is not the protection. The durable part is `tests/test_the_prefilter_sees_the_host_that_will_be_dialled.py`, which asserts AGREEMENT — whatever the pre-filter thinks the host is must equal `urllib.parse.urlsplit(url).hostname` — over a family of adversarial authorities, plus a pin that an ordinary public URL is still not local. Red first: 4 of 12. Regression: 1323 tests across the 47 files touching the sanitizer or the web tools stay green.
- **Status:** `fixed`. **Why the suite could not see it:** every userinfo case in the suite used a single `@`. The tests encoded the example, not the rule.

### MIR-110 — the repair gate's "verified diagnosis" only checks that pytest came back
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-100 (a proxy taking on the authority of the thing it stands for), MIR-108, MIR-109. **Provenance:** found 2026-08-20 by tracing the governance chain end to end after the test-suite audit; banked, not fixed — the semantics belong to the operator.
- **The chain, traced rather than assumed.** `core/governance.py::_repair` spends real authority on one input: an unverified diagnosis is DENIED an `APPLY_CODE_CHANGE`, and may only `PROPOSE_DIFF` once verified. `core/self_repair.py` is the live consumer — `governance.denied` becomes a `governance_denied` step, `requires_approval` goes to the approval queue — and it computes that input as `_diagnosis_verified(baseline.output)`, which is `output.get("timed_out") is False`.
- **Measured, three worlds:** baseline green (nothing failed) -> `verified=True` -> diff `allow`; baseline red (defect reproduced) -> `verified=True` -> diff `allow`; baseline timed out -> `verified=False` -> write `deny`. The gate separates exactly one thing — whether the test command returned. It cannot tell a reproduced defect from no defect at all, which is the distinction its name promises.
- **Not a claim that the agent repairs healthy code.** Other gates (empty diff, confidence floor) stop most of that path, and a human still approves the write. The claim is narrower and worse: the word "verified" in a safety decision is carried by a proxy that never checked.
- **Why nothing caught it.** The consumer-side test (`tests/test_self_repair_controller_branches.py:203`) injects a stub policy that always denies, proving the controller honours a denial — a sound test of the wire, silent about the rule. The policy-side deny branch had no test with `evidence_verified=False` at all; that half is closed today by `tests/test_the_repair_gate_needs_both_its_reasons.py` (red on the break: 1 of 4, exactly the assertion that pins it). Two sound halves either side of an unexamined middle — the same shape as MIR-109.
- **A candidate taxonomy, forwarded by the operator 2026-08-20 and recorded here as a proposal — NOT adopted, because choosing it is the decision this entry is about.** `timed_out is False` is at most `experiment_completed`, and turning that into `diagnosis_verified` is the step to forbid. What class of evidence earns the right to repair depends on the claim: a REPRODUCED FAILURE (the named failure re-run under control); a DETERMINISTIC INVARIANT VIOLATION (a checkable invariant shown broken); BOUNDED NEGATIVE EVIDENCE (for a claim that something is absent — the search space is stated, covered, and the mechanism is not in it); or a GROUNDED CLAIM (a citation or structural exhibit that actually entails the claim). The distinction that makes the taxonomy load-bearing: **a citation may ground a diagnosis without verifying it.** The chain would then read claim -> evidence type -> does this evidence support THIS claim -> discriminating result -> verified / unverified / unknown -> governance, instead of today's `pytest returned -> verified -> allow`. Note the third class exists because a claim like "no mechanism X exists" may have no red test to show, only a coverage argument.
- **Banked** by `tests/test_a_verified_diagnosis_means_a_reproduced_defect.py`, strict xfail, with both boundaries pinned (a timeout is still not verified; a reproduced failure still is). **Status:** `banked`. The direction is now decided (below); the implementation is open.

**The operator's ruling, 2026-08-20.** The question this entry asks is NOT "may the agent change itself". It is: *what evidence lets the agent judge a change well enough founded to use a right it already holds?* Three consequences, in the operator's own framing:

1. **This gate must not become another human-approval checkpoint.** A cycle of find-defect → ask → write-test → ask → edit → ask is not autonomy; it is a very clever CI with a secretary. The gate is to become a **provable entry condition into an autonomous repair lane**: a class of change, a level of risk, a type of evidence, a rollback, a regression run, and no widening of authority — that combination, once proven to work on live evidence, is what allows the human to step off *this level* without stepping out of governance.
2. **A boolean is the wrong shape.** `evidence_verified: bool` currently stands for four unlike situations — pytest finished, a citation exists, a defect was reproduced, an invariant was violated — plus a fifth it cannot express at all, that a mechanism is absent. The target shape carries the claim with its evidence: what is claimed, the evidence type, the observations, whether the evidence DISCRIMINATES between "defect present" and "defect absent", the scope actually proven, the confidence, and the alternatives left UNKNOWN. Today's own audit is the model of a strong case — mutation applied, test RED; HEAD, test GREEN — and `pytest returned before the timeout` is not evidence of a diagnosis at all.
3. **The boundary that stays human is constitutional, not operational.** The agent may not widen its own rights, disable the kill switch, alter human sovereignty, issue itself credentials, spend beyond its budget, open a new external channel, take an irreversible external action, or **rewrite the criterion by which it is allowed to rewrite itself**. Inside those limits, repairing a reproducible local defect, writing a test for a proven invariant, changing an implementation within its contract, rolling back its own failed edit, refactoring at proven equivalence, and choosing among already-permitted models and tools are engineering work that should migrate to the agent.

**Not yet implemented, and deliberately so.** Today's `REPAIR` mode still returns `require_approval` for `APPLY_CODE_CHANGE` even with evidence and rollback, and evolution writes in `IMPROVEMENT` also require a human. That conservatism stays while the system is young. What the ruling changes is the direction: the next increment is to make the evidence structured and the entry condition provable, not to add another place where the agent waits.

**One asymmetry worth keeping in view.** A failure is allowed to happen and to teach — but a digital system can destroy, in one failure, the very mechanism that was supposed to notice the next one. "Let it fall" therefore has to mean: the fall is reversible, the state is preserved, the cause is observable, rollback is available, and authority did not widen.

### MIR-109 — the only test on the safe opener's wiring watched a class name
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-108 (the same axis error, in the lane's git front-end), MIR-100. **Provenance:** found 2026-08-20 auditing the suite; confirmed against the whole suite, then closed the same day.
- **Measured, not argued.** `tools/network_safety.py` re-checks the ACTUAL connected peer IP inside the connection classes its handlers open — the defence against a second DNS answer that returns `169.254.169.254` after the hostname was approved. One test guarded that wiring, `tests/test_network_safety.py::test_build_safe_opener_installs_peer_ip_guard_handlers`, and it asserted that two class NAMES appear among the opener's handlers. Pointing the handlers at the unguarded stdlib connections left it green — **and left the entire suite green: 8237 passed, 3 skipped**, with the protection removed.
- **Why the neighbours did not cover it.** `_assert_peer_ip_global` is proven correct in isolation against a fake socket, and the URL-time policy is proven correct on hostnames. Both are sound; neither says the guard sits on the path a request takes, which is the only thing the attack cares about.
- **Production code was correct throughout.** The defect was in the instrument: the property had no witness.
- **Closed by** `tests/test_the_opener_guards_the_socket_it_opens.py`, which drives the opener with a socket that reports a private peer at connect time and asserts the refusal, plus a boundary pin that a public peer is not refused. Both halves demonstrated: on the bypassed build the new test fails on all four private peers while the old file's 27 tests stay green; on the correct build all 5 pass. It names no class, so a rename cannot redden it and a bypass cannot hide from it.
- **What is now witnessed, and nothing beyond it.** Six cases: four IPv4 private/link-local peers through the HTTP handler and two through the HTTPS handler, each refused at connect time, plus one pin that a public peer is still allowed to send. On the bypassed build 6 of the 7 go red; the seventh is the public-peer pin and is green by design in both worlds.
- **What is still unwitnessed — this is not "DNS rebinding is tested".** No test drives an IPv6 peer through the opener; no test rebinds mid-redirect; nothing exercises the case where `create_connection` tries several addresses. And one property is worse than untested: on the HTTPS path the guard runs AFTER `super().connect()`, so the TLS handshake with the hostile peer has already completed when the refusal arrives. Contact happened; only the request body did not. That ordering is recorded here, not fixed, and it is a separate decision.
- **Status:** `fixed` for the named failure world only. **The general shape, and it is the audit's main lesson:** a test that names the box instead of measuring what is inside is sensitive on exactly the wrong axis — red for a harmless rename, green for a removed defence.

### MIR-108 — four tests forbid four NAMES; the capability they stand for was never bounded
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-100 (a proxy acquiring semantic authority), the "an echo is not a second witness" lesson. **Provenance:** found 2026-08-20 auditing the test suite as an instrument; demonstrated live, then fixed the same day.
- **The claim four tests appeared to make.** `tests/test_safe_vcs.py:116`, `tests/test_self_apply_lane.py:344`, `tests/test_self_apply_approval_bridge.py:408` and `tests/test_self_build_producer.py:292` each assert `not hasattr(SafeVCS, name)` for `push` / `fetch` / `pull` / `remote` (± `clone`, `merge`). Read together they look like four independent guarantees that the self-apply lane cannot move code off the machine.
- **What they actually proved.** That four names are unbound. `SafeVCS` carried a general git executor — `_git(*args)` over an injectable `runner` — and every production construction used the default runner, so no call site narrowed it. Shown in a throwaway repository: with all four name assertions green, `SafeVCS(workspace=d)._git("remote", "-v")` returned 0. Four copies of one assertion are one witness, and that witness was watching the wrong axis: it goes red for a harmless rename and stays green for a real capability under a new name.
- **Not a report that the agent pushes code.** Nothing calls `_git` with a network verb today, and the class docstring says so deliberately. The defect is that the property rested on nobody choosing such a name.
- **Fixed here, at the capability**: `core/safe_vcs.py` now carries `_LOCAL_VERBS`, an allowlist of the eight subcommands the class issues, checked in `_git` before git is invoked, past any leading `-c key=value` pair that `commit` prepends. Red first: `tests/test_the_lane_cannot_reach_a_remote.py` failed 7 of 8 (the eighth, the boundary pin that local verbs still run, was green before and after by design), then all 8 passed; the 221 tests in the nine files that touch `SafeVCS` stayed green. The test uses a spy runner, because a test that let the verb run and checked for an error could not tell "refused" from "tried and failed".
- **Status:** `fixed`. The four name assertions are left in place — they are cheap, and they now sit behind a mechanism instead of standing in for one.

### MIR-107 — the mutation probe refused with a true message and a false cause
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-015 (an instrument reporting the wrong thing). **Provenance:** found 2026-08-20 when four audit agents hit it at once; fixed the same day.
- `scripts/mutation_probe.py` read any non-zero pytest exit code as "the selection is already red". pytest answers a path that does not exist with exit 4 — a usage error, nothing ran — and an empty selection with 5. A run launched with a mistyped test path was therefore refused with the message "the selection is already red — a survivor would mean nothing", sending the reader to debug failing tests that never existed. An accurate refusal with a false cause is worse than a crash.
- **Fixed**: `selection_state()` returns `green` / `red` / `uncollectable`, and `probe()` prints the cause that matches. Red first: `tests/test_the_probe_knows_why_it_refused.py`, three tests, all failing before the change. Behavioural pair, not just the new symbol — old: `run_tests(["missing.py"]) -> False`; new: `selection_state(["missing.py"]) -> "uncollectable"`, `selection_state(["tests/test_evidence.py"]) -> "green"`.
- **Status:** `fixed`. Worth keeping in mind for every other instrument here: a tool that collapses "could not measure" into "measured a failure" will keep producing confident nonsense.

### MIR-106 — a weak spot the agent cannot resolve is still recorded as studied
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-100 (a name acquiring authority without a check), MIR-105. **Provenance:** measured 2026-08-20 from live traces; **the first version of this entry was wrong and is corrected below.**
- **The correction, first, because it is the more useful finding.** The live traces show 10 of 12 named weak spots that do not exist as files — `core/reasoning.py`, `core/citation.py`, `core/user_contract.py` and others the model invented. I recorded that as "nothing checks the named weak spot exists". **That is false for the current code.** Commit `010bdd2` of 2026-08-15, "a lesson may not point at a file that does not exist", added `_checked_focus_area` in `core/reflection.py`, and **every one of the 10 phantoms is from 2026-08-15 — the same day**; none appears after it. Proven by intervention rather than by reading: `('core/reasoning.py', 'repair')` comes back `('', 'monitor')` — the focus is blanked and the repair intent downgraded — while `('tools/file_read.py', 'repair')` passes through untouched. I measured historical logs and attributed their behaviour to today's tree.
- **What remains, and it is narrower.** The guard only questions names that LOOK like paths (`_PATH_SHAPED_RE`).
