A topic-shaped focus area — `"memory subsystem"` — is **unresolvable by shape**, passes through unchanged, and reaches `LearningPlanner` as the goal "study weak areas — memory subsystem". Measured, and sharper than my first number: `"planner interface design"` returns the **same five sources, in the same order**, as naming no weak spot at all and as `"ZZZQQQ nonexistent topic 8811"`; `"memory subsystem"` differs from the default in 1 entry of 5. So naming a topic does not narrow the study — at best it perturbs the tail of a default list. The plan is then consumed and journalled as `reflection_learning_ingest sources=10 claims=80`, indistinguishable from a run that studied what it diagnosed.
- **Fixed here, at the record rather than the policy** (operator prerequisite, 2026-08-20): `core/reflection.py` `learning_grounding()` classifies each pass as `targeted` / `partial` / `substituted` / `invalid_diagnosis` / `unresolvable` / `none`, distinguishing a phantom PATH from a name no disk check can settle, and `ReflectionReport` carries it. A fallback may still happen; it may no longer be written down as the thing it replaced. Pinned by `tests/test_substituted_learning_is_not_recorded_as_targeted.py` (the classifier) and `tests/test_the_journal_says_whether_the_weak_spot_was_studied.py` (the runtime actually run: the journal line now reads `sources=2 claims=2 grounding=unresolvable unmet_targets=["memory subsystem"]`). Falsified by removing the payload key — `KeyError: 'grounding'`.
- **Status:** `partially_fixed`. Still open: nothing decides what the agent should DO when its self-diagnosis names something unresolvable — refuse, study anyway and say so, or raise the unresolvable name as its own defect signal. That is a policy the operator holds.
- **The general shape, worth more than the case.** A name the agent generates about its own anatomy — `core/reasoning.py`, which does not exist — is plausible enough that downstream machinery behaves as though the component were there. Before a self-diagnosis can be judged true or false, the thing it names has to be resolved; existence is not the diagnosis, but nothing can be checked before it.

### MIR-105 — the salience corpus is the agent's own command names, so ordinary words score like identifiers
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-015 (an instrument that fires on the wrong thing), MIR-050. **Provenance:** found 2026-08-20 while proving the cognitive-core edges by intervention — the control half of the experiment failed, and the failure was the finding.
- **Files/functions:** `core/loop_memory_read.py` `_question_salience()`; `core/smart_memory.py` `ProceduralMemoryStore.search_with_report()`; corpus built by `core/topic_tokens.py` `build_salience`.
- **The experiment.** A procedure was minted on the production path and then retrieved twice: once with the question it came from, once with an unrelated one. Both returned it, byte-identical, 392 characters. On the live store of 34 procedures the same shape holds: "what is the HTTP timeout for the web fetch tool?" returns three procedures whose top two are Russian operator sentences about learning to program. **CORRECTED 2026-08-22 — the Russian half of this paragraph is no longer true, and the defect is worse than the entry recorded.** The original text said a Russian question returns nothing because "Russian tokens are absent from the procedure haystacks". Measured today: **23 of 34 procedure names contain Cyrillic** (only 4 are the `Workflow using ...` shape), because operator sentences are minted as procedure names. So Russian is now inside the haystacks, and the query `«что мне делать с workflow using вот это всё»` — function words only — **retrieves 17 of the 34 procedures**, the top hits matching on nothing but `вот` / `что` / `мне`. The immunity was an artefact of what the store happened to contain on 2026-08-20, not a property of the mechanism; the admission gate fires in both languages.
- **The mechanism, in two halves, both measured.** `_question_salience()` documents itself as "how rarely the OPERATOR says a word — by their own questions", and deliberately excludes answers because "их писала модель". But the live corpus of 200 episode questions is **125 single-word command names** — `self-build-produce` 83 times, `self-apply-run` 20, `self-task-produce` 15 — plus a campaign-goal string repeated 10 times. It is the agent's own machine text. Model text was kept out of the front door and came in through the questions. Consequently an ordinary English word the corpus never saw gets the MAXIMUM weight: measured live, `tool`, `is` and `what` all score **5.303**, exactly what `wombat4412` scores, while `the` — common enough to have appeared — scores 1.869. **Quantified 2026-08-22:** the corpus can weight only **130 of the 587 words in the procedure vocabulary — 22%**. The other 78% are unseen and therefore all receive the identical maximum weight, so for most of the store the weighting is not merely flat but indistinguishable by construction. This is why passing a real corpus to `search_with_report` changes nothing: measured, `using`, `workflow` and `read_logs` all score 5.303, the `unseen` value. The remedy cannot be "wire the corpus through" — the corpus and the haystack share too little vocabulary for weighting to discriminate at all. The second half: `search_with_report` admits on `if score:`, with no threshold, so one maximally-weighted common word is enough.
- **Consequence.** On nearly any English question the planner's prompt receives three unrelated procedures presented as relevant experience. Whether that noise changes the planner's output is **UNKNOWN** — it needs a model call and is the same missing edge as MIR-100.
- **Status:** `open`. Pinned by `tests/test_experience_retrieval_admits_on_common_words.py`, which rebuilds the corpus shape deterministically rather than reading the live store: a boundary pin proves the wire carries a signal, and a strict xfail holds the invariant that a question sharing only common words must not retrieve a procedure.
- **ИЗМЕРЕНО НА ЖИВЫХ ДАННЫХ 2026-08-25, и один из трёх предложенных путей починки этим убит.**
- **Состав корпуса подтверждён числом.** 142 вопроса; **100 из них (70 %)** начинаются с ярлыка задачи самого агента (`self…` — 67, `campaign…` — 31), на речь оператора похожи 42. Докстринг `_question_salience` обещал «речь оператора»; строка исправлена по замеру.
- **Цена для обычного вопроса.** «почему тест падает и что с этим делать»: из шести слов темы **три корпусу невиданны**, и каждое получает вес **4,96** против **0,58** у самого обыденного из виденных — местоимение «этим» весит в 8,5 раза больше настоящего термина.
- **Отвергнут путь «поправить состав корпуса».** Пересчёт по корпусу ТОЛЬКО из речи оператора (42 вопроса) даёт **те же 31 % знакомых слов** на трёх пробных вопросах; меняется лишь вес невиданного, 4,96 → 3,76. Чистка корпуса от собственных ярлыков проблему не решает, и предлагать её больше не нужно.
- **Настоящий корень — вырожденность, а не состав.** В корпусе 517 различных слов, из них **250 (48 %) встречаются ровно в ОДНОМ вопросе**, то есть весят столько же, сколько невиданные (максимум 4,96 совпадает у виденного-однажды и у невстреченного). Для половины словаря «видел один раз» и «не видел никогда» неразличимы: при таком размере корпуса у прибора почти нет разрешающей силы.
- **Fix deliberately unprescribed** — остаются пол/порог для невиданных токенов, но выбор между ними по-прежнему требует замера КАЧЕСТВА подбора, а не вкуса. Что изменилось: путь «поправить корпус» закрыт числом, а не мнением.
- **ЗАМЕР, КОТОРОГО ЗАПИСЬ ЖДАЛА, СДЕЛАН 2026-08-26 — и он исключает один из двух оставленных путей.** По 142 живым вопросам и 32 живым процедурам: впущено на вопрос — **медиана 10 из 32**, максимум **все 32**; у **90 %** вопросов впущено ≥3, то есть заявленное следствие подтверждается; и **73 % всех допусков (1521 из 2089) держатся на ОДНОМ общем значащем слове**.
- **Почему «пол для невиданных слов» исключён ДЛЯ ЭТОЙ ЦЕЛИ.** Допуск не имеет порога вовсе (`if score and (q_content & hay_tokens)`), поэтому любой положительный вес впускает. Проверено: девятикратная смена веса невиданного слова (4,96 → 0,57) оставляет **2089 допусков ровно 2089** и меняет тройку, доходящую до планировщика, лишь у **11 вопросов из 142**. Уменьшить шум может только порог на ДОПУСК; перевес — не про шум.
- **Ошибка в первом заходе замера, названная, а не убранная.** Сперва корпус строился из тех же вопросов, которыми я спрашивал, — тогда невиданных слов в запросе нет вовсе и «пол» недостижим по построению (0 изменений из 142). Пересчитано leave-one-out: корпус из 141, запрос — 142-й.
- **Раскол по говорящему, без которого среднее врёт.** У 98 вопросов, начинающихся с ярлыка задачи агента, невиданных слов темы в среднем 2 %; у 44 вопросов человеческой речи — **26 %**. Механизм записи подтверждён именно там, где важен: на речи оператора.
- **Константа порога по-прежнему не выбрана, и теперь названа причина.** Разметка мертва: 29 процедур из 34 указывают на вытесненные эпизоды, осталось 5 положительных и 8 отрицательных пар, J скачет на ±0,05 от одной пары. Заведено как MIR-165 и там же починено; замер станет решающим, когда независимый след накопится.
- **Status:** `open`.

### MIR-104 — two `future/` contracts propose, in large part, machinery that already exists
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-099/MIR-100 (a document asserting a state the code already contradicts). **Provenance:** read 2026-08-20 on the operator's instruction to examine their content rather than decide their fate by their orphan status.
- **Files:** knowledge/doctrine/future/AGENT\_ROLE\_CONTRACT.md (608 lines) and knowledge/doctrine/future/ORGANISATIONAL\_ROLES\_CONTRACT.md (1674). Both written by the agent's own charter campaigns; both carry a DRAFT/TARGET banner; neither has an inbound reference or a runtime consumer.
- **Their seven "hard invariants" are not proposals.** Policy Gate before material execution, human-reserved authority, role outputs are witnesses, governance does not self-mutate, primary evidence is retained, high-impact matters escalate, deep-model escalation is an event — every one of them restates `knowledge/doctrine/CENTRAL_AGENT_GOVERNANCE.md` §1–§9, where they are marked **IMPLEMENTED**. The overlap is not accidental phrasing: the governance doc's §6 is titled "Deep/Opus escalation is an event, not a habit" and both drafts write "It is an event, not a habit."
- **The role contract they propose is implemented, at sub-agent scope.** `core/subagent_contract.py` holds `CanonicalSubagentContract` with `CanonicalMemoryScope`, `CanonicalToolScope` and `CanonicalBudgetScope` — a bounded office defined by contract rather than by model, which is §6.3's demand.
- **The role-performance ledger of §11 is implemented too.** `core/subagent_registry.py` `RoleRecord` — "per-role performance counters plus derived, advisory scores" — carries role_id, status, invocations, successes, failures, vetoes, proposals created/approved, committed/rolled-back, the human value-review counters, cost, `trust_score`, `usefulness_score` and a `recommendation`. §11.1 insists such a ledger must be advisory and must not grant authority; the code's docstring says the same word.
- **What is genuinely unbuilt**, and this is what the two documents are worth: durable role FAMILIES as named standing offices (research / repair / review / synthesis / finance / operations), assignment contracts with explicit closure states, and the ledger refinements §11.2 asks for — segmentation by task class and risk tier, tamper-evidence, retention and redaction policy.
- **No contradiction with the charter.** Both banners subordinate themselves explicitly, naming `core/subagent_runner` as the current bounded behaviour and denying that it equals durable organisational roles.
- **The danger they carry is the opposite of the usual one.** Filed under `future/` and read top-down, they teach that the agent has no role contracts and no performance ledger. It has both. A false self-model that UNDERSTATES the system is as harmful as one that overstates it: it invites rebuilding what exists.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-22 evening). Files kept, as the verdict below ruled; only their self-description was repaired. The merge question stays undecided, as before.
- **RE-VERIFIED 2026-08-22: unmet, and the documents assert the opposite of the truth about a module they name.** Both banners are intact and both say NOT IMPLEMENTED — which is the direction this entry identifies as the danger, not the cure. Worse, `knowledge/doctrine/future/AGENT_ROLE_CONTRACT.md:16` names `core/subagent_registry` in its module-anchor table and rules on it explicitly: *"Read-only seed concept for named sub-agent configuration. Does not establish durable identity, role authority, persistent memory, or enforcement."* Measured against the code the same day, `RoleRecord` carries **19 fields** — `role_id, status, invocations, successes, failures, vetoes, outputs_vetoed, proposals_created, proposals_approved, committed_local, rolled_back, confirmed_value, value_rejected, cost_units, cost_source, last_used_at, trust_score, usefulness_score, recommendation` — which is the §11 role-performance ledger these very documents propose as future work. The sentence disclaiming persistence sits directly above a table of persisted per-role counters.
- **And the strongest counter-evidence is invisible to them.** `core/subagent_contract.py` — holding `CanonicalSubagentContract` with `CanonicalMemoryScope`, `CanonicalToolScope` and `CanonicalBudgetScope`, i.e. the bounded office §6.3 demands — is mentioned **zero times across all five `knowledge/doctrine/future/` documents**. A reader working top-down from the drafts cannot discover it. The closure criterion is therefore not merely unsatisfied: a reader following these documents is actively told that implemented machinery does not exist.
- **CLOSED 2026-08-22 evening — the closure criterion is met verbatim.** `AGENT_ROLE_CONTRACT.md` opens with an «Already implemented» section naming `core/subagent_contract.py` (the §6.3 bounded office) and `core/subagent_registry.py` `RoleRecord` (the §11 ledger, 19 advisory counters), stating that seven of its «hard invariants» restate the IMPLEMENTED governance doc, and naming what genuinely remains unbuilt. Its module-anchor row for `subagent_registry` no longer denies persistence it has. `ORGANISATIONAL_ROLES_CONTRACT.md`'s banner drops «persistent per-agent ledgers» from its list of things that do not exist and carries the same correction block. The protective direction of both banners (target ≠ current, current-code documentation wins) is intact.
- **Closure criterion (met, kept for the record):** each document states, at its head, which of its sections describe machinery that already exists and names it, so no reader can take the implemented part for a proposal.

### MIR-103 — `verify()` is fifteen conditionals deep, and it is the organ everything downstream trusts
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-099 (a count is not a diagnosis), MIR-060 (what this same function can and cannot judge). **Provenance:** measured 2026-08-20 while validating the premise behind 36 complexity warnings rather than acting on their numbers.
- **Files/functions:** `core/verifier_core.py:84` `verify()`.
- **The measurement that separates it from the others.** A count of branches says nothing on its own: a flat dispatch has as many branches as its vocabulary has words and is read one line at a time. So each flagged function was measured for depth, for how many of its returns are guard clauses, and for how many statements sit below depth 4. Fifteen of the 21 came out flat — `handle_meta_command` has 94 returns and every one is a guard; `route_operator_intent` has 34 at depth 1 — and were suppressed with their measurement. `verify()` did not: **349 lines, 51 `if` and 11 `for`, deepest chain `for > if×12 > for > if` = depth 15, 127 statements below depth 4, and only 2 returns.** Nothing is refused early; state is carried through the whole body.
- **Why it matters more here than anywhere else.** This is the function whose verdict every other organ treats as truth — episode admission, procedure credit, the one premise gate in the system (MIR-100) all read counters it produces. MIR-060 already records that it cannot tell a resolvable citation from a true claim. A judgement that is hard to follow is hard to correct, and this one is trusted by construction.
- **Status:** `open`, deliberately not refactored here. Splitting the verifier is its own change with its own risk, and today's rule applies to it too: the measurement licenses "this one is genuinely hard to read", not a particular decomposition.
- **RE-MEASURED 2026-08-22 AND EVERY NUMBER MATCHES EXACTLY** — 349 lines, 51 `if`, 11 `for`, max depth 15, 127 statements below depth 4, 2 returns, measured independently by AST rather than re-read from this entry. `verify()` is unchanged at `core/verifier_core.py:84`. Recorded because a re-measure that reproduces to the digit is evidence the original measurement was sound, and this entry's numbers are load-bearing for MIR-100 and MIR-060.
- **The other six that survived the same filter, in order of how far they are from flat:** `agent_tick.run_tick` (depth 7, 36 deep statements), `scripts/docs_code_conformance.main` (6, 29), `core/campaign.run_campaign` (5, 18), `core/step_sanitizer.sanitize_step` (4, 8, but 665 lines and 20 of its 72 returns are not guards), `core/loop_synthesis._synthesize` (4, 6), `core/self_build_producer.produce_self_apply_proposal` (5, 5).

### MIR-102 — a hostile RSS feed could expand entities during parsing
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** found 2026-08-20 in the same lint-findings audit as MIR-101, reading each finding for the provenance of its input rather than for the warning. **Related:** MIR-101 — the same audit, the same method: read the finding for the provenance of its input rather than for the warning.
- **Files/functions:** `tools/rss_fetch.py` `_parse_feed()`.
- **The gap.** The feed is XML from someone else's server. `xml.etree` resolves no external entities and retrieves no DTD, but it DOES expand internal ones, which is all that billion-laughs and quadratic blowup need: a feed inside the existing 1 MB size cap can expand to gigabytes while parsing. The cap measured bytes on the wire, and nothing measured what those bytes became.
- **Fix without a new dependency.** `defusedxml` is installed but not declared in `requirements.txt`, and the hash-locked set is out of scope by operator ruling, so the guard is structural instead: RSS and Atom have no use for a DTD, so the DECLARATION is the refusal. The check reads only the prolog — skipping processing instructions and comments, stopping at the root element — so the same characters inside a text node or CDATA are not mistaken for a declaration.
- **Status:** `fixed`. Pinned by `tests/test_rss_feed_declares_no_entities.py`, written red first: a billion-laughs payload and a bare `<!DOCTYPE rss>` are both refused, an ordinary feed still parses.

### MIR-101 — a `paths` element could enter pytest's argv as an option
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** found 2026-08-20 while auditing the S603/S607/S310/S102 lint findings for the provenance of their inputs, rather than for the warning.
- **Files/functions:** `tools/run_tests.py` `_build_argv()`.
- **The gap.** `paths` is planner-controlled. Every guard on it asked what a PATH may be — ASCII (`require_ascii_identifier`), no `..` segment, resolves inside the workspace, count capped — and a string like `-p` satisfies all four. It was appended to argv, where pytest reads a leading dash as an OPTION. Probed live before the fix: `-p`, `-x` and `--cov=/etc` all reached argv. `-p <module>` makes pytest import that module, so a run of the test suite becomes an import of anything writable in the tree.
- **Not exploited, and that is not the argument.** No trace shows it used. The boundary was implicit: every guard described a path and none described an argument, so the code expressed "this is a path" and pytest read "this is a flag".
- **Fix.** Refused by SHAPE — a leading `-` on any element of `paths`, and on `pattern` — because the dangerous set is whatever the installed pytest accepts, not a blocklist we could enumerate. Pinned by `tests/test_run_tests_paths_are_not_flags.py`, written red first.
- **Status:** `fixed`.
- **The neighbours were checked the same way and hold:** `core/safe_vcs.py` refuses a dashed branch name (`_BRANCH_RE` demands a leading alphanumeric), `tools/web_fetch.py` and `tools/rss_fetch.py` call `validate_url` (scheme allow-list, egress policy) on the line above the request, `tools/semantic_scholar_search.py` builds its URL from a constant https base, and `tools/shell_exec.py` runs only `_validate_argv`-approved argv.

### MIR-087 — the agent's own instrument lies to it about its own environment (missing PATHEXT)
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-08-04) from a live operator run. The operator told the agent its tools were all connected and demanded it verify rather than believe. The agent did exactly that — and its instrument gave it a false negative.
- **Files/functions:** `tools/shell_exec.py` `_safe_env` (`:877`).
- **What happened:** the agent ran `shell_exec(['where', 'python'])` and got `exit_code=1`, "Could not find files for the given pattern(s)". It concluded that "declared connectivity and actual availability of the tool are different things". Python is on PATH on that machine.
- **Root:** `_safe_env` passed `PATH`, `SystemRoot` and the git identity variables — **not `PATHEXT`**. On Windows PATHEXT is what turns the NAME `python` into `python.exe`; without it every by-name lookup resolves to nothing. Measured with identical PATH, only that variable differing: `where python` → **exit 1, empty** without PATHEXT, **exit 0, `C:\…\Python311\python.exe`** with it. `where git` failed the same way.
- **Why this one matters more than its size:** the agent was doing the right thing — refusing to take the operator's word, running a real check — and the instrument it reached for reported a falsehood about its own environment. Every conclusion it drew afterwards was sound reasoning over a corrupt reading. A defect that punishes an agent for verifying teaches it not to verify.
- **Fix (2026-08-04):** `PATHEXT` is forwarded on Windows. It is a list of file suffixes and carries no credential — the same standard the git identity variables were admitted under.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_shell_exec_windows_pathext.py` — 3 cases, 2 fail-before (PATHEXT is forwarded; a name on PATH actually resolves end-to-end), 1 pinning that the environment stays minimal. Two existing guards in `tests/test_shell_exec.py` broke on the change and were **updated with the reason written in**, not silenced: widening the sandbox environment is a security decision and must stay explicit.
- **Also observed in the same run, registered not fixed:** (a) the agent read a *successful* command execution — a real exit code and a real Windows error string — as evidence that the tool might not work, rather than as proof that it does; (b) `python` and `git --version` are refused by the command whitelist **by design**, and the agent attributed that refusal to "the sandbox" without reading the whitelist that sits in the same file it could have opened.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-04). Measured: 6735 passed, 1 skipped.

### MIR-086 — the operator asks in Russian, memory is written in English, recall scores by word overlap
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-08-04), by applying the "change the input, diff the output" check (notebook §22) to memory retrieval. The output DID change with the question — the surprise was that it was empty for almost every Russian one.
- **Files/functions:** `core/memory_policy.py` `_query_tokens` (the widening point), `_BROAD_PROJECT_MEMORY_TOKENS` (a bilingual set that already existed but only fired for broad self-knowledge questions); `core/bilingual_terms.py` (new).
- **Two standing rules collide.** The operator is answered in Russian; every repository artefact — persistent memory included — is written in English. Retrieval scores by word overlap, so between a Russian question and an English record the overlap is zero **by construction**.
- **Measured on the live store (`data/persistent_memory.jsonl`, 47 records, 46 Latin-only):** «кто владеет архитектурой?» → **0** records, "who owns the architecture?" → 3. «расскажи про карту репозитория» → **0**, "tell me about the repository map" → 3. A question that happened to contain a Latin word ("README") worked in both languages — which is why this was never noticed.
- **Not a comprehension problem, and that distinction matters.** The model reads Russian directly — there is no translation anywhere on the input path (verified: no translate/normalise step in `core/loop.py`, `core/planner.py`, `core/loop_methods2.py`). What does not understand Russian is the literal string comparison inside retrieval. The "everything in English" rule governs what the agent WRITES, not what it understands. <!-- historical-ref -->
- **Fix (operator's decision, 2026-08-04):** `core/bilingual_terms.py` — a table of ~46 domain stems (`архитектур` → `architecture`, `задач` → `task`), matched as prefixes because Russian inflects. Deliberately a table, not a translator: no model call, so retrieval keeps the determinism its own docstring promises. `_query_tokens` widens every question, not just the broad ones.
- **And a miss counter, so the next step is decided from numbers.** Every recall miss now journals `question_script` (cyrillic/latin/mixed) and `bilingual_terms_added`. A miss on a Cyrillic question the table could not widen at all is the case that would justify translating the question or moving to semantic search; a miss with terms added is genuine absence. The operator chose "table now, count misses, decide later" over building the bigger mechanism first.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_memory_recall_across_languages.py` — 10 cases, 3 fail-before (a Russian question reaches an English record; the Russian and English forms of one question agree; several domain terms are reachable). The rest pin what must not change: English questions unchanged, an unrelated question still matches nothing, no unrelated record dragged in, and the four diagnostic shapes.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-04). Measured: 6732 passed, 1 skipped.
- **Named, not fixed:** the same literal-word comparison exists in other deciders (role routing, procedure lookup). Some already carry Russian keys, some may not — to be measured separately rather than assumed either way.

### MIR-085 — a rollback lesson is banked under a key the recall never uses
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-083 (same module, veto classification). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-08-04) while checking the notebook's row 11 claim, which turned out to be wrong — see the correction below.
- **Files/functions:** `core/self_build_memory.py` `build_self_build_episode` (`:86`, path tags at `:137`), `_touched_paths` (new), `recent_self_build_lessons` (`:188`), `_with_untagged_lessons` (new).
- **The two halves disagreed on the key.** `recent_self_build_lessons(agent, target)` searches episodes tagged with `self-build` + `failed` + the file path. The writer took the path from `result["target_path"]` — which a `self-apply-run` result does not carry; it carries `files_changed`. So every rollback was banked with **no path tag** and was invisible to the lookup that exists to prevent repeats.
- **Measured on the live store (`data/episodic_memory.jsonl`):** `rolled_back` episodes are tagged `['self-build','lesson','self-apply-run','rolled_back','failed']` — no path; `proposed` episodes do carry one. `recent_self_build_lessons(agent, "cli/intent_bridge.py")` returned **0**. Two of the three rollbacks present are the **same file, same test, same assertion** — `test_ambiguous_capability_check_still_uses_model_veto`, "assert True is False". The agent repeated its own mistake because its own warning was unreachable.
- **Fix:** the writer tags the episode with the files the attempt actually changed (capped at 10 — tags are for lookup, not a manifest of the change). For episodes banked before the tag existed, the reader falls back to their summary, which names the files, rather than the store being rewritten to match the code. **Re-measured on the live store: `cli/intent_bridge.py` 0 → 2 lessons, an unrelated file still 0.**
- **Correction of a claim in the notebook journal (row 11).** It read "the builder calls the model directly: the result never reaches the verifier". **That is wrong as written:** the patch is checked — the critic applies structural vetoes (empty content, diff instead of full content, identical content, size, critical target, missing tests, missing symbols), and on apply the lane runs targeted tests, then full pytest, and rolls back on red. What IS unverified self-report is the `confidence` number: it comes from the model's own reply and is compared against `_DEFAULT_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD`. Its value is measured, not asserted: **3 live proposals passed that gate and were then rolled back by failing tests** — the number did not predict working code. Registered here as an observation with evidence; changing the gate is a separate decision for the operator, not a silent edit.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_rollback_lessons_are_findable.py` (7 cases: rollback tagged with its files, the next attempt finds the lesson, a successful apply is tagged but is not a failure lesson, an apply with no files still banks, a legacy rollback is still found, an unrelated failure is not dragged in by the fallback, tags do not balloon on a 40-file patch). `tests/test_self_build_memory.py::test_recent_lessons_returns_past_failure_summaries` was **updated, not weakened**: recall now issues a second broader query, and the fixture kept only the last one, so it now asserts the targeted query still happens FIRST.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-04). Measured: full suite green.

### MIR-084 — auto-continuation stitches JSON it cannot resume, and corrupts it
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-083 (the veto that followed blamed the target), MIR-071 (raw retention — without it there would have been no evidence to read). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-08-04); carried in the notebook journal as "not handled" / "not investigated" and fixed on the operator's ruling.
- **Files/functions:** `core/llm.py` `complete` (the continuation loop at `:302`), `core/model_router.py` `complete` / `_call_llm`, `core/self_build_producer.py` `_llm_json_with_raw` (`:390`), `_BUILDER_MAX_TOKENS` (`:111`), `core/builder_reply_diagnosis.py` (new).
- **Two faults, one mechanism.** (1) `_BUILDER_MAX_TOKENS = 16_000` is a **per-leg** cap: `complete()` auto-continues a truncated answer up to `AGENT_MAX_CONTINUATIONS` (default 4) more times, so the true ceiling was five legs — a live reply reached **20 509 tokens** against a "16 000" cap. (2) Far worse: **continuation cannot resume a JSON string.**
- **Evidence (three preserved replies, `logs/self_build_rejects/`, 2026-08-04):** the escaping style flips exactly at the leg boundary. Before it `\\n` and `\\"` (escaped, inside a JSON string); after it real newlines and bare `"` — the model resumed writing plain code, not knowing where it stood. Break positions 32 326 / 32 440 / 37 678 chars all sit at 2.0–2.35 chars per token from the start of a 16 000-token first leg. Every one of those runs paid for extra legs to produce garbage, and the resulting veto blamed the target file (MIR-083).
- **Fix:** `LLM.complete(..., allow_continuation=False)` returns the first leg and sets `last_answer_was_truncated`, so a caller whose answer must parse as a whole can tell "it did not fit" from "the model wrote nonsense". `ModelRouter._call_llm` passes it through and tolerates wrappers that predate the flag — where continuation stays ON, which is the historical behaviour, rather than pretending the request was honoured. The builder declines stitching, and its veto now reads *"reply hit the 16000-token ceiling — the target is too large for a single pass"*.
- **Not a size increase:** the diagnosis wording moved into the new compact `core/builder_reply_diagnosis.py` (also translating it to English per the repository-artifacts rule), so `core/self_build_producer.py` went **1860 → 1841** lines and its ratchet was lowered rather than raised.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_llm.py::TestStructuredAnswersAreNotBlindlyStitched` (4 cases — first leg only, truncation reported, a complete answer not flagged, prose still stitched) and two in `tests/test_self_build_raw_retention.py` (the builder declines stitching; a truncated reply is named as too big, not as malformed). All fail-before, verified by stashing the three source files.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-04). Measured: full suite green; `core/builder_reply_diagnosis.py` registered in the anatomy map (§9).
- **Deliberately NOT addressed (still open, next in line):** the builder calls the model directly, so its output never passes the agent's verifier — notebook journal row 11, `core/self_build_producer.py`.

### MIR-083 — the agent avoids the files its own generator broke on
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-08-04), carried in the notebook journal as "not handled" for several hours; fixed on the operator's ruling that a registered defect keeps costing exactly what an unregistered one does.
- **Files/functions (as of the fix):** `core/self_build_memory.py` `build_self_build_episode` (tagging at `:105`), `_veto_was_about_the_target` (`:222`, new), `recently_vetoed_self_build_targets` (`:236`); `core/veto_cause.py` (new, the classifier).
- **The mechanism:** every `critic_veto` put its target into a cooldown set the producer uses to skip that file on the next run. Sound when the veto judged the target; wrong when the veto happened because the builder's own reply never parsed — the agent then learns to avoid precisely the files its generator chokes on, which are the files most in need of work.
- **Measured against the agent's own `data/episodic_memory.jsonl` (2026-08-04), all five `critic_veto` episodes:** 3 × "builder reply did not parse into usable content (raw_chars=…)", 1 × "empty generated content", 1 × "confidence 0.02 below threshold 0.60". **Four of five blamed the target for a broken pipeline**, and all five went on the avoid list.
- **Fix:** `veto_blames_the_target` (`core/veto_cause.py`) decides from the veto text — markers taken from those real episodes, not imagined ones — and any marker of our own machinery failing wins, because a reply that never parsed drags "confidence 0.00" and "no targeted tests specified" along with it. The episode is now tagged `veto_judgement` or `veto_pipeline` at banking, where the reasons are still in hand; the cooldown reads the tag and falls back to the same rule on the summary for rows banked before the classification existed. **Re-measured on the live store: 4 targets released, the 1 genuine veto kept.**
- **Absent evidence does not punish:** an episode with no reason at all is not treated as a verdict. The asymmetry is deliberate — a missed cooldown costs one wasted attempt, a false cooldown costs the file forever.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_veto_cause_is_distinguished.py` (8 cases, veto strings copied verbatim from the real episodes). `tests/test_self_build_memory.py::test_recently_vetoed_targets_extracts_path_from_veto_episodes` broke on the change and was **updated, not silenced**: its fixture carried no veto reason at all, which no real episode does.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-04). Measured: 6708 passed, 1 skipped. `core/veto_cause.py` registered in `scripts/gen_anatomy.py` (§9) and `docs/AGENT_ANATOMY.md` regenerated — the anatomy guard caught the missing entry.

### MIR-082 — the learning plan ignores the weak spot reflection named
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-08-04). Found by coverage: `core/autonomous_runtime.py:1004` — the branch that feeds the reflection's learning plan into `ingest_files` — had no test at all. Confirmed by measuring the planner against the real repository.
- **The chain, and why it is live:** reflection asks the model for `focus_area` as a **file or module** (`core/reflection.py:375`, e.g. `"tools/web_fetch.py"`, `"core/memory.py"`), builds the goal `"reflection: study weak areas — <focus_areas>"` (`:517`), and the runtime ingests whatever the plan returns (`core/autonomous_runtime.py:1004`). Whatever the plan picks is literally what the agent reads and writes knowledge from.
- **Root:** `core/learning_planner.py` `_score` had no rule for "this file is the subject of the goal". Being named was worth **zero**; the only related bonus was +50 for a keyword match from `_goal_terms`, a short hardcoded list (memory/role/tool/repair/doctrine/confidence) that a path like `core/planner.py` does not hit at all. Generic filename bonuses then decided the plan.
- **Measured 2026-08-04 (read-only, against this repository), five focus areas of exactly the shape the prompt asks for — `core/planner.py`, `core/task_queue.py`, `tools/web_fetch.py`, `core/memory.py`, `core/verifier_core.py`: the named file was picked ZERO times out of five.** The top of the list was the same for every goal — `core/architecture_audit.py`, `tests/test_architecture_audit.py`, … Scores for the `core/planner.py` goal: named file **115** (70 agent core + 45 default spine) against `core/architecture_audit.py` **165** (95 for the word "architecture" in its name + 70). Even when keywords did match (`tools/web_fetch.py`), the +50 lost to the +95.
- **Why no test caught it:** the tests assert that a plan is produced and that its sources are sane. None asserts that the plan **depends on the goal** — the property that makes the whole feature more than decoration. The branch consuming it was uncovered, so nothing measured the end-to-end effect either.
- **Fix (2026-08-04):** `_named_paths` extracts paths the goal names outright, `_is_named` matches them by whole path segments (so `memory.py` never matches `smart_memory.py`), and `_score` adds `_NAMED_BY_GOAL_BONUS = 500` — deliberately above the doctrine (300+) and confidence (360+) priorities, because a file the goal names is the subject of the pass, not a candidate for it. **It only re-ranks candidates already inside the workspace:** a path in the goal is untrusted model output and must never widen what may be read (`tests/…::test_a_named_path_cannot_reach_outside_the_workspace`).
- **Re-measured after the fix:** all five named files come out **first** in their plans. The operator's `:learn <topic>` path, where the goal names no file, is unchanged.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_learning_plan_studies_the_named_file.py` — 6 cases, 3 of them fail-before (named file studied, named file outranks the generic favourites, several named files all studied); the other 3 pin what must not change (an unresolvable path does not empty the plan, a topic-only goal still works, no escape from the workspace).
- **Both neighbouring gaps closed the same day, on the operator's ruling** that a defect left registered is a defect that keeps costing — they were briefly listed here as "registered, not fixed":
  - **Module notation.** The prompt says "file **or module**", so `core.memory` arrives as often as `core/memory.py`. `_named_paths` now turns dotted notation into a path (a token with no slash whose last segment is not a known text suffix is a module), and `_is_named` compares both with and without the suffix.
  - **A narrowed `root` hid the named file.** On the `:learn` path `root` rotates, and a weak spot in `core/` simply never entered the candidate set when the pass was rooted at `tools/` — the plan was silently about something else. `_named_candidates` resolves the named file regardless of `root`. **Only the named file crosses that boundary**; everything else stays inside `root` (`test_a_narrowed_root_still_bounds_everything_else`), and it must still resolve inside the workspace.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-04). Measured: 6700 passed, 1 skipped; 9 tests in the spec file, 6 of them fail-before.

### MIR-081 — claiming a task is not exclusive: two consumers run the same work
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** MIR-040 (the first attempt at recovery wiring was reverted precisely because it could double-run work — the same hazard, reached from the recovery side instead of the claim side). **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-08-04), found by reading the claim path after MIR-080, then reproduced with two real processes.
- **Files/functions (line numbers as of the fix):** `core/task_queue.py` `mark_running` (`:370`, check at `:386`), `_update_one` (`:590` — raises only `KeyError` for a missing id); consumers `core/autonomous_runtime.py` `run_task_queue` (`:317`) and `agent_tick.py` (`:928`); unlocked entry points `app/task_scheduler_cli.py:179` (`:task-run`) and `:385` (`:schedule-tick --run`).
- **Root:** `mark_running` wrote `status="running"` without testing that the task was still `pending`. The queue's file lock made the write atomic but not conditional, so a second claim simply overwrote the first.
- **Why the single-instance lock did not cover it:** only `agent_tick` acquires `app/single_instance.py`'s lock. The two operator-facing drains do not, so an operator typing `:task-run` while a tick is due is enough. And `agent_tick.py:929` carried `except Exception: continue  # another process already claimed it` — a guard against an exception that was never raised, which made the hazard look considered.
- **The window is not microseconds.** `run_task_queue` reads `pending(limit=max_tasks)` ONCE and then works through the list, so a task in the tail is claimed as many minutes after it was listed as the earlier tasks took to run.
- **Reproduction (2026-08-04, out-of-repo temp queue, two real processes):** both consumers claimed one task — `attempts=2` from the claims alone, `owner_pid` overwritten by the loser, so the row no longer named the process running it. After the fix the same probe gives one claimant, `attempts=1`, `owner_pid` of the real owner, and the other gets `task ... is running, not pending`.
- **Fix (2026-08-04):** `TaskAlreadyClaimed(RuntimeError)` — deliberately distinct from `KeyError`, because "somebody took it" and "it is gone" call for different reactions. The status test runs inside `_update_one`'s existing file lock, on the row as just read, so test and write are one transaction; an unhappy claim raises before `_save_unlocked` and leaves the file untouched. Both consumers now skip a lost claim and journal `task_claim_lost` instead of stealing the task.
- **Tests:** `tests/test_task_claim_is_exclusive.py` (8 cases: second claim refused, refusal changes nothing, finished tasks cannot be resurrected via `mark_done`/`cancel`, the message names the blocking status, a re-queued retry can still be claimed, missing id still raises `KeyError`) and `tests/test_autonomous_runtime.py::test_auto_runtime_queue_skips_a_task_another_consumer_took`.
- **The integration test was wrong first, and that is recorded on purpose:** its first version marked the task `running` BEFORE `run_task_queue`, so the task never entered the pending list read inside — it passed on the defective code and proved nothing. The working version steals the tail task from inside the run of the first one, which is where the race actually lives; on the old code the drain then runs the other consumer's task too.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-04). Measured: full suite green (6690 passed, 1 skipped, on the run that also carried MIR-080).

### MIR-080 — a queued task with an unknown `kind` is accepted, written, and then silently unread
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** newly_discovered (2026-08-04) — fell out of the live orphan-recovery probe run for MIR-040, not from a review: the probe passed `kind="status"` by mistake, `add()` returned a task object, the file grew by 626 bytes, and the very next `load()` returned an empty list.
- **Files/functions (line numbers as of the fix):** `core/task_queue.py` `add` (`:214` — used to build `RuntimeTask(kind=kind, ...)` with no validation), `RuntimeTask.from_dict` (`:138` — was the only validator, via `_choice(..., allowed=_VALID_KINDS)`), `_load_unlocked` (`:300`) and its skip site (`:299` — was a bare `except (TypeError, ValueError): continue`). Legal kinds are `RuntimeTaskKind = Literal["auto_run", "resume_checkpoint"]` (`:25`).
- **Root:** validation lived only on the READ side. The write side accepted anything, serialised it, and the row was durable and well-formed — intact envelope, valid checksum, wrong schema. Every later read then rejected it and moved on without a word.
- **Why it matters:** the loss is total and invisible from both ends. The caller holds a `RuntimeTask` with an id and believes the work is queued; the consumer never sees it; nothing raises and nothing is logged. A wrong `kind` can come from a caller typo, an older writer, or a hand-edited queue file — and the queue is how the daemon is told to do anything at all.
- **Reproduction:** `tests/test_task_queue_silent_loss.py` — four cases, all fail-before (measured: 4 failed → 4 passed). The unreadable-row case writes through the real encoder on purpose: a plain-JSON line is taken for a legacy payload and quietly filled with defaults, so it would have modelled the wrong failure.
- **Fix (2026-08-04):** `add` validates through the same `_choice` that `from_dict` uses — one definition of a legal kind, checked where the mistake is made. `_load_unlocked` still skips a bad row (one bad line must not sink the queue) but now counts it in `TaskQueueStore.last_unreadable_rows` and logs `runtime task row dropped` with the id and kind.
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-04). Measured: 6682 passed, 1 skipped (full suite).

### MIR-079 — persistent-memory read-modify-write drops a concurrent save
- **Aliases:** none. **Provenance:** operator brief (2026-08-04), item 1; confirmed by code reading, then reproduced with a failing test.
- **Files/functions:** `core/persistent_memory.py` `update` (`:58`), `archive_record` (`:83`), `delete` (`:223`). Neighbours `save` (`:45`), `save_many` (`:50`) already took `state_file_lock`; the read-modify-write pair did not.
- **Root:** each of the three read the whole file, edited the list in memory, then called `_rewrite`, which took the lock only for the write. Between the read and that write the file was unguarded: anything another writer appended in the gap was overwritten by the rewrite — no exception, no log line, the record simply gone.
- **Reproduction:** `tests/test_persistent_memory_atomicity.py::test_a_save_in_the_gap_between_read_and_rewrite_survives` — models the window by writing at the moment the FIRST critical section exits. Fails on the pre-fix code, passes after.
- **Two earlier versions of that test were wrong and are documented in its docstring:** hooking the rewrite, then hooking the read, both placed the competing write INSIDE a critical section, where even the broken code was safe — they passed on the defective code and proved nothing.
- **Fix:** one lock spans read and rewrite in all three methods. `_rewrite` cannot be reused inside them — the lock is not reentrant and a naive wrapper raises `OSError: Resource deadlock avoided` — so the unlocked primitives are called directly, and two unlocked readers were added: `_active_unlocked` (`:127`, live records, no TTL eviction — the caller is mid-rewrite and writes the surviving set itself) and `_load_raw_unlocked` (`:159`).
- **Status:** `fixed` (2026-08-04). Measured: 6678 passed, 1 skipped; ruff clean; the three methods are shared infrastructure and the full suite is what proves no neighbour broke.

---

### MIR-078 — файлы кода превышают операторский потолок 2000 строк
- **Aliases:** none. **Related:** [[main-py-extraction]] (2250 → 47), [[planner-py-decomposition]] (2872 → 516) — метод уже отработан. **Provenance:** правило оператора (2026-08-03): «файлы не должны пересекать 2000 строк кода — это самое разумное оперативное правило».
- **Измерено 2026-08-03:** `core/loop.py` 4232 (кратно выше потолка), далее `core/smart_memory.py` 1861, `core/self_build_producer.py` 1813, `core/model_router.py` 1719, `agent_tick.py` 1458 — эти четыре под потолком, но близко.
- **Почему это корень, а не косметика:** файл выше двух тысяч строк нечитаем и ненаходим; каждая новая проводка садится в него, потому что «так короче», и съедает труд прошлых расколов. Правило простое и проверяемое, в отличие от «пиши компактно».
- **Status:** `partially_fixed` (2026-08-03, кусок 1). `core/loop.py` 4232 → **3404**: всё про ОДИН шаг плана (политика и одобрение, вызов инструмента, разбор результата, классификация данных, защита от инъекций, план компенсации, параллельный запуск читающих шагов — 7 методов, 812 строк + 3 приватные константы) уехало в новый модуль-миксин `core/loop_step_execution.py`. Перенос дословный: AST-сверка тел против истории пинится `tests/test_loop_step_execution_split.py`; швы сохранены — импорт-пути `from core.loop import _TRUSTED_INTERNAL_TOOLS` живы через ре-экспорт, цель `mock.patch` и тест порядка контракта переведены на новые адреса с сохранением проверяемого свойства.
- **Следующие куски `loop.py` (карта готова; все перечисленные ниже модули — planned, ещё не написаны):** `_synthesize` + сборка промпта синтеза (~420 строк) → `core/loop_synthesis.py`; фаза проверки внутри `_run_inner` (453 строки одним `if self.verifier_enabled`) → `core/loop_verification.py`; пост-обработка ответа (draft, политики, объяснение, переспрос, кредит памяти, ~260 строк) → `core/loop_response.py`; банковка эпизода и профиля (~240 строк) → `core/loop_banking.py`. После них цикл уходит под 2000.
- **Missing tests:** каждый следующий кусок — своя AST-сверка переноса; общий храповик размеров опускается после каждого.

## G. Subagent / multi-agent (planned gaps)

### MIR-029 — declared `MemoryScope` is not enforced on sub-agents
- **Aliases:** SUBAGENT_LIFECYCLE §3, MEMORY_FIX_PLAN Part D. **Provenance:** previously_documented.
- **Sources:** SUBAGENT_LIFECYCLE.md, MEMORY_FIX_PLAN.md, CENTRAL_AGENT_GOVERNANCE.md §3.
- **Files/functions:** `core/subagent_runner.py` (child built with `memory=None`, `persistent_store=None`, `verifier_enabled=False`); `core/subagent_memory_scope.py` `SubagentProposal.memory_scope` (declared, not applied).
- **Symptom:** the proposal's `read_tags`/`write_tags`/`write_requires_review` are ignored by the child.
- **Root cause:** contract exists on paper; runtime enforces an empty scope (memoryless).
- **Production path:** delegation → `SubAgentRunner.run`.
- **Existing tests:** subagent tests cover memoryless behaviour.
- **Missing tests:** child sees only its `read_tags` slice; a child "conclusion" becomes a proposal, not a durable write.
- **Status:** `planned_gap` (intended order per ROADMAP Track F; not a defect — deferred deliberately).
- **Approved direction (doc):** MEMORY_FIX_PLAN Part D — start from an empty read-only scope, widen `read_tags` incrementally.

### MIR-030 — no Subagent Lifecycle Manager (transitions advisory only)
- **Aliases:** SUBAGENT_LIFECYCLE §4/§8. **Provenance:** previously_documented.
- **Sources:** SUBAGENT_LIFECYCLE.md.
- **Files/functions:** `core/subagent_registry.py` (`VALID_STATUSES = active/paused/retired`; `_MIN_JUDGED_FOR_RECOMMENDATION=5`; advisory recommendation only).
- **Symptom:** `candidate/watch/quarantined` are not stored; no auto-promote/pause/retire/quarantine.
- **Root cause:** registry only measures and recommends.
- **Status:** `planned_gap` (explicitly TARGET; the doc states "auto-pause and auto-retire do not exist today").
- **Note:** SUBAGENT_LIFECYCLE's "verified today" claims (`memory=None`, `VALID_STATUSES`, `_MIN_JUDGED_FOR_RECOMMENDATION=5`) were **spot-consistent** but a full re-verify is a Stage-1 carry-over.

## H. External operational failure modes (OFM checklist)

> OPERATIONAL_FAILURE_MODES.md is explicitly a **checklist of external
> observations**, not repo defects. Its one confirmed-gap (OFM-010) is MIR-017.
> The repo-relevant residuals are below; the remainder stay `external-only`.

### MIR-031 — no per-adapter external post-condition (false external success)
- **Aliases:** OFM-004. **Related:** MIR-010 (internal analogue). **Provenance:** previously_documented (external-only; candidate gap).
- **Sources:** OPERATIONAL_FAILURE_MODES.md §4, §11/§12.
- **Files/functions:** `core/tool_receipts.py` (proves the *call*, not the external outcome).
- **Symptom (external system):** a tool reported success while the message was never sent.
- **Status:** `planned_gap` / external — no per-adapter post-condition found; needs an integration test per effectful adapter.

### MIR-032 — single approval/actuation choke-point unproven repo-wide
- **Aliases:** OFM-006. **Provenance:** previously_documented (external-only).
- **Files/functions:** `core/approval_inbox.py`, `core/actuation_gateway.py`.
- **Status:** `needs_investigation` — inbox+gateway exist; that **every** effect path is forced through one point is not proven.

### MIR-033 — task-queue consumer running in the production loop
- **Aliases:** OFM-008. **Provenance:** previously_documented (external-only; verified control present) + independently_reconfirmed (Stage 3).
- **Files/functions:** `core/autonomous_runtime.py:313` `run_task_queue`; second consumer `agent_tick.py:817-896`; `RuntimeTaskStore`, `recover_stuck`.
- **Status:** `needs_investigation` (refined — a consumer exists and is reachable; an **always-on** production drain does **not**).
- **Stage-3 verification (2026-07-18):**
  - **Two distinct consumers exist**, with **divergent lifecycle handling:**
    (a) `core/autonomous_runtime.run_task_queue` — proper `mark_running`→`mark_done`/`mark_failed` (backoff), invoked by `app/task_scheduler_cli.py` (`:task-run`, one-shot, `max_tasks=limit`) and the campaign path (`core/campaign_io.py`).
    (b) `agent_tick.py run_tick` — the cron/scheduled fallback (invoked by `scripts/install_daemon.ps1`); it drains `pending`, runs each via `runtime.run()` directly, and **unconditionally `mark_done`** (see MIR-039). This is the closest thing to a "production loop," and it does **not** use the `run_task_queue` lifecycle.
  - **No always-on daemon consumer.** `app/daemon.py` (`DaemonLoop`) is a generic wake loop with an `on_wake` handler; it does **not** drive `run_task_queue` or the queue. Daemon composition/wiring is explicitly unbuilt (`daemon-progress.md` items 4.2–7.4 `not started`, 2.4 skipped). So OFM-008's residual — "is the consumer actually running in the production loop?" — resolves to: **only when cron invokes `agent_tick`, or an operator runs `:task-run`; there is no continuous drain.**
  - **No backlog alarm.** `summary()` exposes queue depth (`pending_due`, `statuses`) but nothing raises an alarm on a growing backlog; surfacing is advisory via `best_next_action` (relates to MIR-034).
- **Compare with docs:** OPERATIONAL_FAILURE_MODES.md (@ `6dc253e`) credits `run_task_queue` + `recover_stuck` as "Verified control present." Accurate for `run_task_queue`; **misleading for `recover_stuck`**, which is not safely wired — an attempted wiring into `run_task_queue` was found unsafe and reverted-pending (see MIR-040); the `agent_tick` cron consumer also lacks it (MIR-039).
- **Missing tests:** an approved task with no running consumer raises a backlog alarm; an integration test proving a continuous consumer drains the queue end-to-end.

- **FIELD-CHECKED AND FALSIFIED 2026-08-23.** External claim: a visibility timeout is a **lease, not a lock**; at-least-once consumers double-process when recovery hands a task to a second worker while the first is still running, and only idempotency or fencing keeps that correct (SQS visibility-timeout literature; delivery-semantics write-ups).
- **Probed on both paths, and both are closed.** (a) Exclusive claim: `mark_running` on an already-running task raised `TaskAlreadyClaimed` — a second consumer cannot take a claimed row. (b) The lease: `task_heartbeat` refreshes every **60 s** against an orphan timeout of **30 min** — a margin of **30×** — and the refresh was observed moving `heartbeat_at` in a live thread. The tick's consumer additionally runs under `SingleInstanceLock` (`data/daemon.lock`), so two ticks cannot drain concurrently.
- **One probe of mine was an artifact and is recorded as such.** The first version fast-forwarded the clock 31 minutes inside the heartbeat block without letting the refresh thread run, and «reclaimed a live worker» — an artifact of the fake clock, not a defect. Re-run against the real refresh, the hypothesis dies.
- **Two residuals, neither a risk for an unattended week.** The CLI consumer (`app/task_scheduler_cli.py` `:task-run`) and the campaign path do NOT take the daemon lock, so a human draining the queue while the daemon ticks is the one concurrency this design does not fence — and there is no human during the week. `task_heartbeat` is best-effort by construction (a failed write must not take down the run it observes), so repeated write failures could stale a live row; the consequence is bounded by the same 30× margin.
- **Status:** `needs_investigation` → the investigation is DONE: **no double-execution path exists on the autonomous path.** Left at this status only for the two residuals above, which are now named rather than open-ended.

### MIR-034 — dead-runner detection is advisory only
- **Aliases:** OFM-009. **Provenance:** previously_documented (external-only, partial control) + independently_reconfirmed (Stage 3).
- **Files/functions:** heartbeat written by `agent_tick._write_heartbeat` (`agent_tick.py:92`, called at tick start `:755`, kill-switch `:774`, tick-error `:920`, tick-complete `:947`); read by `agent_tick._read_heartbeat`/`_heartbeat_age_seconds`/`_is_stale` (`:106,:116,:133`) and `core/campaign_io.py:57-70`; consumed by `core/best_next_action.py` (heartbeat_missing/stale → advisory "daemon down" recommendation, `_P_DAEMON_DOWN=100`); `core/incident.py` staleness note.
- **Status:** `planned_gap` (confirmed) — detection present, **advisory only**.
- **Stage-3 verification (2026-07-18):** heartbeat is genuinely written on every `agent_tick` path (start, error, complete) so staleness data exists; the stale/missing signal is turned into an **advisory** next-action recommendation via `best_next_action`, not a self-heal and not an active alarm/notification. Confirms OFM-009 residual: a dead runner is *detectable* but nothing acts on it automatically. Note the heartbeat is tied to the **`agent_tick`** path; the `run_task_queue`/CLI path and the (uncomposed) daemon do not emit this heartbeat.
- **Missing tests:** a stale heartbeat produces a visible operator warning and is reported distinctly from "no work due."

### MIR-external-checklist — remaining OFM classes not mapped to repo code
- **Aliases:** OFM-001, 002, 003, 005, 007, 011, 012, 013, 014, 015, 016, 017, 018, 019, 020.
- **Sources:** OPERATIONAL_FAILURE_MODES.md.
- **Status:** `planned_gap` / `external-only` (checklist to drive future integration tests; **not** confirmed repo defects). Notable for this project: **OFM-015** (feedback stored but not applied) directly relates to MIR-035.
